A phrase that named a FEATURE would collide with a real constraint: "live music" is provable from a provider boolean and is a genuine thing to ask for, "live music venue" is a kind of place and can never be one. A test pins that no activity phrase equals any of the strings `constraintEvidence` can emit. **THE CONSTRAINT-LEAK GUARD IS THE SECOND THING THIS STAGE GUARANTEES IN CODE RATHER THAN IN WORDING**, and it is the same bug, and the same fix, the swap engine already carries for a leaked price word and a leaked category. `constraints` is ONE plan-level array applied to EVERY slot: spliced verbatim into every query by `buildQuery`, and proved in `selectVenues` from six provider booleans and nothing else. A kind of place is unprovable BY CONSTRUCTION, so a leaked "art gallery" makes `placeMeetsAllConstraints` false for every candidate in every pool and refuses the entire plan with unmet_constraint — over a preference the user never restated. The planner path had only prompt wording, and CLAUDE.md already records the equivalent dietary wording leaking on a live run. So: firmer wording ("A PREFERENCE IS NEVER A CONSTRAINT", with the reason spelled out) AND `isLeakedActivityConstraint` + `stripLeakedPreferenceConstraints`, applied in the route beside `applyTimeFloors` because it is the same class of thing — a floor over an answer the model does not get to be wrong about. **AN EQUALITY TEST, NOT A KEYWORD LIST**, normalised with `normalizeConstraint` — the SAME function the judge normalises with — copying the category strip's reasoning exactly: "live music" is CONTAINED IN the injected "live music venue" and is also a real request, so anything looser than equality would start deleting what users actually said. Known residual, stated rather than papered over: a model leaking a VARIANT ("art" for "art gallery") is not caught, and a looser rule would cost more than it saves. The strip returns the SAME OBJECT when nothing was removed, so the no-preference path is untouched rather than cloned. One log field: `strippedConstraints`, a COUNT, emitted only when non-zero — the tuning signal for a guard whose other half is wording, and it names nobody's taste. **PART 2 ASKS FIVE OPTIONS AND NOT ONE MORE.** art / outdoors / games / active / music, each mapping to a place kind that searches cleanly, carries a sensible length and is neither weather- nor schedule-broken. Theatre and shows, classes and workshops, nightlife, shopping and spas were considered and deliberately left out: a theatre or a class is a SHOWTIME product and this app has no showtimes (the same gap `durations.ts` already records for movie runtimes), and nightlife pulls against the variety caps the planner applies to an un-named day. Collecting an answer nothing can act on is how a profile fills with dead weight. There is no "Other" either — it would name no kind and could only ever be dropped. **"cultural" WAS RETIRED FROM `style`**, which is now pure vibe (chill/lively/trendy/cozy/adventurous): it was an interest wearing a vibe's clothes, and leaving it would have steered the same choice through both the query adjective and the activity kind. Retiring needed NO migration and NO backfill, and a test proves why — `normalizeAnswerSet` intersects against the closed option set on the way OUT of storage too, so an old profile's "cultural" is dropped on read while the rest of that person's answers survive, and a pre-activities document reads as "chose nothing" rather than undefined. **WHAT DID NOT NEED TOUCHING, AS PREDICTED:** `/api/profile`, `profileStore.ts`, the validator and the pipeline are all dimension-agnostic and are unchanged; `TasteSurvey.tsx` renders by mapping `SURVEY_QUESTIONS` and needed no rendering change. ONE deviation from the investigation, worth naming: that component also hardcodes the word "Three" in its sub-heading, so the copy moved to "Four" — the mapping needed nothing, the count did. `page.tsx`'s skip path went from a `{style,foods,dietary}` literal to `EMPTY_ANSWERS`, so the next dimension reaches it automatically instead of filing a document missing a field. TESTS: `tastePreferences.test.ts` 35→38 (four dimensions in order, the five-option closed set, style as pure vibe, and the retirement proof); `plannerPreferences.test.ts` 18→25 (activity projection, the place-kind-not-feature invariant, the contradiction sweep EXTENDED to activity phrases in `category_signals` — their real destination, a different field from the two already swept — and five leak-guard cases). Deliberately NO test asserts personalized plan CONTENT: a mock that agrees with the fixture proves the fixture, not the prompt, and this project has been burned by exactly that. **REVERT-RUN, and it was run in BOTH DIRECTIONS because a strip can fail two ways.** Disabling the strip (`return false`) turned exactly 2 red — the two that assert it fires — and left the "a genuine constraint is never stripped" case green, correctly, since a disabled strip strips nothing. Loosening equality to containment (`phrase.includes(constraint)`) turned exactly 1 DIFFERENT test red — the genuine-constraint case, where "live music" was eaten by "live music venue" — and left the other two green. So the suite distinguishes under-firing from over-firing separately rather than merely noticing "the guard changed". Restored to 25/25. VERIFIED on a webpack production build at :3200 (port 3000 untouched): the guest contract holds (`GET /api/profile` → `{profile:null,readFailed:false}`), the landing page renders, and `POST /api/parse` with NO auth — the un-personalized path, through the real route including the new strip — planned `park` + `live music venue` + `restaurant`, all `confident:false` with three questions and no constraints, which is the vague behaviour unchanged. NOT VERIFIED BY EXECUTION, stated plainly: the survey overlay itself in a browser. All six `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*` values are configured, so it would mount for a real account — but reaching it needs the owner's Google popup, which an agent must not complete, the same boundary 3A recorded. Its content comes from `SURVEY_QUESTIONS`, which is pinned by unit test. Gate: lint 0 errors (11 long-standing warnings, unchanged), typecheck clean, 42 unit suites green, webpack build, 79/81 mock e2e. The 2 failures are the known clock-dependent window tests, and that was PROVEN rather than assumed: they are `scenarios.spec.ts:501` and `:524` (not the :427/:450 in the task note, which had gone stale), both use 5-9pm/7-9pm windows, the run happened at 20:34 local so `windowUnderway` clamps the start to now and leaves 26 minutes, and stashing every change and re-running those same two specs on clean main reproduced both failures identically.

## Feature: a swap that can't be reached in time PUSHES the later stops back instead of refusing
Goal: when the venue a user swaps in is farther away than the one it replaces, the plan should move the rest of the evening back to make room, rather than refusing with "The replacement can't be reached by 10:19 PM without moving the slot". Refuse only where a real limit blocks it — a venue that would be shut by the time you got there — and ASK, rather than decide, when the only thing in the way is the finish time the user themselves named.
What should be done: reach the cascade that already exists (`resettleTail`) from the inbound-unreachable case instead of refusing there; let the swapped slot's start move LATER and never earlier; inherit the closing-time, locked-stop and real-leg guarantees rather than re-implementing any of them; add ONE new constraint — the user's stated end-time, which nothing enforced after a swap — and make that one a confirmation rather than a refusal, with decline as the safe default that leaves the stored plan byte-identical.
What was done (technical): **THE CASCADE WAS ALREADY BUILT AND ALREADY CORRECT; THE WORK WAS ONE SEAM, ONE NEW GUARD, AND ONE DIALOG.** `resettleTail` (`swap.ts`) already walked the WHOLE downstream tail, recomputed every start from a real `getSingleLeg` rather than a delta, re-checked openness at each new arrival through `usableForProposal` (including the open-at-END occupancy check), kept each venue where it could and adapted where it could not, refused when a stop was stranded, and never moved a locked or past stop. None of it was touched. **(1) THE SEAM — `planAnchorInbound` REPORTS A FACT INSTEAD OF RENDERING A VERDICT.** It used to compare `prevStop.end_time + leg` against the committed start and return `null` when it didn't reach — collapsing "too far to make it" into the same `null` as six genuinely unverifiable cases (missing location, unparseable boundary, leg fetch failure). It now returns `earliestStartMs` alongside the leg, and each of the three call sites sets its own policy: `finalize` PUSHES, while `timeChange` and `durationChange` keep refusing via a shared `reachesStart` — a time swap NAMED that time, and quietly landing the user at 8:20 because 8:00 was unreachable answers a different question than the one they asked. With no previous timed stop `earliestStartMs` IS the requested start (the origin is home; a home departure is not a committed boundary), so the first stop never pushes — today's behaviour, now explicit rather than incidental. **(2) `Math.max(requested, earliest)` IS THE ENTIRE "NEVER EARLIER" GUARANTEE**, one operator, and it also makes the no-push path byte-identical to the old one, which is what lets every existing plain-swap test still pin it unchanged. **(3) ONE REORDER WAS FORCED AND IS LOAD-BEARING:** `usableForProposal` asks whether the venue is open for the whole slot, and it cannot answer that before the slot is resolved — so the inbound leg is now planned BEFORE the usability check rather than after it, and the venue is judged at the RESOLVED start. A venue open at 9:00 may well be shut by the 9:25 you would actually arrive, and pushing the slot is exactly what creates that gap. **(4) THE ONE NET-NEW CONSTRAINT: `itinerary.plannedEndISO`.** Nothing enforced the user's stated end after a swap, and the investigation's finding held up — `checkWindowFit` is imported at `page.tsx:6` and called at `page.tsx:1029` and nowhere else, against a planner result the browser was still holding in memory. `Itinerary` had no end field, `ParsedPrompt` carries only prose `time_window`, and the POST body never received one. The resolved instant is now persisted at creation (`page.tsx` `storeItinerary` → `parseOptionalInstant` → `createItinerary`), for the same reason `ownerIsAnonymous` is recorded at creation: it outlives the request that knew it. **ABSENT MEANS NO CEILING** — most prompts, and every plan made before this shipped — and the push then runs as late as real opening hours allow; code never invents an end, the same rule the planner works under. **(5) THE SOFT LIMIT ASKS; THE HARD ONES REFUSE, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS A FACT ABOUT WHO OWNS THE DECISION.** A closing time is physics: consent cannot open a closed door, so a stranded stop refuses and must never prompt (asserted directly — the closing-time cases assert `confirm === undefined` WITH a stated end present, so the hard limit is proven to win over the soft one). A stated end is the user's own intent, so `SwapResult` gains an optional `confirm: EndTimeConfirm` on its `swapped: false` arm — present means a QUESTION, absent means a refusal. Keeping `swapped: false` is the whole mechanism behind "decline costs nothing": the route already maps it to `changed: false`, so no CAS runs and the proposal was never committed in the first place. Accept re-sends `endTimeAccepted: true`, which skips ONLY that guard; it re-runs the swap rather than replaying a cached proposal, because a server-side proposal cache would be new state with a TTL and an invalidation problem on a serverless deployment where the next request lands on a different instance — `version` still rides along, so a plan that moved underneath 409s instead. The tolerance is the SHARED `WINDOW_OVERRUN_TOLERANCE_MINUTES` (30) rather than a second number: two definitions of "past the stated window" in one codebase is how creation silently accepts a 25-minute overrun and then a swap interrupts over five. **THE JUDGMENT CALL, FLAGGED:** the task said "after the stated end-time", which read strictly is zero tolerance; reusing the existing constant is a deliberate interpretation and a one-line change if it should be strict. **(6) THE "5 ATTEMPTS" TURNED OUT TO BE RETRIES-PER-STOP, NOT CASCADE DEPTH** — it is the `findReplacement` candidate loop for ONE stop (`attempted.add(...); continue`), while the cascade is `for (k = anchorPos + 1; k < timedIdx.length; k++)`, the whole tail, unbounded, terminating at a locked stop or the end of the list. Nothing was raised or removed; there was no artificial ceiling, and the file now says so. Also corrected: the line refs carried into this task were stale — `planAnchorInbound` is at `:837` (not ~:738/:1848), `resettleTail` at `:1632` (not ~:1531/:1873), and `finalize`'s `startISO` argument is passed at `:1417` (not ~:1269). **(7) UI** is `SwapEndTimeDialog.tsx`, mounted beside `StopItineraryDialog` and reusing its `.stopdlg` language rather than inventing a second dialog vocabulary; decline is the safe default at every level (escape, initial focus on "Keep it as it is", and nothing written server-side). Two client fixes the push forced: the swapped stop now gets its own strike-through when its slot moved, read by INDEX because `oldById` is keyed by venue id and the venue is precisely what changed; and the `finally` block no longer yanks focus back into the strip when a dialog just opened. **BOTH REVERT-RUNS DISCRIMINATE.** (i) `endsAfterStatedEnd` short-circuited to null → "END-TIME: a push past the STATED end asks instead of applying" fails, reporting `swapped: true` with the day silently moved to 12:05 AM on an 11 PM plan. (ii) `usableThroughSlot` short-circuited to true → TWO cases fail, the new "PUSH: a stop stranded past its CLOSING time refuses honestly" AND the pre-existing "OPEN-AT-END: a venue that closes mid-slot is rejected" — which is the evidence that the push path INHERITS the closing-time guard rather than carrying a copy of it. Both restored and re-verified. **ONE EXISTING TEST WAS REWRITTEN, DELIBERATELY:** `swap.atomic.test.ts`'s "farther venue replacement cannot reach the fixed target slot" pinned exactly the refusal this feature removes. It now pins the new contract on the same fixture — a 4-stop plan, so it proves a THREE-deep cascade — plus an assertion that the committed chain is internally consistent (no stop starts before the previous ends), and a new sibling case proving a stranded push leaves the plan byte-identical. **GATE:** `npm run check` green — 42 unit suites (swap 79 cases, up from 70; atomic 13, up from 12), 81 mock e2e across 11 spec files (up from 79), lint 0 errors with the same 11 long-standing warnings, webpack build clean. **LIVE-VERIFIED on :3200** (mock data sources, real engine, :3000 untouched) across five cases: no stated end → push applies (drinks 7:48→8:38 PM, dessert 9:01→10:41 PM); stated 9:30 PM → asks with overrun 111 min and decline leaves the store byte-identical; `endTimeAccepted` → applies; a pushed stop that would close is ADAPTED where a later sibling exists and REFUSES with `confirm` absent where none does; and a plain fitting swap still takes the no-shift path with `downstreamShifted: []`.

## Feature: a swap can change the KIND of a stop ("board games instead" on a bar)
Goal: let a user swap a stop into something genuinely different — a board-game cafe instead of the bar, coffee instead of drinks, "something active" — rather than only ever getting another venue of the same kind. The hard part is telling a CATEGORY CHANGE ("board games instead") apart from an ordinary same-kind REFINEMENT ("something more chill"), and doing it without letting a category change disturb the plain swaps that already worked.
What should be done: teach the interpret step that a complaint NAMING a different kind of place sets `category`; make the engine honour that answer, search that kind's pool, and give the stop the new kind's own honest length; fix the constraint trap that would otherwise make every category change refuse permanently; name the requested kind in the refusals; and close the long-standing hole where a venue was only ever checked open at the moment of ARRIVAL. An honest refusal when the new kind doesn't fit the slot — never rescheduling other stops to force it (that is a separate, deferred feature).
What was done (technical): **THE PLUMBING WAS ALREADY ~70% THERE, AND THE WORK WAS FOUR SMALL GAPS PLUS ONE CORRECTNESS FIX.** `interpretRefinement` already returned a `category`, `venueSwap` already routed it into `poolKey` on the research path, and `finalize` already wrote the new kind onto the stop and reset its duration (two existing cases, `swap.test.ts` "swap whose new pick runs longer…" and "VENUE swap that CHANGES category…", covered exactly that). Nothing needed re-plumbing; no new `intent` value, no client-contract change (`parseSwapPayload` still validates the same four `path` values). **(1) THE TRAP, FIXED FIRST BECAUSE NOTHING ELSE WORKS UNDER IT.** `REFINE_SYSTEM`'s "constraint" branch said to fold a different KIND of venue into `constraints`, so "board games instead" arrives as category "board game cafe" AND constraints ["board game cafe"]. `constraintEvidence` proves constraints from six provider booleans, so a KIND of place is unprovable by construction: `placeMeetsAllConstraints` is false for every candidate that exists, the correction retry fails identically, and the swap refuses with unmet_constraint forever — the same failure the price words had, wearing a different word. The strip sits beside the price strip and is an EQUALITY test against the new category (`normalizeConstraint(constraint) === normalizeConstraint(poolKey)`, reusing the judge's own normaliser), deliberately NOT a keyword list: "with a patio" is a genuine provider-provable constraint and any list broad enough to catch "board games" would eventually eat it. **(2) THE SILENT-DROP SEAM.** `poolKey` read `interp.path === "research" ? interp.category : target.category`, so a model that renamed the category while leaving path "refilter" had the change discarded silently — "board games instead" handed back another bar with nothing reporting the request had been dropped. Now ONE `path` is derived once (`interp.path === "research" || changesCategory`) and drives the pool, the search parse, AND the path reported back. That last one was found by the new test rather than by reading: the first cut forced only the POOL, so the engine searched a new category through the refilter parse and then told the client it had narrowed the old pool. **(3) REFUSAL COPY** names the requested kind, and picks the right article — "Couldn't find a board game cafe that fits" (there was never a first one) vs the unchanged "another bar". **(4) PROMPT.** A dedicated `"category"` section with examples on BOTH sides, plus an explicit tie-breaker ("if unsure, keep the category — a same-kind swap the user did not want is a small miss; changing the kind rewrites their day"). The guardrail phrases are pinned two ways: an engine test that a same-category interpretation cannot be hijacked (asserting `path === "refilter"`, which is what proves `changesCategory` did not fire), and a test on `REFINE_SYSTEM` itself, because the instruction lives nowhere else and a careless rewrite is invisible to every other test. Neither can pin the live model's judgment, and the entry says so. **(5) OPEN-AT-END, AND TWO DELIBERATE NARROWINGS OF IT.** Openness was judged at the slot START and nowhere else — `isOpenAt` is a single-instant comparison and every caller passed the start — so a coffee shop closing at 9:30 was a legal answer for a 9:00–10:00 slot. `usableForProposal` now also checks the far end through `deps.isUsableAt` (the ONE availability seam, so a reservation API replaces both ends at once; keep-on-missing inherited unchanged), and `slotMinutes` is a REQUIRED parameter so no future call site can silently keep the old behaviour — the compiler found all five. FIRST NARROWING: the check is against OCCUPANCY, not `total`. The task specified total; the e2e disagreed, loudly and correctly. A stop's total is occupancy plus the table's scheduling margin, and judging against it adapted Ten O'Clock Curfew (4.7, closes 22:00) away from a 20:55–21:55 drinks stop whose buffer ran to 22:05 — the bar was open the entire time anyone was inside it. `occupancyMinutes` mirrors `buildStop`'s own split. The instant checked is one minute inside the end, because occupancy is [start, end) and `isOpenAt` reads the closing minute as closed, so closing exactly when you leave is an ordinary plan, not a conflict (pinned, along with a "closes inside the trailing buffer only" case). SECOND NARROWING: the whole-slot rule applies to venues this swap COMMITS or MOVES — the replacement it picked, a resized stop, a shifted stop — not to a downstream stop that keeps its committed arrival. The initial pipeline's filter is still arrival-only, so re-judging an untouched stop under a stricter rule let an unrelated price swap silently re-kind a stop the user never touched. That asymmetry is real and is FOLLOW-UP: the fix belongs in `filterPools`, not in a swap. **DURATION POLICY IS HONEST LENGTH, UNCHANGED:** a category-changed stop takes the new kind's default (bar 70 → museum 120), `resettleTail` absorbs it downstream, and when it strands a locked stop the swap REFUSES with the collision named and plan-then-commit leaving the itinerary byte-identical — pinned by a new case. The deferred "push later stops back" feature stays deferred. **MOCK E2E:** `mockSwapDeps.interpret` hardcoded the category and could not emit a change; it now has a `MOCK_CATEGORY_CHANGES` table (board games → board game cafe, coffee instead → coffee shop) that deliberately returns the shape a real model has been SEEN to return rather than the tidy one — path "refilter" disagreeing with its own answer, and the kind ALSO leaked into constraints — because `mockSelect` runs the REAL `placeMeetsAllConstraints`, so both guarantees are exercised end to end or the swap refuses. **BOTH REVERT-RUNS DISCRIMINATE.** (i) Constraint strip disabled → the unit case fails on "the kind must not reach the judge as a hard constraint" AND the e2e fails with Velvet Fig still in the strip (the permanent refusal, live). (ii) `usableThroughSlot` short-circuited to true → "a venue that closes at 21:30 must not fill a stop occupying 21:00–22:00" fails, with the engine reporting `swapped: true` on the closes-mid-slot venue. Both restored and re-verified. **GATE:** 70/70 swap unit cases (62 before), 42 unit suites unchanged (cases were added to `swap.test.ts`, no new suite), 79 mock e2e (77 before), `npm run check` green; the only e2e failures are the two clock-dependent stated-window cases, confirmed by a back-to-back stash run to reproduce identically on unmodified main.

## Fix: a price-direction swap with nothing priced to compare returned an arbitrary venue
Goal: the price-direction fix in the entry below made "fancier"/"cheaper" honest wherever prices exist — but on a stop whose category Google prices for nobody, a park or an attraction, the swap still returned a venue, and it was an arbitrary one. "Fancier" on High Park was simply a different park, handed back as the pricier answer.
What should be done: when a price-direction swap has no price signal to act on — nothing priced to compare against — refuse honestly rather than return a no-price venue as the pricier/cheaper result. Keep the best effort that works (current stop unpriced but candidates priced → still the most extreme priced one), keep the verified behaviour for stops that DO have price data, leave a plain "swap this" untouched, and leave keep-on-missing intact for ordinary swaps.
What was done (technical): **THE WHOLE REGRESSION WAS ONE LINE, AND IT WAS AT THE CALL SITE RATHER THAN IN THE RANKING.** `rankByPriceDirection` was already correct — `ranked` has never contained an unpriced venue — but `venueSwap` read it loosely: `const eligible = ranked.length > 0 ? ranked : unpriced`. Traced on a park: no `priceLevel` on the current stop → `currentRank` null → `bestEffort` true → `priced` empty (Places populates price for food and drink and leaves parks and attractions blank) → `ranked` empty → `eligible` becomes the ENTIRE unpriced pool, in pool order, and the selector's pick comes back as the "fancier" result. The only exclusion is still "venues currently in the plan", so that is the PRE-FIX behaviour surviving inside the fix: the arbitrary answer this block exists to prevent, now wearing a price claim. **THE FALLBACK WAS REACHABLE IN TWO SHAPES, NOT ONE, AND BOTH BREAK THE SAME RULE** — a price-direction swap must never present a no-price venue AS a price-direction result. The park (nothing priced on either side) is the reported shape; the second is a PRICED stop whose alternatives are unpriced, which real data hits constantly — the live $$$$ dinner run below had 4 unpriced candidates in a 19-venue pool, and the old line would have handed one of them back instead of refusing. So the fix is not a park special case: `ranked` IS the eligible set, and an empty `ranked` refuses. **THIS IS NOT A KEEP-ON-MISSING VIOLATION, AND THE DISTINCTION IS WORTH STATING PRECISELY**, because that invariant is load-bearing everywhere else. It forbids DROPPING a venue for a missing field — every filter rule and every ordinary swap still keeps these venues, verified live (a plain "don't like it" on the same all-unpriced park pool returned Riverdale Park East) and now pinned by a unit case whose plain-swap pool carries an unpriced candidate that still reaches the selector. What it does not require is answering a question ABOUT price with a venue that has no price. The venue is set aside for being unanswerable, not for being incomplete, and only in the one swap where price is the criterion the user named. `CLAUDE.md` records the exception on the invariant itself and gained the price-direction rule it never had (`AGENTS.md` gets a one-line pointer), because the next reader would otherwise "fix" this straight back — and this entry supersedes the previous one's "explicit LAST RESORT" description of unpriced candidates. **TWO REFUSALS, SPLIT ON A FACT RATHER THAN A TONE.** "It's already the priciest one I can find nearby" is a claim about prices; with nothing priced to compare against it asserts data we never had, and on a $ venue it is an outright lie. So the split runs on `comparable` — a new field on `PriceDirectionRanking`, computed as `priced.length > 0`, a fact about the data rather than a decision, so the pure ranking still decides nothing about refusal. `comparable` true keeps the existing message byte-for-byte (the e2e specs pin it); `comparable` false says "Can't compare prices for this <category> — none of the nearby options have a price listed, so I can't show you a pricier one." Note the split is on the CANDIDATES, not on `bestEffort`: a priced stop with an unpriced pool is the same can't-compare fact as a park, which is why `bestEffort` alone would have been the wrong gate. The `swap_price_direction` log gains `comparable`, and its outcome is now `ranked` / `refused_none_in_direction` / `refused_no_price_data` — `unpriced_fallback` is gone because the path is gone. TESTS: `budget.test.ts` 14 → 15 cases (the new one pins `comparable` on the park pool, on a priced stop with an unpriced pool, on both single-priced-candidate cases and on the empty pool, and asserts the unpriced candidates are still all returned — reported, never ranked); `swap.test.ts` 61 → 62 (a new engine case driving the park end to end: refusal both directions, the can't-compare message, NOT the already-priciest one, an empty offer list proving code refused before the judge, and the plan untouched; the old "unpriced is a last resort" case rewritten to assert the refusal and the already-priciest message, since $$$ IS priced and simply isn't pricier than $$$$; and the plain-swap case extended with an unpriced candidate that must still reach the selector). **REVERT-RUN:** restoring `const eligible = ranked.length > 0 ? ranked : unpriced` (and `candidates = eligible`) turned exactly 2 red — the new park case and the rewritten unpriced case, on the assertions "an all-unpriced pool must not produce a price swap" and "an unpriced venue must not answer a price request" — leaving the other 60 green, including every strictness, ping-pong, best-effort, query-broadening and plain-swap case. Restored to 62/62 and 15/15. **FIVE PRE-EXISTING SWAP TESTS WENT RED ON THE REAL FIX, AND THE PREVIOUS ENTRY HAD ALREADY PREDICTED THEM** — its second revert-run noted that dropping unpriced candidates turns pre-existing tests red "because the default test venue has no price and the existing suite leans on keep-on-missing". Read at the source, all five pass "somewhere cheaper" as a generic venue complaint while their subject is something else entirely: slot mechanics, refilter-vs-research classification, a customized duration surviving a venue swap, description plumbing, and the §3.1 no-stored-parse fallback. They were being routed through the price engine by a word they did not mean. Fixed by re-wording the refinement to "somewhere else", NOT by giving the default fixture a price: a slot-mechanics test should not depend on the price path at all, and pricing the fixture would have hidden the coupling rather than removed it. VERIFIED LIVE on a webpack production build at :3200 against REAL Groq and REAL Google Places, with the premise measured rather than assumed — a real "park Toronto" search returns 20 venues and 0 of them carry a `priceLevel`, while "dinner Toronto" returns 10 with 5 priced. On a High Park stop: "somewhere fancier" and "somewhere cheaper" both refused with the can't-compare message and left the stop untouched (`comparable:false`, `rankedCount:0`, `unpricedCount:19`, `outcome:refused_no_price_data`). The working path is unchanged: a $$ Fox on John climbed to $$$$ Daphne on "fancier" and descended to $ Juicy Dumpling on "cheaper"; a $$$$ Daphne stop refused with the untouched "already the priciest one I can find nearby" (`comparable:true`, 19 candidates, 0 ranked, 4 unpriced — the exact case the old fallback would have answered with an unpriced venue). Best effort survives: the same Fox on John with its `priceLevel` field removed still swapped, to $$$$ Daphne. And a plain "don't like it" on the all-unpriced park pool swapped normally to Riverdale Park East. Gate: `npm run check` green — lint 0 errors (11 long-standing warnings, unchanged), typecheck, 42 unit suites, webpack build, 77/77 mock e2e; the two known clock-dependent e2e failures did not reproduce this run.

## Fix: price-direction swaps ("fancier" / "cheaper") were a no-op that ping-ponged
Goal: asking a stop to be "fancier" or "cheaper" did nothing to the price. A ramen stop swapped for a different ramen shop at the SAME tier, and a second "fancier" handed back the original — A→B→A forever. "cheaper" was broken differently: a flat ≤$$ ceiling rather than a decrease relative to the venue you were looking at.
What should be done: make a price-direction swap move in the requested direction RELATIVE TO THE CURRENT VENUE's own `priceLevel` — strictly higher for "fancier", strictly lower for "cheaper", and an honest refusal when nothing genuinely moves that way, never a same-tier shuffle. Broaden the search so there ARE candidates in that direction to rank. Keep it entirely in code (price is a verifiable fact), never drop a venue for missing price data, and leave a plain "swap this" on its existing path.
What was done (technical): **THE ROOT CAUSE WAS THAT `parseBudget` ANSWERS A DIFFERENT QUESTION.** It reads a stated CEILING, and only cheap-ward — `fancier`, `upscale`, `splurge` and `$$$` all fall through it to null, and `hardPriceLevelMaximum` is a maximum by construction. With null, the objective filter's price rule never fires and `selectVenues` is handed `budget: null`, so the swap degraded to its default: "any candidate not currently in the plan". The only exclusion is venues presently in the itinerary (no memory of what was already shown), which is exactly a two-cycle. A direction is a different question from a ceiling, so it got its own parser rather than a widened `BudgetSpec`. **TWO LAYERS, AND ONLY ONE OF THEM IS A GUARANTEE.** Layer 1 (`rankByPriceDirection` in `budget.ts`, a `PRICE_PATTERN` block in `venueSwap`) is deterministic code reading the RAW refinement — modelled directly on the existing `CLOSER_PATTERN` distance block, which was already the proven-safe shape: read the raw text, rank against the current stop's own value, refuse honestly when nothing beats it. Only strictly-in-direction candidates reach the selector, so the model judges FIT among options already proven to move the right way and never compares prices itself. Layer 2 broadens the Places query (`aesthetic` is the one `buildQuery` input that reaches the text query verbatim, so `"upscale"` / `"cheap casual"` rides in there) — but it is a HELPER, not a guarantee, and the code says so at the call site: Google's notion of "upscale" is fuzzy and returns same-tier venues freely. Layer 2 without layer 1 would be a quieter version of the same bug. **MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED, ON REAL DATA:** a live `"ramen Toronto"` search returns 20 venues — 16 MODERATE, 4 INEXPENSIVE, and NOTHING above MODERATE. That is the reported bug's whole mechanism visible in one line: "fancier" had literally nowhere to go, so the old code returned a different mid-tier shop. `"upscale ramen Toronto"` returns 12, all VERY_EXPENSIVE; `"cheap casual ramen Toronto"` returns 17, all INEXPENSIVE. **MISSING PRICE IS HANDLED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS AND THEY ARE DIFFERENT CASES.** No price on the CURRENT venue means no comparison is possible, so it is BEST EFFORT (hand back the most extreme priced candidate) — refusing there would be refusing BECAUSE of missing data, which keep-on-missing forbids. A candidate with no price is never dropped, but it is an explicit LAST RESORT: a venue proven to move the right way always outranks an unknown, and the unpriced ones are used only when nothing is proven. Both are named in the type (`ranked` / `unpriced` / `bestEffort`) rather than left implicit. **"CHEAPER" WAS FIXED IN THE SAME PASS AND ITS FIX IS SUBTLER THAN IT LOOKS.** The flat cap came from the MODEL: it emits budget `"cheap"`, `parseBudget` turns that into a hard ≤MODERATE ceiling, and on a $$$$ stop that hid the genuinely-cheaper $$$ options entirely. So on a direction swap the judge now uses the PLAN's own stated budget rather than the model's echo of the refinement — the relative rule is the price rule, while a budget the user actually stated still applies. **THE CONSTRAINTS TRAP WAS REAL AND I ONLY GUARDED HALF OF IT UNTIL LIVE TESTING.** `constraintEvidence` emits six provider booleans, so a price word in `constraints` makes `placeMeetsAllConstraints` false for EVERY venue — a permanent unmet_constraint refusal. Two producers were guarded up front: the Groq-failure `localFallback`, which appends the raw refinement to constraints (so a busy model would have failed every price swap for the wrong reason), and a code-side strip of the model's own constraints. Live, `"somewhere nicer"` came back as `intent: constraint` with `constraints: ["nicer"]` and still refused — because `buildQuery` splices constraints into the Places text query verbatim, so the query was `"upscale nicer ramen Toronto"` and the results collapsed back to mid-tier ramen. The strip had been applied to the JUDGE parse but not the SEARCH parse. Fixed to cover both, with a test that pins the search side specifically; only the MODEL's constraints are filtered, never the plan's own. A third floor keeps a price phrase from being classified as a time/duration request, the same way `parseTimeExpr`/`parseDurationExpr` already override the model's classification. **The refusal is logged, not just the success** — `swap_price_direction` fires before the early return with the pool size, ranked count and outcome, because a refusal is the event a user actually notices and "how big was the pool" is the first question when one refuses too often. TESTS: 12 new cases in `budget.test.ts` (the parser including the negation trap — "less fancy"/"not too expensive" all match the up-pattern and read backwards without it — the British "fancy a drink" verb, strict ranking both ways, same-tier exclusion, relative-vs-flat-cap, best effort, keep-on-missing, purity) and 12 in `swap.test.ts` (strict offers both ways, the honest refusal leaving the itinerary untouched, THE PING-PONG pinned as a second "fancier" refusing rather than walking back down, best effort, the unpriced last resort, query broadening, a plain no-price-word swap taking the untouched path, and both halves of the constraints trap). **REVERT-RUNS, both discriminating and turning DIFFERENT sets red:** relaxing `>`/`<` to `>=`/`<=` (allow same-tier) turned exactly 3 red in `budget.test.ts` (both strict-ranking cases and the same-tier case) and 2 in `swap.test.ts`, leaving every keep-on-missing and best-effort case green; dropping unpriced candidates instead of keeping them turned 2 OTHER red in `budget.test.ts` and 6 in `swap.test.ts` — including four PRE-EXISTING swap tests, because the default test venue has no price and the existing suite leans on keep-on-missing — while leaving all the strictness cases green. Restored to 14/14 and 61/61. **THE FIRST REVERT-RUN ALSO EXPOSED A WEAK E2E AND IT WAS FIXED RATHER THAN NOTED:** with same-tier allowed the ping-pong spec still passed, because the dinner fixtures simply ran out above $$$ — a broken gate and a working one looked identical. A same-tier sibling (`Brass and Bone`, $$$, 4.0, on Velvet Fig's hours) was added so the leak has somewhere to show; it is the lowest-rated dinner fixture and shares Velvet Fig's hours precisely so it cannot displace a pick another spec pins. With it, the broken gate returns Brass and Bone and the spec fails. The two e2e tests that encoded the old behaviour were rewritten: the price-refresh one now also asserts the result is STRICTLY fewer dollar signs, and the repeated-swap one (whose comment used to document "re-filters with no budget cap" as intended) now asserts a refusal at the top of the pool, a strict move down, a strict move up, and a second "fancier" refusing instead of bouncing. VERIFIED LIVE on a webpack production build at :3200 against REAL Groq and REAL Google Places, seeding plans from real Toronto ramen venues: from a MODERATE shop, "fancier" → VERY_EXPENSIVE, "cheaper" → INEXPENSIVE, "somewhere nicer" → VERY_EXPENSIVE, "less fancy" → INEXPENSIVE (the negation reading holds against a real model). "fancier" twice on one stop moved up once and then REFUSED — it neither walked back down nor returned to the original. "fancier" on a VERY_EXPENSIVE venue refused and left the stop untouched. A stop with its price field removed still swapped (best effort), rather than refusing over missing data. A plain "swap this" took the untouched path. **NOT VERIFIED, AND WORTH THE OWNER'S EYE: the CATEGORY DRIFT layer 2 buys.** `"upscale ramen Toronto"` returns Miku, La Banane and Antler Kitchen — upscale Japanese and other, not ramen. Google's relevance trades category fidelity for the price adjective, so a "fancier" swap on a ramen stop can legitimately land on a non-ramen restaurant. Layer 1 still guarantees the PRICE direction, and the swap keeps the slot, but whether that is the right product answer (versus keeping the cuisine and refusing more often) is a judgment call, and the lever is the phrase in `priceDirectionSearchTerm` — one line. Gate: `npm run check` — lint 0 errors (11 long-standing warnings, unchanged), typecheck, 42 unit suites, webpack build, 76/77 mock e2e. The one failure is the known clock-dependent `"dinner and drinks from 5-9pm"` window spec, which fails IDENTICALLY on unmodified `main` at this hour (verified by stashing the whole change and re-running it): at 6:20 PM local the stated window is underway, `windowUnderway()` clamps it to ~2h40m, and two stops genuinely no longer fit.

## Feature: Stage 3B — spend the taste profile on vague prompts (+ the food-clustering fix)
Goal: Stage 3A has been storing style/foods/dietary for signed-in users and nothing has ever read them. Make a vague request quietly land on what this person actually likes, while any explicit request overrides completely — and in the same prompt pass, stop vague prompts from collapsing into "dinner and a bar".
What should be done: read the stored profile server-side for the VERIFIED caller only and hand it to the planner as background; apply it PER ASPECT (cuisine, vibe, kind of activity) so a stated aspect always wins and only the unstated ones take a default; make it a confident default rather than a faint tilt; keep it invisible — no badge, no "because you like X"; make a stored dietary a soft default that can never become a fail-loud contradiction against an explicit request; add variety guidance so an un-named day is not all food; and leave the no-profile path — every guest, every mock e2e run, everyone who skipped — planning exactly as it did before.
What was done (technical): **THE MODEL DOES THE JUDGING, WHICH IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** No code writes an `aesthetic`, a searchQuery or a constraint from a preference. The projection hands the planner a small `preferences` object and the PROMPT says how to weigh it, because "does this request already state a cuisine?" is a semantic question and the architecture rule puts those on the model — and its answer goes through the same unchanged validator ladder (`findPlanProblems` → one correction retry → deterministic fallback) as any other plan. That also makes the next two or three tuning rounds pure prompt wording rather than re-plumbing, which was the stated shape of this stage. **THE PROJECTION IS ITS OWN MODULE** (`app/api/parse/plannerPreferences.ts`), pure, beside the planner that spends it rather than inside `tastePreferences.ts` — "what the survey stores" and "what the survey MEANS to a plan" are two things that should be able to change independently, and 3A's file still exports no "apply" of any kind (its header comment was corrected, since "nothing reads these" is now false). Three decisions live there. (1) A preference the app cannot act on is never sent: `dietary: ["none"]` is the ABSENCE of a restriction and would read to a model as the presence of one, `foods: ["other"]` names no cuisine — both are dropped, and a profile that drops to nothing returns NULL, not an empty object the model still has to interpret. (2) The planner's words are not the survey's words: a second small phrase table, not `SurveyOption.label`, because "Cultural (art, music, history)" is BUTTON COPY and rewording a button must not change how plans are searched — and because `aesthetic` is prepended VERBATIM into the Places text query by `buildQuery`, so a phrase with an em-dash in it is a worse SEARCH, not just a worse sentence. A test pins that every phrase is punctuation-free and that every survey option is either given a phrase or explicitly listed as non-planning, so adding an option forces a decision about what it means. (3) The dietary suppression below. **THE ONE GUARANTEE MADE IN CODE RATHER THAN IN PROMPT WORDING IS THE DIETARY TRAP, and it was found by reading `contradictionReason` rather than by testing.** That guard joins the prompt with the parse and fails the WHOLE PLAN loud on "vegetarian" + "steakhouse". Stored vegetarian plus an explicit steakhouse would have hit it — a refusal manufactured out of a preference the user never restated, which is precisely what the task forbade. So the conflict is settled one step EARLIER: `toPlannerPreferences` takes the raw prompt and drops any diet the prompt contradicts before the model is even told about it, reusing the guard's own `DIETARY_VENUE_CONFLICTS` table through a new exported `dietaryConflictsWithPrompt` — a second copy of those pairs, drifting, is exactly how a suppressed case would start failing loud again. Only the CONFLICTING diet is dropped, so a vegan who also avoids gluten and asks for a steakhouse keeps the gluten-free lean; and the table's own distinction survives (halal + steakhouse is fine, halal + bacon is not) because it is reused rather than re-encoded. The prompt ALSO says a dietary preference belongs in the searchQuery and NEVER in `constraints` — `constraints` are hard requirements THIS request stated, and a preference is not one — but that half is wording, not a guarantee; see the honest note below. **THE UID IS THE VERIFIED ONE OR THERE ISN'T ONE.** `/api/parse` had never known about auth; what it learned is deliberately minimal — `verifyCaller(request)` then `readTasteProfile(caller.uid)`, the Stage 3A reader reused unchanged. No `?uid=`, nothing read from the body, no new schema field. Anonymous callers skip the Firestore read, and that is a COST decision rather than a second safety gate (the history reader's precedent argues against duplicating a write-side promise): `/api/profile` refuses the write for a guest, so the query has a known-empty answer, and this one sits on the planning hot path where every guest and every mock-e2e request would pay for it. Nothing in the path can fail a plan — `verifyCaller` returns null for a missing/unverifiable/uncheckable token, `readTasteProfile` never throws and reports a broken query as no profile, and all of those map to null, which is "no preferences were mentioned". `page.tsx` now sends its existing `authHeaders()` on the parse call, because the server cannot identify a caller who never introduced themselves. **THE NULL PATH IS THE ORDINARY PATH, not a special case**, and a test asserts it byte-for-byte: with no profile the payload has no `preferences` key at all and is character-identical to the one built before this stage existed. Mock e2e needs no new seam and got none — `readTasteProfile` already degrades to "no profile" with no Firebase, which is the mock-seam rule (data, never logic) already being satisfied by 3A. **FOOD CLUSTERING** is a new VARIETY section in the same prompt: when the user does not name the activities, at most one eating stop and one drinking stop and the rest something that is neither, with the caps released the moment the request names them ("dinner and drinks", "a bar crawl", "three restaurants") and a stated activity never dropped to make a day look varied. One log field added: `personalized` on `planner_plan` — WHETHER a profile shaped the plan, never WHICH preferences did, because a log line naming someone's dietary restriction is a different thing entirely. TESTS: new `plannerPreferences.test.ts` (18 cases — the closed-set completeness and query-safety invariants, all five "no injection" cases, dimension omission, the projection itself, four dietary-suppression cases including the halal split, a sweep proving no injectable phrase can supply half of a contradiction on its own, and the byte-identical payload claim). Deliberately NO test asserts personalized plan CONTENT: per this project's own lesson a mock that agrees with the fixture proves the fixture, not the prompt. **REVERT-RUNS, both discriminating and turning DIFFERENT sets red:** making the no-profile branch inject a fixed fake profile turned exactly 2 red (the direct no-profile case and the byte-identical payload case) and left every suppression and projection case green; deleting the dietary suppression filter turned exactly 3 OTHER red (all three suppression cases, including the halal split) and left the no-profile cases green — so the suite distinguishes the two guarantees separately rather than noticing "the function changed". Restored to 18/18. VERIFIED LIVE on a webpack production build at :3200 against REAL Groq and REAL Firestore, using a throwaway Admin-minted identity rather than anyone's Google account (profile: style cozy+cultural, foods japanese+italian, dietary vegetarian), each prompt run as a GUEST and as that user back to back. "dinner tonight": guest got a generic `restaurant` marked vague plus "What are you craving?"; the signed-in user got `italian restaurant` / `vegetarian Japanese restaurant`, confident, aesthetic `cozy`, and no craving question. "sushi dinner tonight": both got sushi — the stated cuisine was never overridden. "dinner and drinks tonight": both stops every time (`italian restaurant` + `wine bar` signed in), so the variety caps did not eat an explicit request. "the best steakhouse in town tonight": the signed-in vegetarian's plan was IDENTICAL to the guest's — `steakhouse`, empty constraints, no contradiction, no refusal — which is the suppression working, since the model never heard the word. "activities tonight" and "something to do tonight" both came back with one food stop at most and led with a non-food activity (`live music venue`, `jazz club`). A REAL anonymous ID token (848 chars) logged `personalized:false`, so the guest gate is proven on the branch the no-token runs do not reach; the `personalized` flag came out 11 true / 11 false across the paired runs. All scratch users and documents were deleted afterwards, verified zero remaining. **NOT VERIFIED, AND THE POINT OF HANDING BACK: whether the tuning is RIGHT.** Three things the owner should judge, all one-line prompt edits. (1) In ONE early run the model put `vegetarian` into `constraints` on a bare "dinner" despite the rule forbidding it; later runs put it in the searchQuery correctly. That is a real leak with a real consequence — a stored diet in `constraints` is a hard requirement `selectVenues` can refuse over — and if the eyeball loop reproduces it, the fix is either firmer wording or a code-side strip of preference-derived constraint terms. (2) The bias is currently STRONG: a bare "dinner" resolves to a favourite cuisine AND suppresses the craving question. That is the "confident default" the task asked for, but it is the first thing to soften if it feels presumptuous. (3) `style` fills `aesthetic` inconsistently — `cozy` on most prompts, `unspecified` on the steakhouse one. Also worth noting for the loop: identical prompts do not produce identical plans run to run (temperature 0 notwithstanding, the model chain and Groq itself are not bit-deterministic), so a single sample is never evidence either way. Gate: `npm run check` green — lint 0 errors (11 long-standing warnings, unchanged), typecheck, 42 unit suites, webpack build, 77/77 mock e2e. The two known clock-dependent e2e failures did not reproduce this run.

## Feature: Stage 3A — onboarding taste survey (capture only)
Goal: ask a brand-new signed-in user three quick questions about their taste — style, favourite foods, dietary restrictions — and store the answers under their account. CAPTURE ONLY: nothing reads these preferences yet. Using them to bias the planner is Stage 3B and is deliberately out of scope, so `planner.ts`, the pipeline and venue selection are untouched.
What should be done: a three-question, all-multi-select, all-optional survey shown ONCE, to real signed-in accounts with no profile yet — never to guests, never to returning users; a submit that confirms briefly and auto-closes into the app, and a skip that closes silently but is still RECORDED so the survey never nags again; a single profile document per user written through the Admin SDK for the VERIFIED caller only; and a write path that degrades to a no-op rather than an error when there is no identity or no Firebase, so mock e2e is unaffected.
What was done (technical): **A SKIP HAS TO WRITE, and that is the whole shape of the storage.** "Skip for now" means "not now", but "don't ask me every login" is the promise, so both paths call the same writer and `toTasteProfileDocument` always sets `surveySeen: true`. A second flag, `surveyCompleted`, records WHICH path it was — it gates nothing today and exists because "skipped" and "submitted with nothing ticked" produce identical answer sets, and 3B will want to tell a deliberate "no preferences" from a dismissal it never earned an answer to. **The gate is four distinct "no"s, and each is a case someone would otherwise hit:** not signed in (nobody to file under), anonymous (a guest has no account to save to — checked as `isAnonymous !== false`, matching `AppUser`'s own bias so an unclear provider payload reads as a guest), profile PRESENT (a returning user, whether they answered or skipped), and profile UNKNOWN (the read has not landed — showing the survey in that gap is exactly how a returning user gets asked twice). A fifth, profile FAILED, fails SAFE in the direction that costs least: a survey shown on a failed read is a survey shown to someone who already answered, and the write behind it would overwrite real preferences with a fresh blank set. Skipping one onboarding beats losing someone's data. **`shouldShowSurvey` is pure and lives in `tastePreferences.ts`**, not in an effect in `page.tsx`, which is why all five branches are testable without a browser. **Storage mirrors the history slice deliberately:** `profileStore.ts` is the ONE place `users/<uid>/profile/preferences` is spelled, both directions live in that one file, and every function is NON-THROWING — the write is the tail of an onboarding the user has already finished with, and the read only decides whether to ask a question. Neither is worth failing a request over. **`/api/profile` never fails because of who is asking**, inherited from `/api/history`: guest, no token, no Firebase all get 200. A 401 here would break mock e2e (no Firebase at all) and pop an error over a survey the user had already closed. The anonymity gate is enforced SERVER-side on the write, from the signed token — the client declining to show the survey to a guest is a UI decision made in a browser, and the load-bearing check is the one that reads `caller.isAnonymous`. There is no `?uid=` and no uid in the body; a forged one is ignored, verified live. **Options are stored as SLUGS, not labels** (`chill`, not "Chill & low-key"), so rewording a question cannot orphan every record that chose it, and `normalizeAnswerSet` intersects against the closed option set and returns the QUESTION's order — a stale client sending a retired option loses that option rather than its whole submission, and two records with the same answers compare equal. Empty dimensions normalise to `[]`, never `undefined`, because Firestore rejects `undefined` outright — the same reason `toArchivedPlan` normalises each optional stop field to null. **`TasteProfilePayload` is `Omit<TasteProfileDocument, "ownerUid">`** rather than redeclared, so a write-side shape change breaks the COMPILE. TESTS: new `tastePreferences.test.ts` (35 cases — the five gate branches, the closed option set, junk input of every shape, both flags on both paths, the no-undefined guarantee, and the response→gate composition). **REVERT-RUNS, both discriminating:** deleting the anonymity check turned exactly 3 red (the guest case, the unknown-anonymity case, and the guest-with-everything-else-met case) and left the returning-user cases green; changing the profile check from `=== "absent"` to `!== "unknown"` turned exactly 3 OTHER red (returning user, failed read, and the composition case) and left the guest cases green — so the suite distinguishes the two gates separately rather than merely noticing "the gate changed". Restored to 35/35. **ONE REAL DEFECT AND ONE FALSE ALARM, both worth recording.** The defect: `.empty > *:not(…)` sets `position: relative` and outranks a plain `.taste` rule, so the overlay would have been silently demoted to a rectangle mid-page instead of covering the viewport — `.auth` and `.hist` were already excluded there for exactly this reason and `.taste` now is too; the browser confirmed `position: fixed`. The false alarm: the confirmation measured 2988ms instead of ~2000, and I attributed it to the auto-close timer depending on `onClose`'s identity and being restarted by parent re-renders. Instrumenting `setTimeout` disproved that — the app schedules exactly ONE 2000ms timer and React removes the DOM 1ms after it fires. The real cause was the automation harness: `document.hidden === true`, and Chrome aligns timers in background tabs to 1-second boundaries, so five plain `setTimeout(…, 2000)` probes in the same tab measured 2597–3005ms. The timing was never wrong. The ref fix was KEPT anyway — the dependency genuinely was wrong and a parent re-rendering on an interval would hold the confirmation open indefinitely — but its comment now says it is a correctness fix, not a bug that was observed. VERIFIED live on a webpack production build at :3200, against REAL Firestore, using throwaway custom-token identities rather than anyone's Google account: a new signed-in user gets `{profile:null}` → survey shows; submitting writes the document (junk option dropped, canonical order, no undefined fields) and the same user's next read returns it → survey does NOT show; a different user who SKIPS gets `surveySeen:true, surveyCompleted:false` and is likewise not asked again; a REAL anonymous token is answered `saved:false` and NO document is written; a forged uid in the body is ignored; a malformed body is refused 400. In the browser: the survey renders all three questions (6/9/6 options), chips toggle with correct `aria-pressed`, the primary button is never disabled and relabels "Continue"→"Save preferences", submit shows "Perfect! You're all set" with no buttons and auto-closes to the landing screen, skip closes with NO confirmation screen at any point, a returning user is not asked, and a GUEST never even issues the profile request. All seven scratch users and six documents were deleted afterwards, verified zero remaining. NOT VERIFIED BY EXECUTION, stated plainly: sign-in through the real Google popup, which needs the owner's credentials and which an agent must not complete — the identities above were minted with the Admin SDK and carry `sign_in_provider: "custom"`, which the route's gate reads as non-anonymous exactly as a Google account would, so every branch downstream of "is this a real account" is genuinely exercised. Gate: `npm run check` green — lint 0 errors (11 long-standing warnings, unchanged), typecheck, 41 unit suites, webpack build, 77/77 mock e2e. The two known clock-dependent e2e failures did not reproduce this run.

## Feature: Stage 2 — a view-only History reader
Goal: Stage 1B has been WRITING concluded plans to Firestore since it shipped and nothing has ever read them back. Add a read-only History screen: a signed-in person sees their past outings and can open one to look at it. No re-running, editing, deleting or resuming — this stage adds a window, not a door.
What should be done: a server route that reads `users/{uid}/history` through the Admin SDK for the VERIFIED caller only, returning an empty list rather than an error whenever there is no identity to read for; a History screen reachable from the existing account corner, rendering only what was actually archived; a sign-in nudge for guests, whose plans were never archived by design; and nothing changed about what 1B writes.
What was done (technical): **THE ARCHIVE IS A PROJECTION, AND THAT DECIDES THE WHOLE SCREEN.** A history document holds an id, `createdAt`/`archivedAt`, a zone, and stops with names, categories, times and statuses — there is no title, no original prompt, no city, no coordinates, no legs. So the UI renders dates and stops and invents nothing; the row summary IS the stop names joined by arrows, because that is the most a row can honestly say. `historyView.ts` is where that discipline lives and `HistoryPlanPayload` is typed as `Omit<ArchivedPlan, "ownerUid">` rather than redeclared, so if the write side ever changes shape the reader stops COMPILING instead of quietly rendering blanks. **ONE definition of a usable record, used on both sides:** the server normalises every Firestore document through `parseHistoryPlan` before it goes on the wire and the browser runs the same function over the response — the same "both sides, server wins" arrangement `resolveStopOutcome` has, and the reason there is no second client-side notion of a valid record to drift. **THE READ SITS IN `history.ts` BESIDE THE WRITE**, because `historyCollection()` is the one place the path `users/<uid>/history` is spelled; a reader that re-derived that path elsewhere would be a second copy of the same rule. `archiveConcludedPlan` and everything under it is untouched. **THE ROUTE IS `/api/history`, NOT `/api/itinerary/history`** — deliberate: the writer is already the FILE `app/api/itinerary/history.ts`, so a route folder of that name would leave `history.ts` and `history/` side by side with `../history` resolving on file-before-directory precedence. It works today and is exactly the kind of thing that breaks quietly later; a top-level noun also matches `/api/parse`, `/api/places`, `/api/weather`, `/api/geocode`. **NO IDENTITY IS NOT AN ERROR.** A guest, a visitor with no token, a token that fails verification, a deployment with no Firebase — all get 200 and `{plans: [], readFailed: false}`, because all of them genuinely have no history. There is no `?uid=` on this route and never will be: the only uid it reads is the one the server proved. **`readFailed` exists because "empty" and "broken" must not look alike** — telling someone with twenty saved outings that they have none would read as data loss, so a Firestore query that throws returns an empty list WITH the flag, and the panel says "couldn't load your history just now" instead of "no saved plans yet". Everything is non-throwing, matching `archiveConcludedPlan`'s discipline. **Anonymous callers are NOT special-cased in the reader**, which was a real decision: a guest's query comes back empty on its own because 1B never wrote one, and adding a second anonymity gate here would duplicate the write-side promise and be free to drift from it. The reader just answers "what is filed under you". The PANEL still nudges guests, which is presentation, not a data gate. **The History entry point is offered to everyone, not only to accounts** — the sign-in nudge is the point of the guest state, and hiding the door would make it unreachable by exactly the people it is written for. TESTS: new `historyView.test.ts` (20 cases — zone rendering, which date an outing belongs to, missing/half/blank fields, junk records, ordering, the response envelope) and `app/api/history/route.test.ts` (6 cases — the never-401 promise from four angles, plus that the response names nobody). New `e2e/history.spec.ts` (4 tests) covers the guest path, the only one mock e2e can honestly reach: faking a signed-in account would fork product logic under test, which is the same rule that keeps the login screen from being an arrival gate. **REVERT-RUNS, both discriminating:** making the no-caller branch throw 401 turned the route suite 0/6 red; forcing `toHistoryEntry` to ignore the plan's zone turned exactly ONE case red ("every label renders in the PLAN's zone") and left the other 19 green. Both restored. A third, unplanned one happened by accident and is worth recording: `npm run check` ran while a temporary local override was in place and the new guest e2e test caught it — the test failed for precisely the right reason. **TWO REAL DEFECTS THE BROWSER FOUND THAT NOTHING ELSE DID:** (1) `.empty > *:not(.empty__glow):not(.empty__mark):not(.acct):not(.auth)` sets `position: relative` and outranks a plain `.hist` rule, so the overlay was silently demoted and covered a rectangle in the middle of the page instead of the viewport — `.auth` was already excluded there for the same reason, and `.hist` is now too, with a comment saying a new overlay MUST be added to that list; (2) the landing hero centres its text and the card inherited it, so records floated away from the timeline rail. Lint, typecheck, unit tests and e2e were all green while both were broken. VERIFIED live on a webpack production build at :3200: unauthenticated and bogus-token reads both return `{plans:[],readFailed:false}` at 200 while the server logs `caller_token_rejected` — so `verifyIdToken` genuinely RAN and was refused, rather than the route never getting that far; a REAL anonymous ID token (848 chars, taken from the app's own guest session) produced `history_read count:0 returned:0`, a line that only fires after the Firestore query itself succeeds, so the whole path — token → verified uid → `users/<uid>/history` query — is proven against real Firestore. **With the owner's explicit permission** two throwaway documents were then written to that collection and deleted afterwards (collection confirmed empty again): the route returned them newest-first with `ownerUid` stripped, and the screen rendered a Vancouver plan in VANCOUVER's clock (6:30 PM, not Toronto's 9:30), a stop with no end time as a single time rather than an invented range, a skipped slot with no venue as "Live Music · no time recorded · skipped", and a detail view with zero action buttons in its body. NOT VERIFIED BY EXECUTION, stated plainly: the list rendering above was seen with ONE temporary local line (`isGuest={false}`) because becoming a real signed-in account needs a Google popup an agent must not complete; that line was reverted, the build redone, and the guest nudge re-checked on the committed code. The route, the data and the rendering were all real — only "this browser is an account" was overridden. Gate: `npm run check` green — lint 0 errors (11 long-standing warnings), typecheck, 40 unit suites, webpack build, 77/77 mock e2e. The two known clock-dependent e2e failures did not reproduce this run.

## Feature: end an itinerary on purpose (the stop control)
Goal: until now a plan could only conclude by running out the clock — there was no way for a person to say "I'm done". Add an explicit stop control, with a confirmation whose options depend on whether there is anything to save.
What should be done: a "Stop/End itinerary" control on the planned stage; a confirmation offering save/discard/cancel to a real account and end/cancel to a guest; on ending, conclude the plan so a refresh does NOT bring it back; archive on "Save & end" only, through the EXISTING archiver rather than a second one.
What was done (technical): this is a SECOND route to conclusion, added alongside the end-time one rather than replacing it — `withStatuses` still derives "completed" from the clock and the by-id GET still archives when it first sees that. **The decision lives in one pure module** (`stopPlan.ts`: `resolveStopOutcome(isAnonymous, choice) → { shouldArchive, shouldEnd }`), and **the same function runs on both sides with the SERVER's answer being the one that counts**. The browser reports which button was pressed; whether that button may archive is decided from the VERIFIED caller's anonymity, so a guest client asking to save gets a no-archive answer regardless — the UI choice is validated input like any other. **Reuse, not a fork:** the Firestore write is the existing `archiveConcludedPlan`, so a history doc written by the button is identical in shape to one written by the clock; the plan is mutated through `updateItinerary`, so CAS versioning applies exactly as it does to a swap; and the owner pointer is cleared through the existing `clearActiveItineraryForOwner`. **The one genuinely new piece of state is `endedAt`, and it exists for a specific reason:** "concluded" could not be expressed as `status: "completed"`, because `withStatuses` RE-DERIVES status from the clock on every read and would have recomputed it straight back to "active" on the next GET — a plan ended at 6pm whose stops run to 10pm would have risen from the dead. `endedAt` persists the human decision, and `isResumable` now reads it. That gives TWO independent guards against resurrection (the cleared pointer and the marker on the plan itself), which is why the pointer clear is logged-and-swallowed rather than fatal. Ending is plan-then-commit like a failed swap: if the server does not end it, the user keeps the plan rather than losing it to a half-applied action. Escape cancels and focus lands on Cancel, because a stray Enter must never conclude someone's evening. TESTS: new `stopPlan.test.ts` (8 cases) covering the full truth table, the guest-asking-to-save case, cancel-is-not-a-soft-end, the two-vs-three option split, and rejection of unknown choices. **REVERT-RUNS, both discriminating:** forcing `shouldArchive: true` turned 4 red (discard, guest-end, guest-save, truth-table); dropping only the `&& !isAnonymous` half turned exactly 2 red (guest-save and the truth table) and left the discard case green — so the tests distinguish the anonymity gate specifically, not merely "archiving changed". Restored to 8/8. **The e2e suite caught a real regression I introduced:** `mobile.spec.ts` asserts every `.topbar button` has a 44px hit target, and I had styled the new control at 34px — being the less-used action buys a softer colour, not a smaller tap area. Fixed to 44px; 73/73 green. VERIFIED live on a webpack production build at :3200, as a guest: a guest asking to `save-end` is answered `archived: false` BY THE SERVER and the plan becomes unresumable; `cancel` returns `ended: false` and the plan is still there after a reload; an unknown choice is refused 400; the End button renders at exactly 44px; the dialog shows exactly two options with no save offered and uses no acid-green anywhere (checked against the computed `--live` value); pressing End returns to the landing screen, and **a full refresh afterwards stays on the landing screen rather than resurrecting the plan**. NOT VERIFIED BY EXECUTION: the signed-in three-option dialog and "Save & end" writing to Firestore, because completing a Google sign-in needs the owner's credentials, which an agent must not enter. The server-side half of that branch IS proven — the archive gate is the same `resolveStopOutcome` the unit tests pin, and the writer is the same one the end-time path already uses in production. Gate: `npm run check` green — lint 0 errors (11 long-standing warnings), typecheck, 38 unit suites, webpack build, 73/73 mock e2e.

## Fix: pin jose to the last CommonJS major — stop needing require(esm) at all
Goal: `/api/itinerary` still 500'd on Vercel with `require() of ES Module .../jose from .../jwks-rsa/src/utils.js`, even with `engines: ">=22.12.0"` deployed and the project's Node.js Version set to 24.x. The previous fix assumed the runtime would allow `require(esm)`; Vercel's FUNCTION runtime does not, whatever the build's Node version says. Remove the dependence on that feature entirely.
What should be done: fix it at the module level so a CommonJS `require()` of an ESM-only jose never happens, and prove the fix does not rely on `require(esm)`.
What was done (technical): **the failure was reproduced LOCALLY first**, which changes this from guesswork to a measured fix. `node --no-experimental-require-module` turns off Node's `require(esm)` support, and under it this machine produces Vercel's error verbatim — same message, same `jwks-rsa/src/utils.js` frame: `require() of ES Module …/jose/dist/webapi/index.js from …/jwks-rsa/src/utils.js not supported`. That flag is the local stand-in for Vercel's function runtime, and it is what every claim below is tested against. **Two of the three candidate fixes were dead on inspection:** `npm view` shows firebase-admin@14.2.0, jwks-rsa@4.1.0 and jose@6.2.7 are ALL already the latest published versions, so "upgrade jwks-rsa" and "upgrade firebase-admin" have nothing to upgrade to — there is no newer release that fixes this. **The chosen fix: `overrides: { "jose": "^5.10.0" }`.** The task suggested jose v4, but v4 is not the right target: v5.10.0 ALSO ships a `require` condition (`./dist/node/cjs/index.js`) — only v6 dropped CommonJS — so v5 is the newest version that is still require()-able, and it is one major from the v6 that jwks-rsa was written against instead of two. Less API drift for the same benefit. Compatibility was checked against actual usage rather than assumed: jwks-rsa touches exactly four jose APIs (`decodeJwt`, `decodeProtectedHeader` in `passport.js`; `importJWK`, `exportSPKI` in `utils.js`), and all four are present as functions in the v5 CJS build. **PROOF OF RUNTIME-INDEPENDENCE**, which is the whole point: with `--no-experimental-require-module` still set, jose now resolves through jwks-rsa to `node_modules/jwks-rsa/node_modules/jose/dist/node/cjs/index.js` — a CommonJS file — and both `require("jwks-rsa")` and `require("firebase-admin/auth")` load successfully where they threw `ERR_REQUIRE_ESM` before. The fix therefore works on a runtime with no `require(esm)` support at all, which is exactly the condition Vercel imposes. Note jose is NOT hoisted to the top level; it sits nested under jwks-rsa, which is correct and is how the override reaches only the chain that needs it. VERIFIED further against a webpack production build (`next start -p 3200`): `/api/itinerary` returns 200; a bogus bearer token logs `caller_token_rejected`, so `verifyIdToken` runs THROUGH the jose chain rather than crashing; and the full ownership round-trip still works — plan created (200), stamped with the server-verified uid, `ownerIsAnonymous: true`, and the resume lookup returned that exact plan. The webpack build change (`5f476b7`) is kept, and the `engines` field is kept as well: it is harmless, still accurately states what firebase-admin and jwks-rsa declare, and no longer has anything load-bearing resting on it. Nothing in auth logic, ownership, archive, persistence or the pipeline changed — the diff is one override plus the lockfile. **THE REAL PROOF REMAINS A FRESH VERCEL PREVIEW DEPLOY**, but unlike the last two rounds the failing condition was reproduced and then shown fixed on this machine, so this is no longer being verified only by proxy. Gate: `npm run check` green — lint 0 errors (11 long-standing warnings), typecheck, 37 unit suites, webpack build, 73/73 mock e2e.

## Fix: the Node version, not the dependency tree — require() of ESM jose
Goal: with firebase-admin now loading (the webpack fix held), `/api/itinerary` still 500'd on Vercel with `require() of ES Module .../jose/dist/webapi/index.js from .../jwks-rsa/src/utils.js not supported`.
What should be done: inspect the real dependency tree, establish WHY an ESM-only jose is resolved under a CommonJS jwks-rsa, and pick the fix the installed versions actually call for rather than guessing.
What was done (technical): **the dependency tree is not broken, and none of the three proposed dependency fixes were correct.** The chain is `firebase-admin@14.2.0 → jwks-rsa@4.1.0 → jose@6.2.7`. `jose@6.2.7` is `"type": "module"` and its `exports["."]` offers ONLY a `default` condition (`./dist/webapi/index.js`) — there is no `require` condition, so it is ESM-only. `jwks-rsa@4.1.0` is CommonJS and does `const jose = require('jose')` at `src/utils.js:1`, and it *asks for* `"jose": "^6.1.3"` — the ESM-only major, deliberately. **The decisive evidence is its engines field:** `jwks-rsa@4.1.0` declares `node: "^20.19.0 || ^22.12.0 || >= 23.0.0"`, and those are exactly the Node releases where `require()` of an ESM module is supported. The package is not mis-packaged; it requires a Node new enough to `require(esm)`. `firebase-admin@14.2.0` independently declares `node: ">=22"`. Our `package.json` declared NO `engines` at all, so Vercel provisioned its default Node — evidently older than 22.12 — while local Node v24.18.1 supports `require(esm)`. That is the whole of "works locally, fails on Vercel": a runtime version difference, not module resolution. Demonstrated directly rather than argued: jose's exports contain no `require` condition, yet `require("jose")` SUCCEEDS on Node v24.18.1 and returns working `importJWK`/`exportSPKI` — the local success depends entirely on `require(esm)`. **FIX: declare `engines.node: ">=22.12.0"`** so the deployment provisions a capable runtime. That bound is the exact intersection of what the dependencies themselves demand — firebase-admin needs `>=22`, jwks-rsa needs `^22.12.0` within the 22 line — so it is derived, not picked. **Why the candidates were rejected:** (1) pinning jose to `^4` via overrides directly contradicts jwks-rsa's declared `^6.1.3` and would force an untested major beneath a package written against v6's API, to work around a Node version we can simply select — fighting the tree to avoid naming a runtime; (2) there is nothing to upgrade, firebase-admin is already 14.2.0 and jwks-rsa 4.1.0, and the supposed "mismatch" is an engine requirement rather than a version conflict; (3) tracing config is irrelevant — the file resolves fine, and the failure is Node refusing a module SYSTEM, not a missing file. The webpack build change from the previous commit is kept and untouched. VERIFIED against a webpack production build (`next build --webpack` + `next start -p 3200`): `/api/itinerary` returns 200; a bogus bearer token logs `caller_token_rejected`, which only happens after `verifyIdToken` runs THROUGH the jose chain — so the ESM path executes rather than crashing; and the ownership round-trip still works end to end (plan created 200, stamped with the verified uid, `ownerIsAnonymous: true`, and the resume lookup returned that exact plan). **THE REAL PROOF IS A FRESH VERCEL PREVIEW DEPLOY**, and local testing structurally CANNOT reproduce this one: this machine runs Node 24, which supports `require(esm)`, so the failing condition does not exist here at all. **OWNER ACTION:** confirm the Vercel project's Node.js Version setting is 22.x or newer — a version pinned in Project Settings can take precedence over `engines`, in which case the setting must be raised too. Gate: `npm run check` green — lint 0 errors (11 long-standing warnings), typecheck, 37 unit suites, webpack build, 73/73 mock e2e.

## Fix: firebase-admin failed to load in the Vercel serverless build
Goal: every request to `/api/itinerary` returned 500 on the Vercel preview with "Failed to load external module firebase-admin-a14c8a5423a…", while every other route was fine and localhost worked. Make the Admin SDK load in the deployed build.
What should be done: find why the bundler fails to include/load `firebase-admin`, confirm the cause before changing anything, and apply the correct Next 16 mechanism.
What was done (technical): **THE PROPOSED FIX WAS MEASURED AND IS A NO-OP — the cause is different.** `firebase-admin` is ALREADY in Next 16.2.11's built-in external list (`node_modules/next/dist/lib/server-external-packages.jsonc`, 79 entries, `"firebase-admin"` among them), so adding it to `serverExternalPackages` changes nothing: added it, rebuilt, byte-identical output. Runtime was not the cause either — `firebase-admin` is imported in exactly ONE file (`app/lib/firebaseAdmin.ts`, three subpath imports) and no route declares `runtime = "edge"`; route handlers already default to Node. **ROOT CAUSE, found by inspecting build output.** A production build emits `.next/node_modules/firebase-admin-a14c8a5423a75469/` — a HASHED COPY of the package — and references it by that hashed name. The Vercel error names that exact hash.
