Turbopack (Next 16's default build engine) externalises a package by copying it into `.next/node_modules/<name>-<hash>` and requiring the hashed id; locally `next start` works because that directory is on disk, but Vercel's serverless function is assembled by output-file tracing and does not carry that Turbopack-generated directory, so the runtime require of `firebase-admin-a14c8a5423a…` fails. It is a TRACING/packaging problem, not an externals-config problem — which is exactly why the `serverExternalPackages` fix could not have worked. **THE FIX: build with webpack** (`next build --webpack`, a documented Next 16 flag). Proven by diffing build output: webpack produces NO `.next/node_modules` copy at all and instead emits `import("firebase-admin/app")` / `"…/auth"` / `"…/firestore"` against the real package name — the ordinary external reference Vercel's tracing has always resolved from `node_modules`. `next dev` is untouched and still uses Turbopack; only the production build changed. `serverExternalPackages: ["firebase-admin"]` was KEPT with a comment stating plainly that it is belt-and-braces, measured to be inert, and NOT the fix — a no-op left in without that note is exactly how a future reader concludes the wrong thing. This should be revisited when Turbopack's externalised packages are traced correctly by Vercel; the flag is a one-word revert. VERIFIED against a real production build locally (`next build` + `next start -p 3200`, since `next dev` masks bundling entirely), and the owner's `FIREBASE_ADMIN_*` credentials have landed in `.env` since Stage 1B, so this went further than expected: `GET /api/itinerary` returns **200, not 500**; a bogus bearer token logs `caller_token_rejected`, which only happens after `isAdminConfigured()` passes and `verifyIdToken` actually runs — i.e. the SDK genuinely initialised with real credentials rather than merely failing to crash; and a full ownership round-trip through the built server succeeded — POST created a plan (200), it came back stamped `ownerUid` = the verified anonymous uid with `ownerIsAnonymous: true`, and `GET /api/itinerary` returned that exact plan, so ownership stamping AND resume both work in the shipped build. Nothing was weakened: the degrade-safely-without-credentials behaviour, the auth logic, the ownership/archive logic, the persistence seam and the pipeline are all untouched — the diff is one npm script and one config line. **THE REAL PROOF IS A FRESH VERCEL PREVIEW DEPLOY**, which only the owner can run; local `next start` resolves externals from a full `node_modules` tree and therefore cannot reproduce Vercel's tracing step, which is the thing that broke. Gate: `npm run check` green — lint 0 errors (11 long-standing warnings), typecheck, 37 unit suites, webpack build, 73/73 mock e2e.

## Fix: a recovered sign-in step is not a failure
Goal: a SUCCESSFUL Google sign-in was printing `[auth] google-sign-in failed — code=auth/credential-already-in-use` and tripping Next's dev error overlay, because the previous change logged the intermediate link conflict before knowing whether it recovered.
What should be done: treat `auth/credential-already-in-use` during the `linkWithPopup` attempt as the expected control-flow signal it is ("returning account — switch to credential exchange"), not a failure. Log only genuine failures. Narrow ONLY this case; leave every other logging path exactly as it was, and change no sign-in logic.
What was done (technical): the catch block was reordered so the log call follows the outcome instead of preceding it. It previously ran `logAuthFailure()` unconditionally as the FIRST statement, which is why a conflict that went on to succeed still announced itself as a failure. Now: silent codes log and return exactly as before; the link-conflict branch logs NOTHING on entry and only if it actually dead-ends — either the credential exchange threw (`google-sign-in:credential-exchange`) or the error carried no credential to exchange (`google-sign-in:no-credential-on-conflict`); and every other code falls through to the same `logAuthFailure` + generic message it had before. A sign-in that ultimately succeeds now leaves no line behind at all. **Why this was worth fixing beyond the noise:** the whole point of the previous change was that one line identifying a real fault. A line that also fires on the routine path is worse than no line — it cannot be trusted, and the next real failure is indistinguishable from a returning user signing in normally. VERIFIED live on :3200 (`next dev`, so the dev overlay itself was in play): the anonymous guest session is unaffected and persists across reloads with the same uid; a GENUINE failure still logs its real code and now shows its actionable message — `[auth] google-sign-in failed — code=auth/popup-blocked` with "Your browser blocked the sign-in window. Allow pop-ups for this site and try again. (auth/popup-blocked)" on screen. NOT VERIFIED BY EXECUTION, and stated plainly: the recovered conflict path cannot be triggered without completing a real Google sign-in, which needs the owner's credentials. It is verified by control flow instead — between entering the conflict branch and the `return` that follows a successful `signInWithCredential` there is no `logAuthFailure` call at all; the only two on that branch sit behind an exchange failure and a missing credential. The owner's own sign-in is the execution proof. OBSERVED, NOT CHANGED (out of the stated scope): the silent codes — `auth/popup-closed-by-user` and friends — are still logged via `console.error`, so closing the popup deliberately still prints a failure line and can still trip the dev overlay. That is arguably the same mislabeling one step removed, but the instruction was to narrow only the credential case, so it was left alone; flagging it as the obvious next candidate. Scope: `app/lib/useAuth.ts` only, no logic change. Gate: `npm run check` green end to end — lint 0 errors (11 long-standing warnings), typecheck, 37 unit suites, build, **73/73 mock e2e** (runnable again now that :3000 is free; it was blocked by Next 16's single-dev-server guard last round).

## Fix: Google sign-in — the second popup, and the error code that hid it
Goal: Google sign-in failed silently with a generic "Could not sign in with Google" while anonymous sign-in worked. Find the real cause and fix it.
What should be done: stop swallowing `error.code` so the failure is diagnosable at all; then fix the gesture gap in the anonymous-upgrade path, keeping the popup approach (no redirect switch) and preserving the design intent — a guest links to keep their plan, and `credential-already-in-use` means an existing older account whose plan should NOT carry over.
What was done (technical): **COOP was RULED OUT first, with evidence, and it was not the cause.** The reported console line ("Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy policy would block the window.close call") pointed at a header this app never sends: `next.config.mjs` has no `headers()` at all, there is no middleware, and a repo-wide search for `Cross-Origin` finds nothing outside prose. Measured on the wire, `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy` is ABSENT from both a production `next start` and the running dev server — Next 16 sets no COOP default here. Relaxing COOP to `same-origin-allow-popups` would have been a no-op that looked like a fix; you cannot relax a header you never send. That warning is emitted when the POPUP's own COOP (Google's `accounts.google.com`) severs the opener relationship, and it appears on successful Firebase sign-ins too — a symptom-shaped red herring. **ROOT CAUSE: a second popup opened one tick too late.** Stage 1B made every visitor anonymous, so clicking "Sign in" now takes the `linkWithPopup` branch. When that Google account already existed, it threw `auth/credential-already-in-use`, and the fallback called `signInWithPopup` — AFTER an await, outside the user activation the click granted. Browsers block that as `auth/popup-blocked`, which was not in `SILENT_CODES`, so it landed on the generic string. The bug was introduced by the anonymous-upgrade path added in Stage 1B. **THE FIX REMOVES THE SECOND POPUP RATHER THAN RESCHEDULING IT.** The failed link already carries the Google credential the user approved in the FIRST popup, so `GoogleAuthProvider.credentialFromError()` + `signInWithCredential()` exchanges it for a session with no window and therefore no gesture requirement at all. That is fewer moving parts than the bug it replaces, and it cannot regress into a blocked popup because there is no second popup. Intent preserved exactly: link first (guest keeps their uid and their in-progress plan), and on conflict sign in as the existing older account without carrying the guest's plan over. **ERROR SURFACING**, the reason this was undiagnosable: `logAuthFailure()` now logs `code` + `message` for EVERY auth failure — Google sign-in, the credential exchange, sign-out, and the silent anonymous sign-in (silent to the user, never to the console: if that fails there is no identity, so no plan survives a refresh). The user-facing sentence stays friendly; the raw code is appended only behind the existing `shouldShowDevControls` gate, the same one the dev time-sim uses. `auth/popup-blocked` also got its own sentence — telling someone to "try again" when their browser is blocking the window is useless advice, and this is the one auth failure a user can actually fix themselves. VERIFIED on :3200 (production build — see the deviation below): the page renders, the anonymous guest session persists unchanged, and clicking through to Google now prints `[auth] google-sign-in failed — code=auth/popup-blocked` where the code was previously invisible. **HONEST LIMIT ON THAT EVIDENCE:** that `popup-blocked` came from an AUTOMATED click in an embedded browser that blocks popups regardless of user activation, so it proves the surfacing works and reproduces the failure MECHANISM — it is not proof of the specific instance seen in a real browser, and completing a real Google sign-in needs the owner's credentials, which an agent must not enter. The credential-exchange fallback likewise cannot be triggered without a real linked account. Both paths are the owner's manual check; whatever happens, the code is now printed. DEVIATIONS: this is a header-config-free fix — only `app/lib/useAuth.ts` changed, no auth-flow restructuring, no redirect. Verification ran against `next start -p 3200` rather than `next dev`, because Next 16 refuses a second dev server in the same directory while the owner's manual server holds :3000; production mode also matches what Vercel serves. For the same reason **`npm run test:e2e` could not run at all**: the harness spawns `next dev -p 3100` and hits the identical guard ("Another next dev server is already running. PID 11268"). That is environmental, not a regression — the same suite passed 73/73 earlier in this session when :3000 was down, no e2e spec touches Firebase (mock mode has no auth), and this change is confined to one client-side auth module. Gate: lint 0 errors (11 long-standing warnings), typecheck clean, 37 unit suites, build passes; e2e blocked as described — re-run it with :3000 stopped.

## Feature: Stage 1B — identity, plans that survive a refresh, history on conclude
Goal: give every visitor an identity, make the active plan survive refresh and tab-close for EVERYONE, and archive concluded plans to Firestore for signed-in accounts only. Guests keep the whole product; the only thing an account buys in this slice is history.
What should be done: sign guests in anonymously and silently so there is ONE id path instead of two branches; verify the caller server-side with the Firebase Admin SDK rather than trusting a uid from the browser; stamp plans with the verified owner through the existing store seam; look the owner's active plan up on load and rehydrate it; and on conclusion write a history copy to Firestore only when the owner is a real account. No pipeline or planning-logic changes.
What was done (technical): **The refresh bug was diagnosed before it was fixed, and the diagnosis changed the shape of the fix.** The plan was never lost — it sits in Redis for seven days. `page.tsx` held `itinerary` in React state and nothing else (line 289, no URL id, no storage), so a reload forgot WHICH id it was showing and fell through to the landing page. So this is a lookup, not more persistence: the store now keeps `owner:<uid>:active` → plan id beside the plan, and a new `GET /api/itinerary` returns the caller's resumable plan. Rehydration reuses the existing `applyItinerary()` — every field the stage renders (`homeOrigin`, `displayZone`, map stops, home leg) already derives from the itinerary itself, so nothing needed re-deriving. **IDENTITY.** `useAuth` now signs in anonymously when `onAuthStateChanged` reports nobody, silently and with no UI change; `AppUser` gained `isAnonymous`, which defaults to TRUE unless the provider says an explicit `false` — the safe failure is losing a history entry, never filing a stranger's outing under a real person's uid. Sign-in from a guest session uses `linkWithPopup`, which UPGRADES the anonymous user and keeps the same uid, so a guest who signs in mid-plan keeps the plan they are in; `auth/credential-already-in-use` (that Google account already exists) falls back to a plain `signInWithPopup`, where not carrying the plan over is correct because it is a different, older person. Two UI spots had to stop equating "signed in" with "has an account", or a guest would have seen an account chip and the login card would have dismissed itself the instant it opened: both now key off `isAnonymous === false`. **SERVER-SIDE VERIFICATION.** New `app/lib/firebaseAdmin.ts` (server-only) and `app/api/_shared/caller.ts`. Identity comes from a verified ID token in the Authorization header and NEVER from a request body — a uid in a body is a string the browser chose. `verifyCaller` returns null for every failure (no token, bad token, missing credentials) because every caller responds identically: proceed with no identity. It never falls back to trusting an unverified uid, which is what would turn a missing env var into an impersonation hole. **ENV VARS THE OWNER MUST ADD** (server-only, NOT `NEXT_PUBLIC_`, to `.env` and Vercel): `FIREBASE_ADMIN_PROJECT_ID`, `FIREBASE_ADMIN_CLIENT_EMAIL`, `FIREBASE_ADMIN_PRIVATE_KEY`. The private key is handled for both storage forms — a real PEM and the escaped `\n` form dashboards force. **OWNERSHIP + ARCHIVE.** `Itinerary` gained three optional fields — `ownerUid`, `ownerIsAnonymous`, `archivedAt` — all written through the existing save/CAS path. `ownerIsAnonymous` is recorded at CREATION from the verified token because the archive decision happens long after the request that knew the answer. **Where the archive hook attaches was forced by a finding: there is no "stop itinerary" action in the product and no conclusion event.** `withStatuses` DERIVES "completed" from the clock on every read, so the hook lives on the by-id GET that first observes it, and `archivedAt` is the once-only guard — without it every poll after conclusion would re-archive. The Firestore write (`users/<uid>/history/<planId>`) is keyed by plan id so even a bypassed guard would overwrite rather than duplicate, and it NEVER throws: it is a secondary write on a read path, and failing the user's own plan to protect a feature nobody can see yet would be the wrong trade. Clearing the owner pointer on conclusion applies to EVERY concluded plan, guest included, so a finished outing stops resuming over the landing page whether or not it was archived. TESTS: new `ownership.test.ts` (12 cases) covers stamping, legacy plans, ownership matching, resumability and the archive gate; four owner-index round-trip cases were added to `store.cas.test.ts`; `authUser.test.ts` gained an anonymity case and had its exact-shape assertions updated. **REVERT-RUN:** deleting the `ownerIsAnonymous !== false` line in `shouldArchive` (so anonymous owners archive) turned exactly ONE test red — "an ANONYMOUS owner NEVER archives" — 11/12, and nothing else moved; restored to 12/12. Firebase token verification and the Firestore write are live-only and are NOT unit-tested: per this project's own lesson a mocked auth test proves the mock was called, not that verification works. VERIFIED LIVE on :3200: the page renders with Admin credentials absent, anonymous sign-in lands silently (uid captured, `isAnonymous: true`, no UI change), and — the security case — a REAL 848-character ID token sent while `FIREBASE_ADMIN_*` are unset is treated as unauthenticated rather than trusted, with one `admin_credentials_missing` log line naming the missing vars. NOT YET VERIFIED, blocked on the owner's env vars: owned plans, resume-after-refresh end to end, and the Firestore history write — all three require Admin credentials that are not in `.env` yet. Gate: lint 0 errors (11 long-standing warnings), typecheck clean, 37 unit suites, build passes, 73/73 mock e2e (the clock-dependent failures did not reproduce this run; this change added none).

## Docs: reconcile CLAUDE.md (and AGENTS.md) with the code as it actually is
Goal: CLAUDE.md is the first file every agent reads, so a stale claim in it misleads every future session. Recent sessions kept tripping over the same drift — the visual language especially — so bring both handbooks back in line with what the code does NOW.
What should be done: read the current repo rather than the docs' own claims, verify every section against code, correct only what is demonstrably wrong, flag genuine uncertainty instead of guessing, and preserve structure and voice — a reconciliation, not a rewrite. Docs only, no behaviour changes.
What was done (technical): every correction below was made against a specific piece of code, and the checks that PASSED are recorded too, because "verified correct" is as useful to the next reader as "fixed". **CLAUDE.md corrections.** (1) *The product-UI section described the pre-re-skin look.* `globals.css` says outright that the palette was replaced and the token NAMES deliberately kept — which is exactly how the description stayed wrong: `--paper` is now `#EAF3F8` (hazy pale blue), `--ink` `#1E3A4C`, `--accent` `#2A7890`, plus a hero sky gradient. The map style in `ItineraryMap.tsx` is blue too (`#dfeaf1` geometry, `#bcdcea` water, `#eaf3f8` label stroke), and its route-line constant is `INK = "#2E6F8A"` — a mid-teal, NOT the navy `--ink` token, despite the name; the style constant is still called `PAPER_STYLE`. All of that is now spelled out, with an explicit warning not to read a token name as a colour. Fonts were checked and are unchanged (locally bundled Fraunces + Space Grotesk, `layout.tsx`), and `--live` `#C8F000` survived the re-skin untouched and still reserved. (2) *"the four-mode recovery panel"* — `page.tsx` has exactly THREE (`geocode`, `empty`, `weather-gate`); `time-gate` went with the plausibility gate and does not exist in the file. The doc contradicted ITSELF here, since its own recovery section already said three. (3) *"Only the Maps JS key is browser-side"* — `.env` now carries seven `NEXT_PUBLIC_` values: the Maps key plus six `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*`. Rewritten to name both and to say why the Firebase ones are client config rather than secrets. (4) *The `usableByHours` timezone exception is RESOLVED* — it now judges the instant on the venue's local clock through `isOpenAtInstant`, takes a `timeZone`, and both call sites (`swap.ts:705`, `:1577`) pass the plan's zone; a repo-wide search finds no production `getDay()/getHours()` left, only the comment in `swap.ts` describing the old behaviour and `MOCK_RAIN_HOUR` in the fixtures. FLAGGED rather than resolved: DEPLOY.md still requires `TZ=America/Toronto` and this was its stated justification, so the doc now says to re-evaluate that requirement on its own evidence — this pass did not audit DEPLOY.md. (5) *"`planner.test.ts` is the heaviest-covered file in the app"* — false by both measures: 35 cases and 913 lines, against `swap.test.ts` at 49/1336 and `schedule.test.ts` at 43/956. Softened to "heavily covered (35 cases), keep it that way", which preserves the intent without the false superlative. (6) *Dev/testing listed no commands or counts at all* — added `npm run check` and its five stages, the verified counts (36 unit suites, 73 mock e2e across 10 spec files, lint 0 errors + 11 long-standing warnings), an instruction to update them, and a note that missing Playwright browsers make every e2e fail in ~1ms with no output (`npx playwright install chromium`), which cost a previous session real time. (7) *"nothing from [the 2026-07-18 audit] has been fixed yet"* — false: §1.1 is fixed and `swap.ts` cites it at the fix site. Reframed as a list to CHECK against current code. Also noted that the remediation tracker's Baseline block (Next 14.2.35, 251 tests/18 suites, 41 e2e) is a 2026-07-24 snapshot, not current. (8) *The weather-gate "open gap" is CLOSED* — both engines declare `getWeather` wired to `fetchWeatherHours` (`reroute.ts:114`, `swap.ts:587`), reroute fetches at `:302`, and every `filterPools` call in both files passes a real weather value; no `weather: null` literal remains in either. Struck through rather than deleted, so the history stays legible. (9) The Open-gaps header claimed everything was re-verified on 2026-07-18; it now says which TWO items this pass actually re-checked and warns that the rest still carry the old date. (10) New section for the shipped optional Google login, and the acid-green invariant now says explicitly that auth and other chrome must not borrow `--live`. **VERIFIED CORRECT, left alone:** the pipeline order (traced in `page.tsx` — geocode `:470`/`:506` → parse `:553` → weather `:649` → search `:664` → select `:701`, matching the doc exactly), the mock seam's "eight points" (6 routes + both engines' `realDeps`), `selectModelCall`'s existence and role, the three recovery modes as described in the recovery section, the movie-runtime placeholder (`durations.ts:19-21`, still 120+30 with its TODO), and the ≤560px landing-field stacking. **AGENTS.md** got the same treatment plus a canonical-source banner, because it is a near-duplicate that had drifted further: its pipeline was still the pre-planner `parse → weather → …` with no geocode-first and no planner; it claimed the geocode "reuses the Places key; no separate Geocoding API" when a dedicated `GOOGLE_GEOCODING_API_KEY` exists; it documented a `time-gate` recovery mode that no longer exists while omitting `geocode` entirely; and its gate numbers read "192 unit tests across 15 suites, 34/34 @mock e2e". The banner says CLAUDE.md wins on disagreement, and says plainly that two hand-maintained copies will drift again — the root cause of this whole entry. NO CODE CHANGED: docs only, no behaviour change, and the gate was re-run to prove the repo is untouched.

## Feature: optional Google login (Stage 1A — login only)
Goal: add Google sign-in and a signed-in/signed-out state, and nothing else. Login is OPTIONAL and guest is first-class: in this slice a signed-in user and a guest get IDENTICAL app behaviour, and signing in only CAPTURES a uid for later stages to use.
What should be done: install the Firebase Web SDK (client only), add one guarded init module that cannot crash the app when its env vars are missing, expose auth state via `onAuthStateChanged`, add Google popup sign-in/sign-out, and put a Discord-STRUCTURED but Itinerary-THEMED login card behind an unobtrusive hero entry point — with no API, pipeline or gating changes anywhere.
What was done (technical): additive throughout — **nothing under `app/api/**` was touched, no route, engine, planner, scheduler or pipeline file changed**, and no existing behaviour is gated on auth. New: `app/lib/firebase.ts`, `app/lib/authUser.ts` (+ test), `app/lib/useAuth.ts`, `app/LoginScreen.tsx`. Modified: `app/page.tsx` (one hook call, one `loginOpen` flag, one hero corner block, one overlay mount), `app/globals.css` (a new `.acct`/`.auth` section plus one edit to an existing rule, below), `package.json`/lock for `firebase@^12.17.0`. **Resilience is the load-bearing part.** `firebase.ts` does NO work at import time and every entry point returns null rather than throwing: `readConfig()` requires all six `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*` values and treats blank/whitespace as absent, because a partial config otherwise fails deep inside the SDK at popup time with an opaque message. It is all six or none, so the UI can say "sign-in unavailable" honestly. The six reads are spelled out literally rather than looped, because Next inlines `NEXT_PUBLIC_` vars at BUILD time only for complete static expressions — `process.env[key]` is undefined in the browser. The SSR bail-out deliberately happens BEFORE the resolved-cache is set, since caching a server-side null would poison the client where the answer differs. `useAuth` starts at `loading` only when Firebase is configured, and every failure path (unconfigured, SDK threw, listener error) lands on `signed-out`, which is a fully working state — the UI can never strand on a spinner waiting for a session that is not coming. `signInWithPopup` failures that are really the user closing the popup (`auth/popup-closed-by-user`, `cancelled-popup-request`, `user-cancelled`) are silent, not errors. Sign-in never calls setState from the popup's return value; `onAuthStateChanged` is the single source of truth, so the login card and the account corner cannot disagree about who is signed in. **DEVIATION, deliberate, with evidence.** The task asked for the login screen on arrival when signed out. That cannot coexist with the requirement that the existing 73 e2e specs pass UNCHANGED: every spec does `page.goto("/")` then immediately fills `.prompt__input`, so an arrival interstitial breaks all 73. The alternatives were worse — gating the interstitial on a test-only flag would make the product behave differently under test, which the mock-seam rule explicitly forbids ("never let mock mode fork logic, only data"). So the login screen is built exactly as specified (centred card, brand area, primary "Continue with Google", "Continue as guest" below a divider) but is REACHED from a "Sign in" affordance in the hero corner rather than forced in front of every arrival — the task's own "show the login screen / a way to reach it" wording permits this, and it makes guest genuinely zero-friction rather than a dismissal. Escape and "Continue as guest" both dismiss; dismissing without signing in IS the guest path, so there is one `onDismiss`, not two concepts. The card auto-closes off auth STATE, not the popup result. Signed in, the corner shows avatar (or initials) + name + Sign out; while auth is still resolving it renders nothing, rather than a "Sign in" that would flip to a name a moment later. **One existing CSS rule was edited**: `.empty > *:not(.empty__glow):not(.empty__mark)` gained `:not(.acct):not(.auth)` — that rule forces `position: relative` on hero children and would otherwise beat the absolutely-positioned corner and the fixed overlay on specificity. It is the same exclusion `.empty__mark` already had, for the same reason. Acid green is untouched and unused here: a computed-style scan of the whole auth subtree in a live browser returned ZERO uses of `--live`/#C8F000 (card white, title ink-navy `rgb(30,58,76)`). One lint fix worth recording: React 19's `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rejected the synchronous `setStatus("signed-out")` in the effect body; it now defers to `requestAnimationFrame`, matching the pattern `ItineraryMap` already uses for its projection probe. TESTS: no tests for the live popup flow, as agreed — it is the SDK plus a real Google popup, and this project has been burned by a mock that coincidentally matched buggy real behaviour. The one piece with real logic IS tested: `authUser.test.ts` (7 cases) covers the pure mapper and its helpers — uid required (null/undefined/blank/whitespace/non-string all reject, because a record without a uid is not an account), blank decoration normalised to null (Google really does send `""` for a missing photo, which would render a broken image), whitespace trimmed, non-strings dropped rather than coerced, and the label/initials fallbacks including the email case ("AG" for anshiwang@gmail.com would read as a surname that does not exist). Unit suites 35 → 36 by exactly that file. **VERIFIED LIVE on :3200** (port 3000 untouched): with Firebase env vars PRESENT the hero renders and the Google button is enabled with no unavailable note; with them ABSENT the hero still renders, the search pill is present and typeable, the plan button enables on valid input, the Google button is disabled with "Sign-in is unavailable right now. Everything below still works.", guest dismisses cleanly, and the console has ZERO errors. Recording a false start honestly: the first attempt at the absent-vars run set them to `""` in PowerShell, which on Windows REMOVES the variable, so Next loaded `.env` normally and the check silently proved nothing — caught because the disabled button and the note were missing. Redone with a temporary `.env.local` (which takes precedence over `.env` in Next), then deleted; `.env` was never modified. Gate: full `npm run check` green end to end — lint 0 errors (11 pre-existing warnings), typecheck clean, 36/36 unit suites, production build, 73/73 mock e2e unchanged. Note for the environment, not the code: Playwright's browsers were missing on this machine (every e2e failed at launch in ~1ms); `npx playwright install chromium` fixed it, which is why e2e is verifiable again this round.

## Tests: pin the fallback chain's exact walk (extending modelFallback.test.ts)
Goal: prove `withModelFallback()` deterministically and offline — that the chain falls through on a rate limit, refuses to fall through on anything else, and reports exhaustion accurately.
What should be done: the task called for a NEW `app/api/_shared/modelFallback.test.ts` with seven required cases, on the premise that the fallback layer ships untested. **That premise was stale and is corrected here:** the file already existed with 14 passing cases, added by the model-layering commit itself (it is the 35th suite, not a 36th). Writing a "new" file would have overwritten working coverage, so the existing suite was audited against the seven required cases and EXTENDED with the genuine gaps instead.
What was done (technical): audited first. Already covered: 429 advancing, 401/403/400/404/422 failing immediately, provider 5xx advancing (500/502/503), non-ProviderError propagating untouched, exhaustion carrying the specific message + `Retry-After`, the last-seen retry-after surviving, and the env override driving the real chain. Four genuine gaps found, all now closed: (1) **`AllModelsRateLimitedError.call` was never asserted anywhere** — the field that tells an operator WHICH budget ran out, since a planner exhaustion and a swap exhaustion need different responses; (2) the first-model-success path asserted its LOG but never that exactly one call was made, on the chain's head, returning that model's own answer; (3) `.retryAfterSeconds === undefined` was only checked indirectly through the absent `Retry-After` header, never on the error itself; (4) the non-retryable cases asserted `instanceof` + code but not object IDENTITY, so a re-wrapped lookalike would have passed. A fifth, structural gap: nearly every existing case drove the LIVE default chain, which is a real check of the shipped config but a weak check of the walk — it can assert "two models were tried", never "these two, in this order". The five new cases pin a synthetic chain (`alpha,beta,gamma` / `one,two`) through the existing `withEnv2` helper and make the sequence itself the assertion, which also keeps them from going red the day someone legitimately reorders a chain. Conventions matched exactly — `node:assert`, the `cases` array of `[name, fn]`, the existing `rejected()` / `capturingLogs()` / `withEnv2()` helpers, no new framework. Suite goes 14 → 19 cases; suite COUNT stays 35. **REVERT-RUNS — all three discriminated, none stayed green:** breaking `modelFallback.ts:75` to `const retryable = true` (advance on ALL errors) turned 4 red — the 401 case, the 403/400/404/422 case, the non-provider-error case, and the new same-error-identity case (15/19). Breaking the same line to `const retryable = false` (advance on nothing) turned 10 red — every advance and exhaustion case including the new ordered-walk, call-type and undefined-retry-after cases (9/19). Breaking `modelFallback.ts:93` to pass `undefined` instead of `lastRetryAfter` turned 3 red — the exhaustion-message case (expects 30s), the last-seen-retry-after case (expects 45), and the new call-type case (expects 7) — 16/19; the new "no retry-after carries undefined" case correctly stayed green, since that break forces the very value it asserts. `modelFallback.ts` was restored from git after each run and verified byte-identical; no product file changed. GATE: `npm run lint` 0 errors (11 pre-existing warnings), `npm run typecheck` clean, `npm run build` compiles, **35/35 unit suites (exit 0)**. `npm run check` does NOT complete, for an environmental reason unrelated to this change: **Playwright's browsers are not installed on this machine** (`C:\Users\anshi\AppData\Local\ms-playwright` is empty; Playwright is at its pinned 1.61.1 and expects `chromium-1228`), so all 73 mock e2e tests fail at browser launch in ~1ms with no assertion ever running. Pre-existing and independent — a unit-test file cannot affect a browser launch. Fix is `npx playwright install chromium`; not run here because it is a large download onto the developer's machine rather than a repo change.

## Diagnostic: Groq daily-capacity probe for the planner fallback chain
Goal: measure empirically how many planner calls and tokens the layered fallback chain pushes through Groq's free tier in one day before the WHOLE chain is daily-exhausted, and how much more that is than the primary model alone. Groq's free-tier limits are PER MODEL, so the chain's ceiling should be roughly the SUM of its models' daily budgets — that sum is the number worth knowing, and nothing in `app/` reads `usage` or the `x-ratelimit-*` headers, so today it is unmeasured.
What should be done: a standalone `tsx` script that drives the REAL production call (`withModelFallback("planner", model => planWithModel(..., msgs => callGroq(...)))`) in a loop over realistic prompts, capturing per-model token usage and rate-limit headers on the way through; back off through minute windows, stop at the daily wall, and report per-model and chain totals with an explicit label saying whether the number is a measured ceiling or only a floor. Zero product-behavior change: import from `app/`, edit nothing there.
What was done (technical): `scripts/groq-capacity-probe.ts` (new, additive) plus an `npm run probe:groq` script and a gitignore line for `scripts/probe-output/`. Nothing under `app/` changed — the probe imports `modelChain`, `withModelFallback`, `AllModelsRateLimitedError`, `fetchProvider`/`readProviderJson`/`requireProviderRecord`/`ProviderError`, and `buildPlannerMessages`/`planWithModel`, and reproduces `parse/route.ts`'s `callGroq` byte-for-byte (same URL, body, `response_format`, `temperature: 0`, `cache: "no-store"`, same 50K content guard). **The instrumentation is non-invasive by construction:** on a rejection it reads the body off `res.clone()` and hands the UNTOUCHED original to `readProviderJson`, so the ProviderError that reaches `withModelFallback` — status, code, retry-after — is exactly production's, and the chain advances exactly as it does live. **SCHEMAS WERE READ OFF THE WIRE, NOT ASSUMED** (one live call, before the accounting layer existed), and that call corrected a premise of the task. Groq returned `x-ratelimit-limit-requests: 1000 / reset-requests: 1m26.4s` and `x-ratelimit-limit-tokens: 12000 / reset-tokens: 9.24s`. Those resets decode the windows exactly: one request spent needs 86.4s to refill, and 86400/1000 = 86.4, so the REQUEST budget is DAILY; 1848 tokens reserved need 9.24s, i.e. 200 tokens/sec = 12000/MINUTE, so the TOKEN header is per-minute. **So the headers expose RPD and TPM, and there is NO header for tokens-per-day — the limit that actually binds (~100K on llama-3.3-70b, ~200K on gpt-oss-120b).** The task's proposed evidence for a daily wall ("remaining-tokens = 0 with a day-scale reset") is therefore unobtainable for TPD, and inferring one from a large `retry-after` is a guess a long burst penalty would fool. The probe instead classifies each 429 by reading the dimension out of Groq's own message (`classifyLimit`: "on tokens per day (TPD): Limit 100000, Used ..."), which is direct evidence; `resolveWait` prefers the reset headers as the task asked, with one deliberate exception — when the body names a PER-DAY limit the reset headers describe the per-minute buckets and are actively misleading (a TPD wall can sit behind a 9-second `reset-tokens`), so there retry-after is the only honest number. Also observed: `usage` carries no `prompt_tokens_details` on a plain response, so cached-token capture is best-effort (absent = zero, not an error), and the first raw `usage` block and header set are stored verbatim in the report so a reader can re-derive the windows instead of trusting our decoding. Accounting is per model (`plans_served`, `groq_calls`, `rate_limited_calls`, prompt/completion/total/cached tokens, `first_dayscale_429_at_iter`, `dimensions_seen`) with `source` tracked per plan for the correction rate; a plan is 1–2 Groq calls and usage sums across them. Backoff: on `AllModelsRateLimitedError` the round's 429s are inspected — any non-day-scale model whose wait is ≤ `MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS` (default 120, env-configurable) sleeps `max(shortWait) + 2s` and retries the same prompt, and the log names which signal drove it; all-day-scale stops. Guards: `MAX_CALLS` (100000), `MAX_WALL_MINUTES` (240), 401/403 aborts immediately with a "check GROQ_API_KEY" message, and five consecutive non-retryable failures abort. SIGINT finishes the current plan and still writes a report. Output goes to stdout and a timestamped `.json`/`.txt` pair (ISO colons flattened — they are illegal in Windows filenames), with progress every 25 calls and a headline whose wording tracks the stop rule, because "before its daily wall" is a claim about a measured ceiling and a run that timed out has not earned it. The `RESULT:` line is the point of the whole report: TRUE-CEILING only when every model in the chain returned a 429 naming a per-day limit, otherwise LOWER-BOUND with the reason. NO TESTS, deliberately: this harness makes live Groq calls, and by this project's own lesson (the mock fixture that coincidentally matched buggy real behaviour) a mocked test of a capacity probe proves nothing about real capacity — the live run IS the verification, and there is no product logic here beyond accounting read directly from that run. Verified without spending budget: an invalid-key run exercised env loading, chain resolution, the real fallback loop, the abort path and the report writer end to end, and made exactly ONE call rather than three — the 401 correctly did not advance the chain, which is `isModelRetryable` behaving as designed. ASSUMPTION, flagged: this measures the PLANNER only. Planner and select share the chain and the same per-model budget, and the planner prompt is the dominant cost (~1847 prompt tokens/call observed), so full-prompt capacity is derived as ~plans/2 rather than measured. A genuine end-to-end probe would need a real select candidate list; synthesizing a fake one would measure a fiction, so it was NOT built — owner's call whether to build it properly. Gate: `npm run lint` 0 errors (11 pre-existing warnings, none from the new file), `npm run typecheck` clean, `npm run build` passes. The probe was NOT run to exhaustion — that run is the owner's, since it spends the day's free tier.

## Model layering — survive a rate limit instead of failing
Goal: a Groq rate limit should degrade the plan, not fail the request. Groq limits PER MODEL, so asking a different model genuinely gets you unblocked — unlike a retry, which hits the same wall by definition.
What should be done: surface the upstream status so a 429 is distinguishable from a 401; put every model id in one config with an ordered chain per call type; advance the chain on rate limits only; live-verify every fallback model, because the prompts were tuned against Llama 3.3 70B and mock fixtures cannot detect a weaker model following the schema less reliably.
What was done (technical): **Part 1 — the status stops being discarded.** `readProviderJson` checked `response.ok` and threw the same generic `${provider}_rejected_request` 502 for everything, so a 429, a 401 and a 403 were indistinguishable downstream — nothing could react to a rate limit because nothing knew it was one. `ProviderError` now carries a `ProviderFailure { upstreamStatus, retryAfterSeconds? }`, 429 gets its own `${provider}_rate_limited` code, and `parseRetryAfter()` handles both header forms (seconds incl. Groq's fractional values, and HTTP-date), treating absent/malformed/past as "no wait" rather than inventing one. The public message and status are unchanged — this is about what the SERVER knows. `isModelRetryable` is the whole judgment in one place: **only 429 and provider-side 5xx**. A 401/403 is the same key failing identically on every model, so advancing would burn the chain and bury the real problem under matching failures; a 400 is our own malformed request and the next model will reject it too. **Part 2 — one config.** `app/api/_shared/models.ts` owns every model decision, replacing three independent `const MODEL = "llama-3.3-70b-versatile"` declarations in `parse/route.ts`, `selectVenues.ts` and `swap.ts`. Chains are per call type and env-overridable (`GROQ_MODELS_PLANNER` / `_SELECT` / `_SWAP`), defaults in code. **The per-call-type split is NOT cosmetic and the live checks proved it:** limits are per model, so giving swap a different primary means swap traffic never spends the planner's budget — under the load that actually causes this problem (someone re-planning and swapping repeatedly) a single shared chain would have swaps pushing the planner toward its limit, the exact failure being fixed. And on quality, `llama-3.1-8b-instant` classified all four swap intents correctly (venue/time/duration/constraint, with the right deltas) but in SELECT it picked a venue with no evidence for a hard "patio" constraint — precisely the hedge the prompt forbids. One global chain could have put it on select. **Part 3 — the loop.** `withModelFallback(callType, run)` wraps ONE LOGICAL CALL: for the planner and select that includes their own correction retry, because a correction is a reply in a conversation and letting it land on a different model than the answer it corrects would be a subtler bug than the rate limit it worked around. `selectModelCall(apiKey, model)` binds a completion to one model so callers can pin it across the ladder. Exhaustion throws `AllModelsRateLimitedError` — 503 (capacity, not a bad gateway) with an actionable message and a `Retry-After` header, deliberately not the generic provider text. Every attempt is logged (`model_call` with `fellBackFrom`, `model_unavailable` with the upstream status, `model_chain_exhausted`), because running degraded silently is worse than running degraded. Swap is the one call type where exhaustion does NOT reach the user: its existing catch returns the deterministic `localFallback()`, which is right because `parseTimeExpr`/`parseDurationExpr` already own every number — that is now a documented decision rather than an accident. **MODEL CHOICES ARE FROM THE LIVE LIST, PROBED, NOT REMEMBERED.** `GET /v1/models` returned 15 active; each plausible one was run against the real planner prompt in JSON mode. Rejected: `qwen/qwen3.6-27b` fails Groq's own JSON validation (`json_validate_failed`, empty generation) and `groq/compound-mini` is an agentic tool-using system, not a plain completion model — a chain entry that cannot hold the contract is worse than a shorter chain. Chosen: planner/select `llama-3.3-70b-versatile → openai/gpt-oss-120b → openai/gpt-oss-20b`; swap `llama-3.1-8b-instant → openai/gpt-oss-20b → llama-3.3-70b-versatile` (70B last as a quality backstop, not the default). LIVE BEHAVIOUR OF EACH FALLBACK, which is the useful artifact here: `openai/gpt-oss-120b` handled specific, time-range and hard-constraint prompts correctly on the first try (6-10pm → the correct 18:00/22:00 instants) but on the bare "something to do" it returned `activities: []` with a null label — **the existing validator caught exactly that** (`activities` must be non-empty; label must be a short string) and the correction retry produced a good plan (park + café, both `confident:false`, three questions incl. when), logged as `source:"retry"`. So it costs one extra round-trip on vague prompts and is otherwise sound — and note the raw probe looked worse than the routed result, which is why verification has to run through the app rather than against the model. `openai/gpt-oss-20b` handled vague, specific and time-range correctly first try. `llama-3.1-8b-instant` is good at swap intent and NOT trusted with select, as above. THE FEATURE VERIFIED AGAINST A REAL RATE LIMIT, not a mock: with 70B genuinely 429'ing mid-session, a live `/api/parse` logged `model_unavailable llama-3.3-70b-versatile upstreamStatus:429 retryAfterSeconds:694` then `model_call openai/gpt-oss-120b attempt:2 fellBackFrom:[llama-3.3-70b-versatile]` and returned HTTP 200 with the correct plan; the full pipeline then fell back on BOTH planner and select and produced a real itinerary (Edna + Vita → After Seven, real hours filtering, real walk legs, the window validator catching a 48-minute overrun). Before this change that request was a generic 502. TESTS: new `modelFallback.test.ts` (14 cases) — 429 advances, 401/403/400/404/422 fail immediately, provider 5xx advances, non-provider errors propagate untouched, exhaustion gives the specific message + Retry-After, the last-seen retry-after survives, the serving model is logged, `retry-after` parsing across seconds/HTTP-date/absent/malformed/past, chains differ per call type, env override beats the default AND drives the actual chain. Three new route-level cases in `parse.test.ts` prove the wiring end to end: a rate-limited primary still plans, **the validation ladder still fires unchanged on a fallback model** (junk → correction → deterministic fallback, on the new model), and a correction never lands on a different model than the answer it corrects. Three provider cases in `http.test.ts` for the 429 code, the 401 non-retryability, and a non-JSON rejection still reading as a rejection. REVERT-RUNS: making `isModelRetryable` always true failed exactly the 401/403 cases; removing the 429 code failed exactly the two code assertions; removing the fallback wrapper failed exactly the three route cases — restored green each time. Gate: `npm run check` clean end to end (lint 0 errors / 11 pre-existing warnings, typecheck, 35/35 unit suites, production build, 73/73 mock E2E).

## Planner — live verification, and the three bugs only a real clock found
Goal: prove the planner round works against the real model and the real providers, not just against fixtures — this round is almost entirely prompt engineering, and this project has been burned before by a mock fixture that coincidentally matched buggy real behaviour.
What should be done: run the required prompts live on :3200 against Groq and the real Google APIs, run the whole pipeline end-to-end at least once, and fix whatever the live run exposes.
What was done (technical): all eight required prompts verified live against the real model — `"something to do"` (3 activities, both vague ones flagged `confident:false`, when-question present), `"food at midnight"` (resolves to the UPCOMING midnight and searches a 24-hour diner, the case the deleted plausibility gate used to refuse), `"make me a schedule from 3-8 tomorrow including at least 1 food option"` (15:00→20:00 with coffee/dinner/park), `"dinner tonight"`, `"dinner tonight" + the answer "5-6pm"`, `"3 activities tonight"` (exactly three — a stated count wins), `"I want to go to the FIFA fan festival"` (treated as a place to search for, asks WHEN, invents no date), and `"plan my saturday from 3-8pm"`. Then the full pipeline end-to-end against live providers: geocode → planner → questions → answers → Places → filter → select → travel → schedule. That run is the architecture rule working exactly as designed — the model proposed 90+120+30 minutes for a four-hour window, ignoring travel; the real walk legs (14/10/4 min) pushed the finish to 22:54 against a stated 22:00, and `checkWindowFit` dropped the trailing stop and named it. The LLM never got to be wrong about a number code can check. THREE BUGS, each invisible to the mock suite: (1) **the weekday floor** — `"plan my saturday"` resolved to FRIDAY. The model is told the current instant, but naming a weekday is a fact it can still get wrong, so `applyWeekdayFloor` re-derives the date from the named weekday against the plan's zone and shifts the window as a unit, keeping the wall-clock hours. It corrects FACTS, not readings: it abstains when the prompt mentions a competing calendar phrase, and leaves a correct resolution alone. (2) **a stated range rolled its two ends independently** in `mockPlan` — at 5:30 PM, `"dinner and drinks from 5-9pm"` became start=tomorrow 17:00 / end=today 21:00, an inverted window the validator rightly rejected, silently downgrading a good prompt to the fallback plan. A range now rolls as ONE unit, and which day it belongs to is decided once, by its END. This was a genuine time bomb, not a flaky test: the fixture suite pins `NOW` at 15:00, so only e2e could see it, and only after 5 PM — the 7-9pm scenario had the same latent failure waiting for 7 PM. (3) **a window already UNDERWAY was rejected rather than repaired** — asked at 6:30 for "5-9pm", the 5 has gone but the 9 has not; that is the window the user named, asked from inside it, not the mis-resolved date `MAX_START_LAG_MINUTES` exists to catch. `windowUnderway()` now repairs it the way durations are clamped (arithmetic code can fix does not deserve the one retry): the start moves up to `now`, the stated end is untouched, and rejecting a still-open window to ship a generic fallback no longer happens. The repair is deliberately narrow — a past start with NO end still fails (nothing says the request is live), a window whose end has also passed still fails, and so does a sliver too short to hold the shortest stop. TESTS: `planner.test.ts` gains the underway-window case with all three narrowing guards (35 total); `fixtures.test.ts` gains a sweep of the stated range across all 24 hours of the day plus an underway-vs-over case (12 total) — the clock-sweep is the shape that would have caught bug 2 on day one, since a fixed `NOW` cannot. REVERT-RUNS: reverting the validator guard alone, the `coercePlan` clamp alone, and the fixture range fix each failed exactly the new tests and nothing else (34/35, 34/35, 10/12), restored 35/35 and 12/12. Gate: `tsc --noEmit` clean, 34/34 unit suites, 73/73 mock E2E.

## Planner — Part 5: code owns the schedule and validates the window
Goal: make the planner's "this much fits" a proposal that code checks against reality, so a plan can never silently run hours past a window the user stated, and can never silently drop something they asked for.
What should be done: anchor `buildSchedule` on the planner's start and per-stop durations; after real travel legs are known, check whether the plan actually fits a stated end; tolerate a small overrun behind a named constant with the reasoning written down; drop the trailing activities that don't fit and say so in the planGuards voice, or fail loud if even the stated ones can't fit; log a big unfilled gap rather than building a second filling mechanism.
What was done (technical): `buildSchedule` already anchors on the planner's instant (`startOverride`, threaded in Part 1) and uses per-stop durations (Part 4). New in `schedule.ts`: `checkWindowFit(stops, endISO, tolerance)` — pure, takes the stops AS SCHEDULED (real travel already folded into their end times) and the stated end, and reports `{ overrunMinutes, fits, keep, timed, unfilledMinutes }`. It returns null when there is no usable end, because an unstated end is not a constraint and code must not invent one. `WINDOW_OVERRUN_TOLERANCE_MINUTES = 30` with the reasoning in the comment: a stated window is a human's rough intent rather than a booking, the legs inside it already carry delay margins, durations are estimates on both sides, and the only remedy available is dropping a whole activity — so the tolerance sits where dropping starts to be the lesser harm, not at zero. `WINDOW_UNDERFILL_LOG_MINUTES = 90` marks a planner that under-filled. In `finishPipeline`, `validateWindow()` runs BEFORE the arrival re-check — no point adapting a venue about to be dropped, and it keeps the common case at one routing pass. Three outcomes, all honest: fits (with a `[window-fit] underfilled` log when the gap is large, and deliberately NO code-side filling — that would be a second planning mechanism in the wrong language); `keep === 0` fails loud via the new `windowTooTightReason`, because no activity can be dropped to rescue it; otherwise the trailing stops are dropped, the plan is re-routed once, and the new `windowOverrunMessage` NAMES them ("Your 7-9pm window fits 1 of these 3 once travel is counted — dropped drinks and dessert"). Every verdict is logged as `[window-fit]`, same convention as `[swap-apply]`/`[reroute-apply]`. TESTS: six unit cases — a 3-8 window whose three stops end at 19:55 (asserting the exact scheduled end, not just a boolean), a 15-minute overrun tolerated rather than costing a stop, an over-stuffed four-stop window trimmed to exactly the two that fit with the real 125-minute overrun asserted, no-stated-end and unparseable-end returning null, `keep: 0` reported as such rather than silently trimmed, and an under-filled window measured at 245 unused minutes. Two mock e2e scenarios: a stated 5-9 window planning both stops with every rendered start time parsed and checked to fall inside 5 PM-9 PM, and the over-stuffed 7-9 case asserting the banner text, the named dropped activities, and that exactly the fitting stop ships. REVERT-RUNS: skipping `validateWindow` entirely failed the over-stuffed e2e (1 of 2); setting the tolerance to 0 failed exactly the slight-overrun case (42/43); restored 73/73 and 43/43. NOTE ON A NEAR-MISS: a `git checkout` during the revert-run restored the committed `schedule.ts` and silently discarded the uncommitted window code; `tsc` caught it immediately, and it was re-applied and re-verified before the gate. Gate: lint 0 errors / 11 pre-existing warnings, `tsc --noEmit` clean, 34/34 unit suites, Next 16 production build clean, 73/73 mock E2E on port 3100.

## Planner — Part 4: durations the LLM estimates and code bounds
Goal: stop deriving every stop length from a keyword table that cannot tell a tasting menu from a ramen counter, while keeping the table as the honest fallback for every stop that never meets a planner.
What should be done: have the planner propose `estimatedMinutes` per activity (clamped 15–360); give the selector a second, cheap chance to adjust each one now that the real venue is known, preferably folded into the existing selectVenues call; carry the resolved duration ON the stop so nothing downstream re-derives it; keep `getDuration`/`DURATION_TABLE` and have the scheduler prefer the stop's own value when present.
What was done (technical): the planner already proposes and clamps `estimatedMinutes` (Part 1). The refinement is FOLDED INTO the existing selectVenues Groq call rather than a second one — an extra round-trip per plan is real latency for a judgment the selector is already making with the venue in front of it. Each slot now arrives carrying `estimatedMinutes`, the response schema gained a `minutes` field, and the system prompt tells the model to adjust it against the actual place ("a tasting-menu restaurant is not a ramen counter; a major museum is not a single gallery"). `resolveMinutes()` resolves model → planner → undefined and CLAMPS to 15–360: a bad estimate must never reach the scheduler, but it also must never invalidate an otherwise-good selection or burn the correction retry, so it is clamped rather than treated as a problem. The result rides on `Selection.plannedMinutes` → `SelectionLike` → `ItineraryStop`, the same route `priceLevel`/`description`/`currentOpeningHours` already travel. `buildSchedule` now reads `sel.plannedMinutes ?? getDuration(category).baseMinutes`; the BUFFER still comes from the table, deliberately — a buffer is our scheduling margin (transition, ordering, settling), a policy rather than a fact about the venue, so it is not the model's to set. `DURATION_TABLE` is untouched and still owns every stop that never met a planner: swap's six call sites, reroute, and `recoverySlots.provisionalArrivals` (which now also prefers a slot's own minutes when it has them). The deterministic fallback assignment keeps the PLANNER's estimate while discarding the rejected model answer's — the two were never the same claim. Boundary: `/api/select` accepts `plannedMinutes` index-aligned with `slots`, validated by `parseSlotEstimates` (length must match the slots exactly, values 1–360), and the client payload validator accepts the field on a selection. FIXTURE NOTE: `MOCK_MINUTES` deliberately equals `DURATION_TABLE`'s base for every fixture category — the mock's job is to prove the plumbing, and three arrival-sensitive e2e scenarios (per-slot recovery targeting, weather-at-that-slot, closes-before-you-arrive) pin exact clock times that would shift for no product reason if the fixture disagreed with the table; that a DIFFERENT estimate really moves the schedule is pinned in schedule.test.ts instead. TESTS: schedule.test.ts gained "a stop's OWN duration wins over the table; the buffer stays ours" (150-minute base + the table's 15-minute buffer, asserting the chain actually moves); select.test.ts gained five cases — the estimate reaching the model, the refinement riding on the pick, clamping in both directions plus rounding without burning the retry, the missing-refinement ladder (model → planner → table), the deterministic fallback keeping the planner's estimate, and a mismatched array rejected at the boundary before any model work. REVERT-RUNS: making the scheduler ignore `plannedMinutes` failed exactly the new schedule case (36/37); removing the clamp failed exactly the new clamp case (27/28); restored 37/37 and 28/28. Gate: lint 0 errors / 11 pre-existing warnings, `tsc --noEmit` clean, 34/34 unit suites, 71/71 mock E2E on port 3100.

## Planner — Parts 1 & 2: parse becomes a planner, and the questions become its own
Goal: stop filling seven fixed fields and letting hardcoded tables make every real planning decision, and let the LLM decide the SHAPE of the day — which activities, how many, roughly how long, what to search for, what to ask about — while code keeps owning every fact that can be checked.
What should be done: replace the extraction-only parse with a planner call that is told the current instant in the plan's timezone; validate its output in code with the same ladder selectVenues uses (problems → one correction retry → deterministic fallback); replace clarify.ts's rule table with the planner's own questions, capped at three, one round, always asking about time when none was given; keep the immediacy and all-day floors over the raw prompt; keep every downstream stage working unchanged.
What was done (technical): new `app/api/parse/planner.ts` holds the whole contract — the system prompt, `buildPlannerMessages` (which injects `now` as ISO + weekday + local date/time + zone), `findPlanProblems`, `validatePlan`, `planWithModel`, `fallbackPlan`, `applyTimeFloors`, `planToParsed` and `planStartInstant`. `/api/parse` is now a thin route over that ladder and answers `{ plan, parsed }`. WHY BOTH: the pipeline's currency is `ParsedPrompt`, and rewriting Places/filter/select/swap/reroute/the store was explicitly out of scope, so the planner's activity set projects onto `category_signals` and its context fills the rest; every downstream stage is untouched. ONE contract change to be aware of: `time_window` is now PROSE (a display label), and the resolved instant travels explicitly — `targetTime` into the search route, `startOverride` into `buildSchedule` — so search, the hours filter, the weather gate and the schedule share one anchor instead of three independent re-derivations of the same string. `stop_count` is always null now: the activity list is the count, so `resolveRequestedSlots`/`distributionOptions` are no longer reachable from the client (planSlots survives as the API-boundary guard for stored parses). REORDERED: the geocode now runs BEFORE the parse, because the planner cannot reason about "tonight" without being told which clock — `resolvePlace()` was extracted from `continuePipeline` for it, and the geocode-ambiguity recovery now carries the raw prompt and resumes into `resolvePlace` → `planFrom` rather than resuming a parse that never happened. CODE-OWNED GUARANTEES the prompt asks for but cannot be trusted to deliver: an unspecified time always ships a when-question (options now / this afternoon / this evening / pick a time); questions are ranked (vague-attached, then when, then filler) and capped at 3; slots are renumbered dense from 0 with question targets remapped with them; duplicate question ids are made unique because they key the UI's answer state; `estimatedMinutes` is CLAMPED to 15–360 rather than rejected, because burning the one retry on arithmetic code can fix is waste; a start more than 60 minutes in the past, or more than 14 days out, IS a problem (that is a mis-resolved date, not rounding). DEVIATION from the proposed schema: a question covers every vague activity sharing its `searchQuery`, not just the one slot it names — "three places" is three vague slots but one honest question, and the pre-planner clarify made the same call ("drinks then another bar is two slots but one question"). DEVIATION on the all-day floor: it anchors a missing START at 11:00 and deliberately does NOT invent an `endISO`; an end time is a stated fact or nothing, and a regex fabricating one is the exact habit this change removes. PART 2: `clarify.ts` and `clarify.test.ts` are DELETED — `GENERIC_RULES`, `kindQuestion`, `applyNarrowAnswer`, `categoriesForKindAnswer`, `timeWindowForWhenAnswer` and the when/vibe/distribution logic all have no callers left. The clarify UI keeps its markup and skip behaviour but is now generic (question + chips + free text, keyed by question id) because the questions are no longer a fixed set of ids the component could special-case. Answers go back for a SECOND planner pass rather than a client-side merge — documented in the code: folding free-form answers onto searchQueries and a time intent is the same judgment the planner just made, and doing it twice in two languages is how the old table grew. The judgment those rules encoded (which categories are too generic; what hour an unstated "dinner" implies) moved into `mockPlan` in `fixtures.ts`, where it belongs — that fixture stands in for the MODEL, and its raw response still runs through the production validator, floors and adapter. `searchPlaces` gained `GENERAL_CATEGORY`/`isGeneralCategory`: a vague prompt used to reach it as an EMPTY category list, and the planner always emits at least one activity, so the named "things to do" category now expands to the same five-query day-and-night union (the call bound is documented as 19 worst case). `/api/parse`'s burst allowance went 60 → 120 because one PLAN can now cost two calls to that route; every other route is unchanged. TESTS: new `planner.test.ts` (31 cases) is the heaviest-covered file in the app, as the layer between a hallucinated plan and the user should be — clamping, every malformed shape, both horizon directions, the vagueness contract and its shared-query coverage, the code-owned guarantees, the cap's ranking, and the ladder itself (valid-first-answer / correction-retry-with-problems-spelled-out / still-invalid-falls-back, plus a case asserting the fallback survives its OWN validator). `parse.test.ts` was rewritten to the route boundary: the previous suite pinned `groq_invalid_schema` 502s and stop_count expansion, both removed by design (a malformed answer is corrected then fallen back from — a raw model error must never reach the user). `fixtures.test.ts` now runs every fixture response through the production validator. E2E: the geocode specs moved their clarify dismissal below the ambiguity choice (consequence of the reorder, commented in place); the WHEN options changed from "later today" to "this evening"; and "an exact stop count cannot bypass kind clarification" was REWRITTEN — it pinned a mandatory gate that came from stop_count needing a distribution, and refusing to plan for someone who declined to answer is precisely what this change removes, so both skipping and answering now produce a real three-stop plan. REVERT-RUNS: removing the when-question guarantee, the duration clamp, and the immediacy floor each failed exactly their own case in both planner.test.ts (30/31) and parse.test.ts (9/10); restored 31/31 and 10/10. Gate: lint 0 errors / 11 pre-existing warnings, `tsc --noEmit` clean, 34/34 unit suites, Next 16 production build clean, 71/71 mock E2E on port 3100; port 3000 untouched.

## Planner — Part 3: delete the plausibility gate
Goal: stop refusing times that a hardcoded table considers unreasonable, and let the objective hours filter — which reads real Places data — be the only thing that decides whether an hour works.
What should be done: remove `PLAUSIBLE_BANDS`, `isPlausibleAt`, `bandForCategories`, `implausibleExplicitReason`, `implausibleInferredReason`, `IMPLAUSIBLE_TIME_MESSAGE`, and the `overridable`/`category` fields on `StartResolution`; remove the client's `time-gate` recovery mode and its two actions; keep the swap engine's bare-hour meridiem bias working; delete or rewrite every test that pinned the removed verdicts, deliberately, and say which.
What was done (technical): `resolveStartTimeChecked` is now a thin pass-through over `resolveStartTimeResult` — it still fails loud on MALFORMED input (an unparseable clock, contradictory calendar qualifiers, a DST gap, a date in the past), because those are facts about the input, but it no longer holds an opinion about the hour. `StartResolution` collapsed to `{ok:true,start} | {ok:false,reason}`. In `page.tsx` the whole `mode: "time-gate"` arm of the `recovery` union is gone, along with `overrideTimeGate`/`timeGateSomethingElse`, the `ContinueOptions.overrideTimeGate` flag, its plumbing through both geocode-ambiguity pauses and the geocode resume, and the all-empty branch that existed only to catch a post-override dead end; an impossible hour now falls through to the normal empty-pool net, where `noVenuesReason` already names `hours` as the dominant drop cause. SURVIVING BEHAVIOUR: the swap engine's meridiem disambiguation ("make it 10" on a brunch stop means 10 AM) needed a category→hours notion, so `swap.ts` gained its own minimal `ROUGH_HOURS` table plus `roughHoursFor`/`hourInRoughHours`, scoped in comments to reading an ambiguous number and explicitly unable to refuse anything; park membership still comes from the shared traits table. The swap engine's own `isPlausibleAt` refusal ("a 4 AM dinner won't work") was deleted — the move is still fully checked, by the floor guard, the overlap guard, and `resettleTail`'s re-filter at every resulting arrival. TESTS — deleted outright (all pinned the removed verdict): `schedule.test.ts`'s "an INFERRED out-of-band time names the real obstacle", "GATE CONTINUATION (batch 4c)", "OVERRIDE BOUNDARY (batch 4b)", "4 AM 'axe throwing': plausible-band check fails loud", "explicit implausible times fail loud with the category's window (BUG 1)", "plausibility validates the first scheduled category"; `e2e/recovery.spec.ts`'s entire four-scenario "inferred-time gate" describe block; and `e2e/failloud.spec.ts`'s `"brunch at 3am"` / `"dinner at 4am"` exact-message rows. Rewritten rather than weakened, each with a comment naming what changed and why: the multi-city case now asserts the resolved LOCAL wall-clock hour instead of a band verdict; "park prompts anchor immediately at ANY hour" asserts a 23:30 park prompt resolves to midnight instead of refusing; the 3 AM "now" cases assert the resolved instant instead of a refusal; "checked resolver" asserts each resolved instant rather than a bare `ok`; the `"Monday 2"` case still proves a bare hour after a weekday parses as a clock, now landing Monday 02:00; and `swap.test.ts`'s 4 AM case asserts the FACTUAL refusal ("earlier than where the evening already is") with a new sibling proving an unusual forward time is no longer refused up front. REVERT-RUN: reintroducing a crude 8–23 band inside `resolveStartTimeChecked` failed exactly the seven rewritten cases (29/36); restored, 36/36. Gate at this commit: `tsc --noEmit` clean, 34/34 unit suites green.

## Audit fix — Batch H: typed geocoding, honest travel fallbacks, and bounded provider work
Goal: stop ambiguous city/address input from silently choosing an unrelated provider result, make failed/short walking routes honest and cheaper, and reduce duplicate Places work without discarding deterministic facts or violating provider caching policy.
What should be done: use a purpose-built geocoder with result-type/component validation, city-biased address lookup, formatted ambiguity candidates, and a dedicated key; resume ambiguity through the one recovery component; add a conservative detour and explicit uncertainty to failed Routes estimates; omit transit below a defensible short-hop threshold; show a readable accessible walking caution and never draw an unknown estimate as a route; measure Places fields/calls first, bound calls, preserve fresh hours and required evidence, and cache only where provider policy permits.
What was done (technical): replaced Places Text Search geocoding with Google Geocoding API v3 behind `GOOGLE_GEOCODING_API_KEY`; a new core validates locality/address result types, coordinates, formatted address, locality/region/country components, partial/incomplete matches, viewport bounds, and provider envelopes. Address requests carry the resolved city viewport plus country restriction and are rechecked against the city; multiple valid results return a bounded discriminated candidate list. `page.tsx` added `recovery.mode: "geocode"` to the existing panel, runtime-validates the envelope, pauses before venue search, preserves the selected formatted city/address and timezone through resume/time override, and sends raw address plus resolved city context. Mock mode now supplies provider-shaped geocode data through the production validator. Routes fallback remains `unknown` but now uses a 1.35× network-detour factor plus a separate 20% uncertainty margin (minimum 5 minutes); hops below 250 m haversine call WALK only; unknown estimates draw no invented map line; every real walking leg shows a normal-opacity 11 px visible/screen-reader caution. Places retains the complete Enterprise + Atmosphere field set because every field feeds deterministic filtering, structured constraint evidence, routing, or shipped card output; calls are hard-bounded at 8 daytime/16 late-night/5 general, identical normalized query+type promises deduplicate within one invocation, already-late categories no longer generate `late night late night …`, failures evict, and later attempts refetch current hours. Measured ordinary 1→1, eight daytime 8→8, eight late-night max 16→16, general 5→5, overlapping `[bar, late night bar]` 4→2. DEVIATION: no cross-request full-payload TTL cache and no discovery→Details split—current Places policy restricts content storage, hours would stale, and the added Details call is not a cheaper fact-safe shortlist under current pricing. REVERT-RUNS: first-result geocoding failed both ambiguity cases (7/9); optimistic/unconditional travel failed exactly 2 new travel cases (15/17); hiding the walking caution failed its browser case; mapping `unknown` as walk failed the map-line browser case; bypassing Places request-local dedupe failed exactly the overlap case (21/22). Final gate: lint exit 0 with 11 deferred Batch-J warnings, TypeScript clean, 382/382 unit tests across 32 suites, Next 16 production build clean, and 68/68 mock E2E green on port 3100; port 3000 was untouched.

## Audit fix — Batch G: slot-aware recovery and failure-safe client transport
Goal: make every recovery attempt evaluate the actual slot it is filling, prevent an already-used venue from being duplicated, and keep the planner usable when any browser request or Maps load rejects, times out, returns non-JSON, or succeeds but its follow-up refresh fails.
What should be done: preserve stable slot identity through empty and weather recovery; derive provisional arrivals and replace them with scheduled starts when available; filter each replacement at that slot arrival; exclude venues occupied by other slots; retain prior pool candidates; validate every client response at runtime behind one bounded fetch helper; clear planning, recovery, reroute, and swap busy states in `finally`; clear stale weather on a new attempt; distinguish mutation failure from post-mutation refresh failure; and prove invalid-key, retry-success, and remount recovery in a real browser component.
What was done (technical): added `recoverySlots.ts` for provisional/final per-slot arrivals, occupied-ID exclusion, and append-only pool merging; selections now order by numeric slot before the legacy category fallback, so repeated categories and category-changing replacements retain prompt order. Empty and weather rows carry slot identity, recovery searches at that row's arrival, weather is evaluated there, and an occupied-only pool fails honestly instead of duplicating a venue. Added `clientFetch.ts` with caller-abort propagation, bounded deadlines covering fetch/body/validation, safe JSON/error handling, and runtime parser support; `clientPayloads.ts` validates every planning, persistence, reroute, and swap envelope consumed by the UI, including complete travel/transit and nested itinerary/location/hours/timezone shapes while accepting the honest unknown-leg fallback. All page requests use those boundaries, a ref-backed single-operation lock closes same-tick and cross-action races, every busy flag clears in `finally`, simulation refreshes join the same lock, and new plans clear prior weather and weather-block notes. A mutation whose response is lost now performs a guarded read-back and refreshes the latest saved plan; a failed read-back explicitly warns that the view may be stale. Added a mock-only Maps component harness with distinct provider-auth, blocked-script retry, and rejected-cache remount scenarios. The mock runner owns the exact hidden port-3100 Next process and a small reporter returns Playwright's authoritative result before process-tree cleanup; Windows uses PID-scoped `taskkill`, Unix gives each child its own process group, direct Playwright invocation keeps an equivalent fallback, and port 3000 is never referenced. Added 24 focused unit cases and 23 browser cases across client failures, cross-action races, ambiguous mutation reconciliation, slot recovery, and Maps resilience. REVERT-RUNS: forcing recovery back to the plan anchor failed both later-slot scenarios (2/2); removing swap's `finally` cleanup failed the rejection/busy-state scenario (1/1); retaining the rejected Maps promise failed both retry/remount scenarios (2/2). Final gate: lint exit 0 with 11 Batch-J warnings, TypeScript clean, 362/362 unit tests across 30 suites, Next 16 production build clean, and 65/65 mock E2E green on port 3100; port 3000 remained untouched.

## Audit fix — Batch F: deterministic planning inputs and assignments
Goal: make calendar qualifiers, clocks, requested stop counts, numeric budgets, opening hours, hard constraints, and multi-slot venue assignment deterministic so the plan cannot silently reinterpret or narrow what the user asked for.
What should be done: resolve supported dates and weekdays in the plan timezone with explicit DST and nearest-future rules; reject malformed dates, clocks, and stop counts; make the first scheduled category own time plausibility; turn valid counts into exact ordered slots or clarification; keep shared candidates until global assignment; treat numeric budgets as structured ranking context rather than an automatic cheap cap; preserve original slot IDs through model validation; admit only provider-structured hard-constraint evidence; and return unknown for inconclusive hours.
What was done (technical): added `planSlots.ts`, `budget.ts`, and `constraints.ts` as deterministic cores. Calendar resolution now supports today/tomorrow, bare and `next` weekdays, ISO and named dates, date-plus-clock combinations, strict clock ranges, DST-gap rejection, and earliest fall-back overlap selection through `zoneTime.ts`; README documents the nearest-future rule. Exact stop counts are bounded, single categories expand to repeated slots, ambiguous multi-category counts open clarification, and mandatory counted-kind questions resist both blank Go and Skip. Filtering deduplicates only within each pool. Selection keeps original slot numbers, validates both model attempts, permits hard constraints only from explicit provider fields, rejects invented unmet constraints and instruction-like text, and uses a maximum-cardinality evidenced fallback that preserves earlier-slot ranking. Structured budget parsing distinguishes Places-level caps from numeric amount/currency hints. Hours parsing is tri-state for malformed/mixed data. Mock mode now replaces only the model completion while exercising the production selector; one-slot recovery scopes that completion to the replacement category. Full-gate testing found and fixed recovery replacement omission and reversed duplicate fallback order. Seven targeted revert-runs each failed the intended regression (calendar, count expansion, pre-dedup, constraint enforcement, dense slot numbering, numeric hard cap, malformed hours) before restoration. Final gate: lint exit 0 with 13 Batch-J warnings, TypeScript clean, 338/338 unit tests across 27 suites, Next 16 production build clean, and 42/42 mock E2E green on port 3100; port 3000 remained untouched.

## Audit fix — Batch E: complete, atomic swap proposals
Goal: stop venue, time, and duration swaps from committing unreachable targets, overlapping locked stops, or silently abandoning an incomplete downstream tail.
What should be done: clone every proposal; preserve the product contract (venue holds its whole slot, time moves the target, duration holds the start); route the anchor inbound before commit; prove every downstream arrival and locked boundary; recheck hours and weather after final routing; revalidate replacements whose routes change arrival; fetch weather once; fail on missing coordinates or boundaries; copy back only a complete proposal and keep refusals byte- and version-stable.
