Net effect: the documented try → adapt → notify ladder never adapts in production; it only fires in mock mode, where `fixtureHoursById` supplies the hours the real path lacks. That also means §1.1's severity in production was lower than the audit stated (the wrong-zone verdict was masked by never being consulted) — but the underlying gap is arguably larger, and it is exactly the kind of thing mock-only exercise hides. The Group A fix stands on its own (the helper is now correct and the trap is gone); whether to give the seam real hours data — carry `currentOpeningHours` onto stored stops, or re-fetch in `placeOf` — is a product call for you, so it is recorded here and NOT silently implemented.

## Docs + audit: README/CLAUDE.md refreshed to the shipped feature set; full logic audit run (report only)
Goal: both documents had drifted behind what actually ships (multi-city + per-city timezones, the three-mode recovery panel, the late-hour and weather gates, distance-aware selection/swaps, the broken-transit walk escape hatch), and no systematic logic review had been done since that work landed. Two deliverables in one pass: bring the docs current, then audit the codebase with fresh eyes. NO application code was touched — the audit is a report to be triaged into batches later, not a batch itself.
What should be done: refresh README (live feature list, every env var the code actually reads, limitations reconciled against CLAUDE.md's open gaps) and CLAUDE.md (open gaps re-verified item by item, plus any section whose shape no longer matches the code); separately produce a dated logic-only audit with file+line, impact, severity, and a test-coverage note per finding.
What was done (technical): DOCS — README rewritten in place (same voice/structure, not a from-scratch rewrite): the Ossington-only framing is gone, a "What it actually does" section now covers multi-city zones, distance-aware picks, the recovery panel's three situations, and the walk-relabel rule; the env section was rebuilt from an actual `grep` of `process.env.*` rather than memory, adding a second table for `KV_REST_API_URL`/`KV_REST_API_TOKEN` (+ the `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_*` aliases), `VERCEL`, `E2E_MOCK`, and `TZ` — with each one's reading module named, and the note that there is NO Geocoding API key (geocode reuses the Places key); "Known limitations" is now an explicit short mirror of CLAUDE.md's gap list so the two documents can't contradict each other. CLAUDE.md — "What this is" de-Ossingtoned; a new "Shipped — interactive recovery panel" operational section records the union's three modes and their load-bearing rules (overridable set ONLY on the inferred path; weather override = `weather:null` and nothing else; "something else" scopes to the slot, not the plan); the fail-loud guard-order line now says where the panel sits and when a refusal string is the wrong answer; "Currently in progress" replaced (it still described pre-deploy state and 16/16 e2e). OPEN GAPS re-verified item by item against code rather than trusted as written — most stand; three changed: the weather item was NARROWED (the panel closed the "no recourse" half; the residual "a declined block has no pin" is still true and kept), "reroute skips weather gate" was CORRECTED to "reroute AND swap" (both pass `weather: null`), and the standing `TZ` note was corrected — server TZ no longer drives scheduling, but it does still leak in via the swap engine's `usableByHours`, which is an audit finding, not a policy. AUDIT — `code-audit-2026-07-18.md` at the repo root: 27 findings (3 high / 13 medium / 11 low) grouped in the seven requested areas, each with file+line, user-visible impact, severity, fix shape, and whether a passing suite would currently catch it. The two specifically-asked-about traces were run rather than guessed: `parseTargetTime`'s `day` field is CONFIRMED dead (every consumer traced — `schedule.ts:239` null-checks only, `:299-301` reads hour/minute, `filter.ts` builds its own target; documented as a live trap since the field is server-local and `TargetTime.day` is load-bearing for `isOpenAt`), and `createItinerary` DOES still write directly to the Map, with all 13 callers traced — harmless today (the only production caller saves immediately after) but carrying two latent effects, alongside its unused sibling `getItinerary` which bypasses KV entirely. Headline findings: the swap engine's availability check reads the SERVER's wall clock (high — and untestable today, since every unit test injects a stub and the mock fixture reproduces the same flaw); the hours filter and weather gate judge EVERY category at the first stop's instant, so later stops are filtered at the wrong time (high); duplicate category signals collapse silently into one stop, and in reroute plan the same venue twice (medium, and exactly the silent-drop class the recovery work exists to prevent). Weak typing confirmed as still exactly four `any`s (page.tsx ×3, weather route ×1) with no creep, each with its concrete replacement type. An appendix records what was checked and found CORRECT (dedup ordering, select's ID validation ladder, reroute floor/anchor math, zoneTime's weekday conversion, the weather-override mechanism) so a future pass doesn't re-derive it. Verified (no app logic changed, confirmed anyway): 192 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 34/34 mock e2e; port 3000 untouched.

## Feature: weather-blocked category gets an honest gate, not a useless widen offer
Goal: when a category came back empty specifically because the WEATHER gate blocked it (rain/cold at the resolved time) while other categories survived, it landed in the generic recovery panel offering to "widen the search area" — useless, since bad weather isn't a neighbourhood problem. Same shape as batch 4b's late-hour gate: an honest message plus a real choice. Scope: PARTIAL failures only; the ALL-weather-blocked path (`weatherBlockedReason`) stays exactly as-is.
What was done (technical): the recovery union gained `mode: "weather-gate"` (same panel component, third trigger — no new UI). In `continuePipeline`'s partial-failure branch, empty categories are PARTITIONED by `_weatherBlocked` membership: weather-blocked ones open the weather-gate first (message = the filter's real reason verbatim — "Rain likely at 3pm — park walk might not be great right now. Still want it, or something else?"), carrying any generically-empty categories behind it as `pendingEmpties` so they get their normal rows afterwards. THE OVERRIDE MECHANISM costs zero server changes: "Still want it" re-searches ONLY the blocked category (`categoriesOverride`) with `weather: null` — `filterPools` skips the weather gate by its existing keep-on-missing policy while hours/rating/price/business-status all still run. A surviving pick merges into the plan (shared `mergeSlot`); still-empty becomes a NORMAL empty slot routed into the EXISTING generic recovery flow (widen/replace) — never a third dead end. REFACTOR for reuse, not duplication: `resolveEmpty`'s fetch core was extracted into `searchSlot(ctx, category, {dropLocation, ignoreWeather})` + `mergeSlot`, now shared by widen/replace AND the weather override — one search path. "Something else" (JUDGMENT CALL, documented): the time-gate's version re-opens the whole-plan kind picker, but here OTHER STOPS SURVIVED — discarding them would be wrong — so "something else" scopes to the slot via the existing recovery replace rows (the input IS the "different direction without retyping" affordance), with the weather reason carried onto the row, the widen button suppressed per-row (`noWiden` — rain isn't a radius problem), and "Plan without it" available as a real third option since other stops exist. Stale-note fix: a successfully overridden category clears its "Skipped the X — rain…" map note (the stop is planned now, not skipped); declined/unresolved blocks keep theirs honestly. Tests: 4 new @mock e2e — deterministic at any run hour via the stated "3pm" (server+client agree) and MOCK_RAIN_HOUR=15: gate shows the REAL reason with NO widen button; "Still want it" plans both stops (the dinner pick — Noodle Letterpress over the closed-at-3PM Velvet Fig — proves the hours filter still ran) and the stale note is gone; the empty-even-without-weather case ("beach") lands in the existing generic recovery and replace recovers to a 2-stop plan; "something else" shows the no-widen replace row with skip offered and recovers via replace. REGRESSION (the one that matters most): the all-blocked "rained-out outdoor plan fails loud" e2e passes byte-unchanged — the partition only runs in the NOT-all-empty branch. Verified: 192 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 34/34 mock e2e. LIVE on :3200 with REAL weather (a Saturday-3-PM thunderstorm, precip 85%, happened to be in the forecast): "dinner and a walk in the park tomorrow at 3pm" → the gate fired (`[weather-gate] … verdict=BLOCK — rain likely at 3pm`), panel showed "Rain likely at 3pm — park walk might not be great right now" with both buttons; "Still want it" replanned BOTH stops (Earls Kitchen + Philosopher's Walk 4.7★ Free) with no stale skipped-note. Honest note: the first live attempt raced the 24-hour forecast horizon (target minutes past its edge → `forecast=NONE`, gate skipped, plan built normally — correct keep-on-missing behavior); minutes later the horizon covered the storm and the gate fired.

## Fix (batch 4c): "something else" after the late-hour gate stays TONIGHT
Goal: confirmed live — reaching the gate at ~10:54 PM ("outdoors" implausible), choosing "something else", then picking "drinks" planned TOMORROW 8 PM. Root cause: the continuation fell through to the picked category's plain default-time resolution (bar's 20:00 default → already passed → rolled forward, correct per its own logic, wrong in this context). Reaching the gate at all means no time was typed and the app's own RIGHT-NOW guess was the problem — the person is clearly asking about tonight; the "still want it" path already keeps that context (it planned Grange Park at 11:09 PM), "something else" must too.
What was done (technical): DESIGN CHOICE (per the brief's own framing — and documented as instructed): the gate's `timeGateSomethingElse` stamps `time_window: timeWindowForWhenAnswer("now")` onto the carried parse — the EXACT semantics of answering the original When question with "now" — rather than adding a gate-specific resolver mode. One line in the gate handler; the top-level clarify flow's own when-handling is untouched (it already asks explicitly, and its pins all pass unchanged). Consequence accepted and documented: picking a still-closed direction after the gate (e.g. "outdoors" again at 11 PM) now gets the explicit-window refusal instead of a second gate — identical to typing now+outdoors fresh, and consistent with 4b's pinned rule that a stated time is never overridable (the user was already offered the override once and declined it). Skipping the re-shown kind picker also resolves "now" → tonight's general pool, an improvement over the old inferred fall-through. FIXTURE (data-only): the BAR pool gained **Night Owl** (4.1, NO listed hours) — the keep-on-missing any-hour survivor, same role as "Fixture … Three", lowest-rated so it never displaces the picks other specs pin (Curfew evenings / Standing Room late) — without it the new e2e's server-side hours filter empties the bar pool on morning/small-hours runs. Tests: +1 schedule case pinning BOTH resolver shapes at 22:54 ("unspecified"+bar → tomorrow 20:00, the bug shape; "now"+bar → ok, TONIGHT 23:00 inside the bar band's wrap); +1 gate e2e (frozen 22:54 → gate → something else → drinks → first stop is a bar with NO "tomorrow" prefix at 11:xx PM) — REVERT-RUN PROVEN: with the stamp removed, exactly this test fails on the "tomorrow" label (the reporter's symptom) and nothing else moves. Verified: 192 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 30/30 mock e2e; the non-gate clarify pins (vague-but-sincere "later today"; batch-4.3 kind-steering with "later today") all pass unchanged — the top-level flow's time behavior is untouched. LIVE on :3200 (clock frozen 22:54, real APIs): the exact reported sequence → "drinks" continuation now TARGETS TONIGHT — the server log reads `time_window="now"` (previously "unspecified"→default→tomorrow) and the surface message quoted "around 11:00 PM"; the pool itself came back honestly empty because the REAL clock was ~5 AM and the server-side hours filter runs on the server's clock (the documented dev-sim split — in production both clocks agree, and the mock e2e pins the full drinks case deterministically); the same live sequence with "something to do" completed end-to-end TONIGHT: **Velvet Underground (nightclub, 4.3★), board 11:00 PM, 11:20 PM–12:30 AM** — no "tomorrow" anywhere.

## Feature (batch 4b): the inferred-time refusal is now a real choice, not a dead end
Goal: product decision follow-up to batch 4.1 — when the user typed NO time and the app's own inferred slot landed outside a known category band ("outdoors" as the vague-prompt kind at 11 PM), the specific-but-final refusal was still a dead end. If someone genuinely wants a park late, let them say so: an interactive panel with "Still want it" (informed override) and "Something else" (change direction without retyping). The explicit-time guard must not move an inch.
What was done (technical): THE LOAD-BEARING BOUNDARY first — `StartResolution`'s failure arm gained `overridable?: boolean` + `category?`, set in exactly ONE place: the `implausibleInferredReason` path (no clock time, no day-part, no explicit "now"; a KNOWN category band blocked our own guess). `implausibleExplicitReason` failures and the generic add-a-time case carry NO flag, so the UI cannot wire the override onto the wrong path even by accident; a new "OVERRIDE BOUNDARY" unit case pins `overridable === undefined` for "brunch at 3am", "dinner at 4am", day-part brunch, 3 AM "now", and "now"+park at 11 PM (a stated time + park stays a hard fail — the override is only for OUR guesses, never theirs). The inferred message dropped its trailing suggestion ("the buttons are the suggestion"): "It's 11 PM — late for a typical park visit (park around here runs about 6 AM to 10 PM)." PANEL — the existing Bug-1 recovery component was GENERALIZED, not duplicated: `recovery` state became a discriminated union (`mode: "empty" | "time-gate"`), one `recoveryBlock` renders both variants with the same classes; the gate shows the reason + "Still want to try one, or do something else?" with the two buttons. "Still want it" re-enters `continuePipeline` with `opts.overrideTimeGate` — the gate is skipped for THAT run only (the instant itself is unchanged; the objective hours filter still runs, and hours-less venues pass via keep-on-missing, which is exactly why late park overrides genuinely find things). "Something else" re-opens batch 4's kind picker via the new `kindQuestion()` export (single source — clarifyQuestions uses the same object, pinned by a unit test) on the same prompt with categories cleared. OVERRIDE-THEN-NOTHING: an all-empty result after an override routes into the SAME recovery panel (synthesized null-id selections, honest per-category reason, widen/replace available; "Plan without it" is suppressed whenever nothing real was picked) — the normal all-empty path keeps its plain fail-loud message, pinned unchanged. Scope note: built generically (any banded category with no start default arms the gate), but that set is naturally the park family today — categories with start defaults roll forward instead, which is why only parks ever hit this. FIXTURES (data-only): generic "Fixture … Three" now carries NO hours — the keep-on-missing any-hour survivor that makes late-night e2e deterministic across run hours; new deliberately-EMPTY "beach" pool (park-family band, so the gate fires; nothing found, so override→recovery is exercisable at any hour) + its mockParse signal; both documented in e2e/README. Tests: 191 unit across 15 suites (schedule 31: inferred failures now assert `overridable`+`category`, explicit failures assert its absence; clarify 12: kindQuestion single-source) and 29/29 mock e2e — 4 new gate scenarios (panel not string; override plans; override-empty lands in recovery, widens honestly, replace recovers; something-else returns to the kind picker and continues), all deterministic at any run hour. LIVE on :3200 (real Groq/Places, clock frozen 22:54): "not sure what to do" → kind "outdoors", no time → the PANEL ("It's 11 PM — late for a typical park visit… Still want to try one, or do something else?"), not the old refusal; "Still want it" → **Grange Park, 11:09 PM, Free, 9-min walk** (a real late-night park plan — the reporter's exact wish); "Something else" → kind picker → "drinks" → a real bar plan, no error. Observed interaction noted honestly: the drinks continuation planned for TOMORROW 8 PM — that's the pre-existing bar category-default (20:00) rolling forward past 22:54, not a 4b regression; answering When="now" gets tonight (bar band wraps to 2 AM).

## Feature (batch 4.3): richer clarifying questions for ultra-vague prompts
Goal: the reporter's own framing — a prompt this open-ended "should probably get MORE clarifying questions, not fewer, to make a good guess at what's wanted". Phase 2 asks only When?/vibe, neither of which narrows WHAT the person wants; the plan then rests entirely on the general pool. Extend the existing clarify.ts infrastructure (not a second questions system).
What should be done: add a "what kind of thing" question for genuinely ultra-vague prompts, without regressing Phase 2's skip logic (a prompt that already gives enough must still get zero questions).
What was done (technical): `clarify.ts` gained a third question id `kind` — asked ONLY when `hasCategory` is false (the ultra-vague case), placed FIRST (it's the highest-value answer), with the cap raised 2 → 3. JUDGMENT CALL on wording/options (per the brief): question "What kind of thing?", options **food / drinks / something to do / outdoors** — deliberately FOUR broad buckets rather than an exhaustive taxonomy, matching the reporter's "not necessarily exhaustive, just a good guess": one tap, no scrolling, and they span the axes that actually change the search (eat / drink / do / outside). New `categoriesForKindAnswer` maps them onto terms the REST of the pipeline already understands, so bands/durations/search all work with zero new plumbing: food→["restaurant"], drinks→["bar"], outdoors→["park"], and — a deliberate choice — **"something to do" → []**, i.e. it stays on the general pool, because after 4.2 that pool IS the right tool for "surprise me"; free text passes through as its own category so a typed "bowling" behaves exactly like a typed prompt. Wired through page.tsx (`clarifyKind` state, chips + free-text input, merged into `category_signals` on submit, reset per run). Note the kind answer composes with the bands honestly: choosing "outdoors" at 11 PM now yields 4.1's "too late for park today" rather than a silent bad plan — the questions help the app make a good guess, they don't override reality. Tests: clarify unit cases updated/added — ultra-vague now asks `["kind","when","vibe"]`; `kind` is asked ONLY with no category and never displaces the others; every mapping pinned incl. the general-pool and free-text cases; **Phase 2's skip logic re-pinned unchanged** (category+time → [], category+aesthetic → [], vibe suppressed by group/constraints/aesthetic). New e2e "clarify: the KIND answer steers the plan, and repeated answers don't leak": answering "drinks" lands a bar plan (not the general fixture pool), then a SECOND interaction in the same session re-asks the kind question and answering "outdoors" lands a park — pinning the reported state-leak hypothesis as permanently false. Verified: 189 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 25/25 mock e2e.
LIVE (:3200, both reported repros, real Groq/Places): (1) "not sure what to do" at **10:18 PM** → "now" → **The Berczy Tavern, 11:21 PM–12:31 AM** (was: "Couldn't plan that for 11 PM — nothing much is open then"). (2) "sit in a park" at **10:54 PM** → **"It's 11 PM — too late for park today (park around here runs about 6 AM to 10 PM). Try tomorrow, or something else tonight?"** (was: the misleading "Couldn't find a sensible time for this — add one, like 'dinner at 7PM'").

## Fix (batch 4.2): the general/vague pool was daytime-only
Goal: investigation Q4 of the late-night report — "there genuinely ARE things open at 10:18pm, not just parks, but bars, clubs, escape rooms, board game cafes". Confirm whether the general fallback pool is broad enough for late hours, and broaden it if not. (Distinct cause from 4.1's band refusal → separate commit.)
INVESTIGATION (evidence, live against real Places): a vague prompt parses to `category_signals: []`, so `searchPools` ran ONE text search — `"things to do Ossington Toronto"` — which Places answers with DAYTIME TOURIST ATTRACTIONS. Probed at an 11 PM target: 20 results, **15 dropped as "closed at target Thu 23:00"** (Royal Ontario Museum, Casa Loma, CN Tower, Ripley's Aquarium, Art Gallery of Ontario, Toronto Zoo, Harbourfront Centre, Little Canada, Botanical Garden, Ontario Science Centre…), leaving **5 survivors, ALL parks** (Toronto Skyline Viewpoint, Music Garden, Roundhouse Park, Queen's Park, HTO Park) — and they survive only via keep-on-missing hours, not because they're open. Zero bars, zero live music, zero late food ever entered the running. This ALSO explains the reporter's other observation — that "later today" on a vague prompt produced a PARK: the general pool is essentially parks plus closed attractions, so a park is the only thing that can win.
What was done (technical): the general pool is now the UNION of `GENERAL_QUERIES` = ["things to do", "bar", "live music", "late night food", "entertainment"], each run through the SAME `buildQuery` (so aesthetic/constraints/neighbourhood/city shape them identically to any category query), merged and deduped by place id (queries overlap on popular venues). KEY DESIGN CALL: no time-awareness in the query set — the objective hours filter already drops whatever is closed at the resolved instant, so ONE query set self-selects by hour; that keeps the "code does verifiable facts" rule intact and avoids a second time-of-day heuristic to maintain. Cost: 5 Places calls per vague prompt instead of 1 (only for the no-category case; fine at demo scale, noted per the existing cost note in travel.ts). MOCK BLIND SPOT (the one that bit the patio fix): `mockPools` has its own path and does NOT exercise this, so the mock e2e proves nothing here — verified live instead. Tests: +1 searchPlaces case pinning the query set covers both day and night and that each general query builds a properly located/aesthetic-shaped query. Verified: 187 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 24/24 mock e2e. LIVE on :3200 (real Places): the same 11 PM probe now returns **44 survivors including LIBRARY BAR, Bar Poet, Bar Maaya, Earls Kitchen + Bar** — real nightlife — while a 2 PM probe still leads with ROM/Casa Loma/CN Tower/Ripley's (daytime behavior intact, no regression). END-TO-END, the reporter's exact scenario: "not sure what to do" at 10:18 PM → "now" → **The Berczy Tavern, 11:21 PM–12:31 AM (4.6★)** — a real open tavern, where the same flow previously dead-ended on "no options".

## Fix (batch 4.1): late-night band refusals — generic band + the "add a time" lie
Goal: two live reports, INVESTIGATED FIRST and proven to have DIFFERENT proximate causes (so: separate commits, per the brief). (1) 10:18 PM, "not sure what to do" → clarify → "now" → refused. (2) 10:54 PM, "sit in a park" → the exact `IMPLAUSIBLE_TIME_MESSAGE`.
INVESTIGATION (four questions, evidence not assumption). Q1 REPRODUCED both, deterministically via a resolver probe AND live on :3200 with a 22:18 clock override: (1) "now" rounds UP to the next full hour = 23:00; `DEFAULT_PLAUSIBLE_BAND` was 8–23 with an EXCLUSIVE end, so 23:00 failed by a minute of rounding → "Couldn't plan that for 11 PM — nothing much is open then. Try a time between 8 AM and 11 PM?" — refused BEFORE search (no `[schedule-resolve]` line is even emitted). (2) park band 6–22 rejects the inferred 23:00; with no clock/day-part/"now" signal the code takes the generic add-a-time branch → the reporter's exact quote. Q2 STATE LEAKAGE — DISPROVEN, the reporter's hypothesis is wrong: the second run's message reads "between 8 AM and 11 PM", which is the DEFAULT band; a leaked "park" would have produced "6 AM and 10 PM". Each Plan/Replan re-parses fresh and `clarify.parsed` comes from that fresh parse — no state bug, nothing to fix. Q3 PARK BAND = 6–22 (confirmed, not assumed); 22:54 is genuinely outside, and with Toronto's ~9 PM July sunset a 10 PM close is defensible — the band is NOT the bug, the message is. Q4 (in 4.2's entry) — the general pool is genuinely daytime-skewed.
What was done (technical): (a) `DEFAULT_PLAUSIBLE_BAND` 8–23 → **8–1** (wraps midnight; `inBand` already handles wrapping). It applies ONLY where no category matches a band — an unrecognized category or a general/vague request — which is exactly this batch's scope. Every RECOGNIZED category band is untouched, so the explicit guards that matter still fail identically ("brunch at 3am" → 8 AM–3 PM message; "dinner at 4am" → 11 AM–11 PM message — both pinned unchanged). 3 AM stays refused (4 AM slot is outside 8–1). JUDGMENT CALL on 1 AM: "something to do" in a city genuinely runs to last call — bars/clubs/late food are open at 11 PM–1 AM, which is precisely the reporter's point — while 1 AM still refuses the 3–7 AM dead zone. (b) NEW `implausibleInferredReason`: when the user gave NO time and the resolver's OWN inferred slot lands outside a KNOWN category's band, "add a time" is a lie (nothing they type fixes tonight) — so it now names the real obstacle and the real ways out: "It's 11 PM — too late for park today (park around here runs about 6 AM to 10 PM). Try tomorrow, or something else tonight?" (direction-aware: a 4:30 AM park says "too early … Try later today?"). A category with NO band keeps the generic add-a-time message (nothing specific to say). Tests: the previously-flagged "KNOWN INTERACTION (flagged for the ambiguous-prompt work)" assertion — a vague 11:30 PM prompt cannot get an immediate itinerary — is now inverted to `ok: true`, which is this batch's whole point (it was written as a placeholder for exactly this work); +2 schedule cases pinning both repros (22:18 vague "now" → resolves at 23:00, past-midnight to the 1 AM edge works, 3 AM still refused; the 22:54 park inferred-message exact text, the too-early variant, and the no-band → generic fallback); the generic-band message text updated to "8 AM and 1 AM". Verified: 186 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 24/24 mock e2e.

## Fix (batch 3.C-b): a 75-minute "walk" must never displace a similar transit ride
Goal: the other half of batch 3.C (mechanism distinct from C-a's distance-blind pick, hence its own commit): the initial cinema plan presented a 75-minute WALK with no transit, despite the transit-first design. Investigation found TWO code paths that can produce it, one fixable here: (1) `buildLeg`'s walk-competitive relabel had NO ceiling — `walkRaw <= transitRaw + margin` calls a 75-min walk "competitive" against a 72-min transit ride and labels the leg walk (the rule was designed for "a 7-minute walk beats a 13-minute bus", never capped); (2) when Google returns NO transit route at all, the walk route is the only real data and is presented as-is (live: the 905-minute Milton casino walk).
What should be done: cap the relabel so long walks never displace similar transit, with an escape hatch for genuinely broken transit; keep the transit-missing fallback honest (can't invent transit that doesn't exist) — C-a's anchored select is what makes those absurd-distance legs rare.
What was done (technical): new exported `MAX_WALK_LABEL_MIN = 30` in travel.ts; `walkCompetitive` is now `walk <= transit + margin AND (walk <= 30 OR walk*2 <= transit)` — within the cap the old behavior is unchanged (7-min walk still beats the 13-min bus; a 30-min walk at the boundary still relabels), beyond it the leg stays TRANSIT unless walking beats transit outright (twice as fast = transit effectively broken, e.g. a 35-min walk vs a 90-min three-transfer ride). Short-hop (<400m) rule untouched. JUDGMENT CALL: the transit-route-MISSING fallback (path 2) is left as-is — a walk is the only real data there and inventing a transit label would lie; with C-a's home-anchored select, picks far enough to hit it are now rare (documented residual, not papered over). CLAUDE.md's standing transit-policy line updated to state the cap + escape hatch. Tests: +2 travel cases — the mentor's exact shape (walk 75 / transit 72 → TRANSIT with margin; boundary walk-30/transit-28 still relabels) and the escape hatch (walk 35 / transit 90 → walk; walk 35 / transit 45 → transit); existing relabel tests (7–10-min walks) unchanged. Verified: 184 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 24/24 mock e2e; live plan on :3200 still labels sanely (15-min subway home leg, 11-min walk between adjacent stops).

## Fix (batch 3.B + 3.C-a): proximity-aware venue selection — "closer" swaps + home-anchored picks
Goal: two live findings with ONE root cause, so they share this commit (documented choice; 3.C's other mechanism is a separate commit below). (B) "find a closer cinema" kept an equally-far venue — nothing anywhere operationalizes "closer". (C-a) initial picks are distance-blind: selectVenues judges "proximity" from raw lat/lng with NO anchor, so a single-stop plan literally has nothing to be near to — live-proven repeatedly (Imagine Cinemas Promenade in Thornhill for "a movie tonight"; Elements Casino Mohawk ~50 km away for "casino tonight", with a 905-minute walk fallback).
What should be done: make "closer"-style refinements genuinely re-rank by proximity to the right anchor with the original excluded; give the initial select a real distance signal — keeping the architecture rule intact: code computes every distance, the model only judges fit.
What was done (technical): NEW `haversineMeters` in travel.ts (the one code-side distance fact, exported for select + swap). (C-a) `parsed.home` — the plan's geocoded start — is app-injected after geocode (exactly the parsed.city pattern), persists on itinerary.parsed so swap/reroute re-searches inherit the anchor; `selectVenues.candidateView` attaches a CODE-computed `kmFromHome` to every candidate when the anchor is present (absent on legacy plans → no invented distances), and the SYSTEM_PROMPT gained a DISTANCE rule: distance is a real cost, not a tiebreaker — never pick a venue tens of km away when an acceptable option exists much nearer. The LLM judges with a verifiable code-computed fact; it never does arithmetic. (B) swap.ts: a deterministic `CLOSER_PATTERN` (closer/nearer/nearest/closest/close by/nearby/walking distance/walkable — the same floor pattern as the time/duration parsers) detected on the RAW refinement in `venueSwap` (so it applies on both refilter and research paths, however the model classifies); anchor = the previous timed stop's location, else `itinerary.home ?? HOME`; candidates are filtered to strictly nearer than the current venue (original already excluded) and sorted nearest-first, then the model picks fit among genuinely-closer options; nothing nearer → honest refusal "Couldn't find a <category> closer than <venue> — it's already the closest option I can find." Tests: +3 swap cases (distance ranking beats the pool's listed order and lands the nearest; nothing-nearer refusal keeps the original; first-stop swaps anchor to the itinerary's HOME) and +1 select case (parsed.home → every candidate payload carries a positive kmFromHome and the system prompt states the rule; no home → no field). Verified: 182 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 24/24 mock e2e. LIVE on :3200 (real Groq/Places): "a movie tonight" now picks Scotiabank Theatre downtown — a 14-MINUTE WALK (was the Thornhill mall, 70-min transit); "find a closer cinema" swaps it to Cineplex Yonge-Dundas, 12-min walk — a different, strictly-closer venue with a shorter journey (the mentor's exact repro); "casino tonight" composed with 3.A now picks Delta Bingo & Gaming, 59-min TRANSIT, reason citing "one of the closest options to home" (was Elements Casino Mohawk, ~50 km, 905-minute walk).

## Fix (batch 3.A): "casino" search returned a nightclub
Goal: confirmed live — searching "casino" planned a small nightlife club instead of an actual casino. Investigate parse vs search before fixing; check whether real casinos even exist within sensible reach of Toronto.
What should be done: if parse is at fault, fix the prompt; if search, bias the Places request toward Google's real casino place type (the Phase 3 park technique); a genuinely-empty nearby result must flow into the EXISTING Bug 1 recovery machinery, never a mismatched substitute. Tests at the deterministic layer; live-verify on :3200.
What was done (technical): INVESTIGATION — parse is NOT the problem: real Groq keeps `category_signals:["casino"]` distinct. The SEARCH is: the free-text query "casino Toronto" returned a mixed pool where the highest-RATED entries were nightlife lookalikes (Poker Club Toronto 4.9, Mallard Club 4.8, FreePlay Retro Arcade Bar 4.7, jazz lounges 4.4–4.5) while the REAL casinos rate 3.6–4.2 (Casino at Great Canadian Toronto, Pickering Casino Resort, Casino Ajax…) — so select (or the rating fallback) naturally drifts to a club. Real casinos DO exist within reach (Great Canadian at Woodbine is in Toronto), and RATING_FLOOR is 3.5 so they survive the objective filter — the right fix is exactly the park technique. `includedTypeFor` in searchPlaces.ts became a small TYPE_FILTERS table: the existing park pattern plus `/\bcasinos?\b/i` → Places `includedType: "casino"` (a real Places API Table A type) — a HARD type filter, so free-text relevance can't drift into nightlife; the text query itself is unchanged. Empty-nearby needs no new code: an empty filtered pool already flows into the Bug 1 recovery panel (partial) or noVenuesReason (all-empty). Tests: +1 searchPlaces case (casino/casinos/"casino night" → "casino"; nightclub/club/poker club stay unfiltered; query text unchanged). Verified: 178 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 24/24 mock e2e. LIVE on :3200: the same probe now returns an all-casino pool (Great Canadian Toronto, Pickering, Ajax, Rama, Fallsview…) — zero clubs/bars — and the full flow plans a REAL casino (Elements Casino Mohawk, 3.8★, "slot machines… racetrack"). HANDOFF NOTE: that live pick exposed batch 3's B/C root cause perfectly — the select, having no distance anchor, chose a casino ~50 km away (Milton) over the in-city Woodbine one, and the missing transit route fell back to a 905-minute "walk" — fixed next in this batch, not here (this fix's scope is pool integrity).

## Fix (batch 2.B): "dessert with a patio" planned two stops instead of one constrained stop
Goal: confirmed live — a venue-FEATURE modifier attached to a category ("with a patio") became its own category_signals entry, so the app planned a dessert stop AND a separate "patio" stop. The parse SYSTEM_PROMPT's "capture EVERY distinct activity" rule is right for genuinely distinct activities ("dinner then a bar") but too aggressive for feature modifiers — the same category-vs-constraint judgment the prompt already applies to dietary words ("vegan" → constraints), not yet generalized.
What should be done: extend the parse SYSTEM_PROMPT so feature-modifier phrases ("with a patio", "with live music", "with a view", "outdoor seating", "rooftop") land in constraints, attached to the ONE preceding category; don't touch the dietary logic; must not collapse real multi-stop prompts; pin the mock mirror; live-verify one patio-evidenced dessert stop plus regressions.
What was done (technical): SYSTEM_PROMPT's category_signals field doc gained the rule — "A VENUE FEATURE attached to an activity is NOT its own category … the feature goes in constraints, exactly like dietary words do. Only a genuinely distinct activity gets its own entry: 'dinner then a bar' is two." — and the constraints field doc now names venue-feature modifiers alongside dietary/accessibility with examples ("patio", "live music"). No code changes; the fix is entirely the prompt rule, mirroring how the existing dietary distinction is specified. TESTS (same judgment as Phase 3's select rule): a unit test through a stubbed Groq proves nothing about a prompt rule, so the deterministic pin lives on the MOCK MIRROR — fixtures.test.ts gained a case asserting mockParse's contract ("dessert with a patio" → signals ["dessert"] + constraints ["patio"]; "vegan dinner" unchanged; "dinner then a bar" still two signals — mockParse already behaved this way, which is why the mock e2e never showed the live bug; the pin keeps it honest). The REAL rule is live-verified with actual Groq on :3200: "dessert with a patio" → `category_signals:["dessert"], constraints:["patio"]`; the rule generalizes ("a bar with live music" → ["bar"] + ["live music"]); regressions hold ("dinner then a bar" → two categories; "vegan dinner" → ["dinner"] + vegan constraint). Full UI flow live: "dessert with a patio" → ONE stop, Queen Mother Cafe, patio-evidenced in its own Places editorial ("back garden patio") and cited in the labeled reason — no separate patio stop. Verified: 177 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 24/24 mock e2e.

## Fix (batch 2.A): gibberish detection was keyboard-row-only
Goal: `degeneratePromptReason` only caught mash where every 4+-letter word is a literal contiguous substring of a keyboard row — "asdfghjkl" was rejected, but random-character gibberish like "xkjvbz" sailed past into the clarify-questions flow. Broaden detection WITHOUT regressing Phase 2's fix (sincere real-word vague prompts must keep reaching clarify — the opposite failure mode, the one to guard hardest).
What should be done: a low-dependency heuristic for non-row gibberish (vowel-less words, y treated as vowel so "gym"/"myth"/"sync" don't false-positive); no dictionary/wordlist; unit tests for newly-caught gibberish AND still-passing sincere-vague prompts; live-verify both directions on :3200.
What was done (technical): extracted a word-level `gibberishWord(w)` — a 4+-letter token that is a keyboard-row substring OR entirely vowel-less (`/[aeiouy]/` absent) — and the prompt is degenerate when EVERY word is gibberish; one real word anywhere lets the prompt through to the parse (mixed prompts may carry a real request — "dinner xkjvbz" passes, pinned). Same shape as before, one new predicate. JUDGMENT CALLS (documented): (1) y counts as a vowel, so real y-words never trip it (pinned: "gym then dinner", "myth trivia night", "sync up over coffee"); (2) considered and REJECTED consonant-run scoring — runs ≥5 false-positive on real words ("strengths"), ≥6 on obscure-but-real ones ("watchstrap"), and a single-word prompt would be wrongly rejected; (3) no dictionary — heavier than this warrants, per the ask; (4) sub-4-letter vowel-less tokens ("dj", "tv", "tbh", "brr") never count as gibberish alone — too risky. Residual honesty: voweled nonsense ("qwerqwer") still passes — no vowel-free signal to catch it without a wordlist; it falls to the parse/empty-parse guard like before. Tests: +1 planGuards unit case (xkjvbz / qzxwvk / "bcdfg hjklm" / "sdfsdf dfgdfg" / mixed mash+noise all rejected; "dinner xkjvbz" passes) and hardened the not-degenerate case with the y-words plus ALL the Phase-2 sincere-vague pins ("not sure what to do", "no idea, surprise me", "something fun I guess", "bored, help", "whatever works tbh"); +1 fail-loud e2e case ("xkjvbz qzwvk" → exact UNPARSEABLE text). Verified: 176 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 24/24 mock e2e. LIVE on :3200: "xkjvbz qzwvk" and "bcdfgs hjklmn zzxwv" both rejected with the unparseable message; "not sure what to do" still reaches the clarify questions.

## Fix (batch 1.3): stop card — reasoning text where the description should be
Goal: mentor screenshot after a cinema swap showed justification language ("…perfect for a night out at the movies") in the card's description area. Per architecture, `description` (Places editorialSummary, factual) and `reason` (Groq pick-justification) are separate fields and the card must never present a reason as a description. Instruction: investigate the actual cause before fixing — hypothesis was that swap-produced stops drop `description`.
What should be done: confirm whether swap paths (buildStop/finalize/findReplacement), reroute adapt, and initial select actually populate `description` from the candidate's editorialSummary; fix whatever the real cause is; a swap test pinning description ≠ reason; live-verify the exact cinema-swap scenario on :3200.
What was done (technical): INVESTIGATION FIRST — the hypothesis was DISPROVEN. Code audit: every `description:` assignment in the app sources `editorialSummary` (selectVenues valid-pick + fallback branches, swap's buildStop picked branch + kept passthrough, swap's finalize; reroute carries it via Selection spread through `buildSchedule`'s `{...sel}`); the strip renders `stop.description` with NO reason fallback, and the map card never renders reason at all. Live proof on :3200: an initial "a movie tonight" pick (Imagine Cinemas Promenade) stored `description: null` because Places genuinely has NO editorialSummary for it (verified in the fresh pool response: 2 of 17 cinema candidates lack one), and a "find a closer cinema" constraint-swap produced Fox Theatre carrying its real editorial as `description`, distinct from its `reason` — the pipeline is faithful end to end. ACTUAL CAUSE: on a venue with no Places editorial, the description line is legitimately absent (keep-on-missing) and the SELECTED card's only prose is the Groq reason — 13.5px ink-soft body text in the same slot area, and Groq writes reasons in a description-like voice, so it reads as a (bad) description. THE FIX (presentational, honest): the reason line is now labeled — a small "why here" eyebrow (`.lstrip__why`, mechanical grot small-caps like the category eyebrow) renders above the reason text inside `.lstrip__reason`, so justification can never masquerade as fact; the description slot still renders ONLY `description` and stays empty when Places has none (no invented copy). Tests: +1 swap unit case (mkVenue fixtures now carry an editorialSummary; a "somewhere cheaper" venue swap stores the pick's editorial as `description` ≠ `reason`, and the time-shift ADAPT path's replacement venue does too — pinning finalize AND buildStop); the description e2e gained the mentor-shape assertion (select the deliberately description-less Sundown Scoops → the "why here" label renders, the desc slot stays empty). Verified: 175 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 23/23 mock e2e. LIVE on :3200, the exact scenario: "a movie tonight" → Fox Theatre card shows the factual editorial line, then WHY HERE + the Groq reason; "find a closer cinema" → The Royal, real editorial ("Refurbished 1939 cinema…") + labeled reason — no unlabeled justification anywhere.

## Fix (batch 1.2): recovered category restored to its position in the prompt
Goal: flagged as a known gap in Bug 1's own DEVLOG entry, then confirmed live — "ramen then a quiet bar" through the widen-recovery flow rendered BAR then RAMEN. Root cause as documented there: the real `selectVenues` answers empty-pool categories without the LLM and appends them LAST (`[...selections, ...emptySelections]`); the recovery flow resolves the empty in that appended position and `finishPipeline` built the schedule in whatever order the array happened to have.
What should be done: before building the final schedule, reorder the resolved selections to match the ORIGINAL `category_signals` order from the parse — whether the category resolved normally, via widen, or via the decline/follow-up replacement (which changes the category name, so the replacement must inherit the replaced slot's position). Test the FIRST-category-empty case specifically; live-verify ramen-before-bar on :3200.
What was done (technical): pure `orderByRequest(selections, categorySignals, slots)` in `planGuards.ts` (stable sort by the signal index; a `slots` map sends a replacement category to the requested category whose slot it fills; categories not in the request — "general", legacy — sort after the known ones keeping relative order; no signals → untouched). `page.tsx`: `PlanCtx` gained `slots: Record<string,string>` (empty on creation); `resolveEmpty`'s merge records `slots[searchCategory] = slots[category] ?? category` when the replace path changes the category; `finishPipeline` now runs `orderByRequest(ctx.sels, parseData.category_signals, ctx.slots)` before building points/schedule/store — one place, covering the normal path (no-op: already in order), widen, and replace. FIXTURE-FIDELITY BUG FOUND BY THE TEST DISCIPLINE: with the fix temporarily reverted, the new e2e order assertions still PASSED — because `mockSelect` pushed empty categories IN PLACE (pool order) instead of appending them last like the real `selectVenues`, so mock mode could never reproduce the live misordering. Fixed `mockSelect` to mirror the real contract (empties collected and appended last — the seam swaps data, it must not quietly swap CONTRACT SHAPE); after that, revert-run fails exactly the two order assertions (bar rendered first), fix-run passes 5/5 — the tests now genuinely discriminate. Tests: +1 planGuards unit case (recovered-first restored; replacement inherits slot incl. a 3-category middle-replacement; unknown categories after known; vague no-op; normal path no-op) and order assertions on both recovery e2e scenarios (single widen → Citywide Dumpling Bar FIRST; two-empty → dumplings first / dessert-replacement in bao's middle slot / bar last), scoped to `.lstrip__stop .lstrip__name` (the first name overall is the home card). Verified: 174 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 23/23 mock e2e. LIVE on :3200: "ramen then a quiet bar in Ossington" → widen → **RAMEN Ikkousha Ramen Toronto 7:09 PM first, BAR 222 Bar 9:25 PM second** — prompt order restored (pre-fix this rendered bar-then-ramen).

## Fix (batch 1.1): contradiction messages dropped the dead-end "Which matters more?"
Goal: both contradiction messages — the original cheap/fancy `CONTRADICTION_MESSAGE` and Bug 2's dynamic dietary-vs-venue named-pair one — ended with "Which matters more?", a question the app has no mechanism to hear an answer to. A question the UI can't act on is worse than not asking; mentor-style live testing flagged it as a dead end.
What should be done: remove the trailing question from both messages, keep the part that names the actual contradiction; deliberately do NOT add a follow-up mechanism (the ask is removal, not replacement); update every test pinning the exact old copy.
What was done (technical): `planGuards.ts` — `CONTRADICTION_MESSAGE` is now "That's a bit contradictory — cheap and fancy pull opposite ways." and the dietary branch returns "That's a bit contradictory — <diet> and <venue> pull opposite ways." (no trailing question in either). Searched the whole repo for the old string: five pinned literals updated — `failloud.spec.ts` ("cheap fancy dinner", "vegan steakhouse") and `planGuards.test.ts` (vegan/steakhouse, vegetarian/bbq, kosher/pork); the other planGuards assertions compare against the exported constant so they follow automatically. The two remaining occurrences are historical records (the Bug 2 DEVLOG entry and the QA sweep report) and were left as documentation of what shipped at the time. No behavior change beyond the copy. Verified: 173 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 23/23 mock e2e.

## Tests (QA follow-up): multi-empty recovery e2e + restored unmet-constraint e2e + README fix
Goal: verification after the Bug 1/Bug 2 commits found two coverage gaps and one doc-drift. (1) The two-empty-categories case was unit-tested (partialEmptyCategories) but the interactive multi-empty recovery flow — resolve one, panel persists for the next, plan finishes only when all are resolved — had no e2e. (2) Bug 2 retargeted the only e2e that reached the page-level unmetConstraintReason wiring (the old "vegan steakhouse" test now asserts the contradiction message), leaving that path e2e-uncovered even though selectVenues' deeper logic stayed unit-covered in select.test.ts. (3) e2e/README.md still described "vegan steakhouse" as the unmet-constraint example.
What should be done: an e2e exercising TWO empty categories in one request (resolve the first, panel shows the second, plan completes only after both — mixing widen and the replace follow-up); an e2e reaching unmetConstraintReason via a prompt that can't trip the new contradiction guard; update the stale README lines and note where unmet-constraint coverage now lives. No behavior changes.
What was done (technical): FIXTURE (data-only, same seam discipline): a second neighbourhood-sensitive recovery trigger "bao" (`BAO_CLOSED` in-neighbourhood / `BAO_OPEN` hours-less city-wide, mirroring dumplings) + its mockParse signal — one trigger can't produce two distinct empty categories. New recovery.spec.ts case "TWO empty categories → both surface; plan finishes only after BOTH resolve": "dumplings and bao then a bar at 7pm in Ossington" → panel lists BOTH reasons (bar resolves normally); widening dumplings leaves the panel showing only bao with NO strip rendered (the plan must not finish half-recovered); replacing bao with "dessert" completes a 3-stop plan (bar + Citywide Dumpling Bar + dessert) — so widen AND the replace follow-up are both exercised in the multi-empty flow. New failloud.spec.ts case "dessert with a patio at 8pm" → the exact unmetConstraintReason text: "patio" is a non-dietary constraint (nothing matches the dietary/venue-type patterns, so the contradiction guard stays silent by construction, not by luck) and no dessert fixture carries patio evidence (it lives only on The Standing Room, a bar), so the prompt flows through select's id:null + unmetConstraint into the page-level message; also asserts the recovery panel does NOT appear (unmet ≠ empty pool). README: the "vegan steakhouse" bullet now documents it as the contradiction-guard example (named-pair message, pre-search), a new bullet documents the patio-dessert unmet example, the fixtures section documents the dumplings/bao recovery triggers, and the Files list gains the (previously undocumented) recovery.spec.ts. FLAGGED WHILE DESIGNING THE TEST (not fixed — out of scope for a test-only commit): a weather-blocked category in a MIXED request (e.g. "dinner and a park walk at 3pm") also produces an empty pool beside a real pick, so it would enter the recovery panel with the generic drop-less reason ("Couldn't find any park walk near X") instead of the weather reason — and widening can't help since weather applies city-wide; recovery should probably exclude (or reword for) weather-blocked categories. Verified: 173 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 23/23 mock e2e (21 + the 2 new).

## Fix (QA Bug 2): "vegan steakhouse" gets refused, not planned
Goal: QA Scenario 9 — "vegan steakhouse" planned a real (genuinely vegan) restaurant under a "vegan steakhouse" label instead of the honest refusal the mocked contract promises. The parse correctly treats "vegan" as a hard constraint (evidenced + enforced by selectVenues' hedge-guard) but treats "steakhouse" as a mere category/search term, so the contradiction BETWEEN them is never evaluated by anything.
What should be done: extend `contradictionReason` in planGuards.ts — the guard that already catches "cheap fancy dinner" — with a dietary-vs-venue-type check, same shape as the CHEAP/FANCY pair; when a hard diet and an obviously-incompatible venue type both fire, return a contradiction message in the same voice, naming the actual pair; guard against false positives (mixed-group "vegan options at a steakhouse") the way the existing check handles "nothing fancy".
What was done (technical): a `DIETARY_VENUE_CONFLICTS` table of `[dietRegex, incompatibleVenueRegex]` pairs — crucially PER dietary term, because the incompatibility depends on the diet: vegan/vegetarian/plant-based clash with meat-/seafood-defined venues (steakhouse, chophouse, butcher, bbq, churrascaria, smokehouse, rib house, meatery, seafood/oyster/raw bar/fish house); halal/kosher clash with pork-/shellfish-defined venues (pork, hog, bacon, pig roast, oyster/raw bar, shellfish, lobster/crab/clam). JUDGMENT CALLS (documented): (1) BBQ is in the plant-based row but NOT the halal/kosher row — halal/kosher BBQ is common, vegan BBQ is a contradiction of the venue's identity; (2) "gluten-free" is a recognized diet but has NO incompatible-venue row — a gluten-free steakhouse is fine, there's no obvious venue-type it contradicts, so it stays satisfiable rather than inventing false clashes; (3) false-positive guard: a `DIETARY_ACCOMMODATION` regex strips "<diet> options / <diet>-friendly / a <diet> friend / <diet> menu" before the dietary match, so mixed-group asks ("vegan options at a steakhouse", "steakhouse with a vegan friend") are NOT flagged, while a whole-venue ask ("vegan steakhouse") carries no such qualifier and still fires — the same spirit as NEGATED_FANCY. `category_signals` was added to the guard's haystack so a parse-derived venue type is caught even if phrased oddly. The message is dynamic and names the matched pair: "That's a bit contradictory — vegan and steakhouse pull opposite ways. Which matters more?" — same voice/shape as CONTRADICTION_MESSAGE. Guard order is unchanged (contradiction runs before clarify/search/select), so the refusal happens up front, never after a hedged pick. Tests: +3 planGuards unit cases (the exact Scenario-9 parse → contradiction; other obvious clashes named correctly; the Scenario-10 regression — "vegan dinner"/"vegan cafe"/"vegetarian ramen" and the per-diet non-clashes "halal steakhouse"/"gluten-free steakhouse"/"kosher bbq" all stay null; accommodation phrasing stays null). Updated the existing `@mock` "vegan steakhouse" e2e to assert the new contradiction message (it now fails at the contradiction guard, earlier than the old unmet-constraint path — the intended behavior change). 173 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 21/21 mock e2e. LIVE on :3200: "vegan steakhouse" → "That's a bit contradictory — vegan and steakhouse pull opposite ways. Which matters more?" (refused, no plan); "vegan dinner" still passes the guard (reaches clarify, then plans a vegan pick) — no false positive.

## Fix (QA Bug 1): silent category drop → interactive recovery
Goal: QA Scenario 1 ("ramen then a quiet bar in Ossington") returned a ONE-stop plan (just the bar). Ramen's pool came back empty after the objective filter (the only nearby match was permanently closed) and the category was dropped with no banner, no note — silent by construction. Turn that into an honest, general recovery flow for ANY category that comes back empty while others resolve.
What should be done: detect the PARTIAL failure (some real picks + ≥1 empty pool) after select, before the plan is built; name the honest reason from the objective drop log (permanently closed / closed then / low-rated / over budget); offer to widen the search city-wide for that ONE category (reuse searchPools' categoriesOverride, drop the neighbourhood, leave the rest of the plan untouched); if declined, a clarify-style follow-up ("what would you like there instead?") re-resolves just that slot; apply per-category (only the empty ones), never a ramen-specific special case; keep the ALL-empty case on its existing noVenuesReason path.
What was done (technical): pure helpers in `planGuards.ts` (the fail-loud surface, so the voice matches): `partialEmptyCategories(selections)` (returns the empty categories ONLY when ≥1 real pick coexists — all-empty and none-empty both return [], so the all-empty net keeps noVenuesReason; unmet-constraint null picks are excluded, they're a different, harder failure), `emptyCategoryReason(category, drops, location)` (dominant objective drop rule → "the only one nearby is permanently closed", etc.; no-drops → softer "Couldn't find any X near <loc>"), `widenOfferLabel(location)`. The places route gained an optional `categoriesOverride` body param threaded to `searchPools`/`mockPools` — the SAME subset-search the reroute engine already uses (no forked search path). In `page.tsx`, `continuePipeline` now captures the drop log, and its build-and-store tail was extracted into a reusable `finishPipeline(ctx)` (a `PlanCtx` snapshots parse/zone/home/weather/pools/sels/drops); on a partial failure it pauses into a new `recovery` state instead of building. `resolveEmpty(category, {searchCategory, dropLocation})` re-searches ONLY that category (widen = drop neighbourhood + same category; replace = keep neighbourhood + the user's new category) via categoriesOverride + `/api/select`, merges the recovered pick back into the SAME sels array position and pools, and either finishes the plan (all recovered) or keeps the panel; "Plan without it" builds without the empty slot (the old behaviour, now an explicit choice). The recovery panel reuses the clarify visual styling with its own `.recover*` hooks. JUDGMENT CALLS (documented, not silent): (1) widen is exactly ONE step wider — drop the neighbourhood, stay in the same city — never an open-ended radius negotiation; (2) the recovered category takes the position `selectVenues` assigned it (empty categories are appended last), so a recovered FIRST-listed category (ramen) lands after the others (the bar) — a real but pre-existing ordering quirk; restoring the exact requested order would mean reordering inside selectVenues, out of this fix's scope; (3) the recovered category isn't written back into the stored `parsed.category_signals`/`location` (kept minimal) — swap/reroute key off each stop's OWN category, so it's inconsequential. Mock fixture: a neighbourhood-aware "dumplings" trigger (`DUMPLING_CLOSED` in a neighbourhood → filter empties it with a businessStatus drop; `DUMPLING_OPEN`, hours-less/keep-on-missing, city-wide) makes the whole flow reproducible + deterministic; `mockPools` now takes the parsed so it can react to the neighbourhood the way real Places does. Tests: +5 planGuards unit cases (partial vs all-empty vs none; unmet excluded; reason phrasing per drop rule; widen label) and a new `e2e/recovery.spec.ts` (+4 @mock: honest reason + widen offer not a silent plan; accept widen re-searches only that category city-wide and recovers the slot with the bar untouched; decline → follow-up replace; all-empty still uses the plain fail-loud message). 170 unit tests across 15 suites green, tsc clean, 21/21 mock e2e. LIVE on :3200 with Scenario 1's exact prompt: instead of the silent one-stop plan, the panel read "Couldn't find any ramen open near Ossington — the only one nearby is permanently closed." with a "Look further than Ossington" offer; accepting it re-searched only `categories=["ramen"]` (log) and completed the plan with a real city-wide ramen spot (AFURI ramen + dumpling Toronto, 4.6★) alongside the untouched bar.

## Follow-up (Phase 5): pin the timeChange zone fix + live-verify editing on a non-Toronto plan
Goal: two gaps left open when Phase 5 shipped. (1) The multi-city bug fixed inline in Phase 5 — timeChange's absolute "to 6pm" set the SERVER's wall hour instead of the plan zone's — never got its own regression test, though every other fix in this project ships with one pinning the exact bug. (2) Phase 5's live verification proved plan CREATION works in a non-Toronto zone but never exercised EDITING (swap/time-swap/reroute) on such a plan, even though that code was all made zone-aware.
What was done (technical): NO source change — both the fix and the editing code were already correct; this is test + verification only, nothing folded into any earlier commit. (1) New `swap.test.ts` case "MULTI-CITY: an absolute time-swap lands 6pm in the PLAN's zone, never Toronto's": a Vancouver-zoned itinerary (`createItinerary(..., "America/Vancouver")`, stops in Pacific wall time via a new `V(h,m)` = −07:00 helper alongside the existing Toronto `T(h,m)` = −04:00), "move it to 6pm" must land `start_time` at exactly `2026-07-03T18:00:00-07:00` — asserted as the string AND that its epoch ≠ the Toronto-6pm epoch (the two differ by 3h, so the assertion actually discriminates), plus the kept 105-min duration ending 7:45 PM Pacific. PROVEN to catch the bug: temporarily reverting the fix to `nd.setHours(h,m,0,0)` made ONLY this new case fail (33/34) while the pre-existing Toronto "move it to 6pm" case still passed — which is precisely why the bug slipped through originally (a Toronto-server test can't see a server-vs-plan-zone divergence); fix restored, 34/34. (2) LIVE on :3200, a real Vancouver "dinner then drinks" plan (log `zone=America/Vancouver`, "now" → dinner anchored 9:16 PM PDT, real venues Chambar → The 515 Bar) exercised all three editing paths, every result in Pacific and confirmed in both the `[swap-apply]`/`[reroute-apply]` logs and the rendered strip: VENUE swap ("somewhere higher rated") → Chambar→Glowbal 4.6★, slot held (`after start=…21:16:00-07:00`, display 9:16 PM); TIME swap ("move it to 10pm") → `after start=…22:00:00-07:00`, display "10:00 PM" — NOT the 7:00 PM a server-zone bug would render on the Toronto box — drinks reflowed to 11:50 PM; REROUTE (cancel the dinner→drinks leg) → dinner's committed 10:00 PM PRESERVED (`after: dinner@…22:00:00-07:00`, the Phase-1 reroute floor holding in Pacific), drinks re-planned (The Boxcar, walk 20 min) to "tomorrow, 12:05 AM" with the "tomorrow" prefix correctly computed in the PLAN's zone (12:05 AM is past midnight in Pacific, still same evening for the viewer). No new bug found. Verified: 165 unit tests across 15 suites green (swap 34), tsc clean, 17/17 mock e2e. Port 3000 untouched; all live work on :3200; committed locally, not pushed.

## Feature (Phase 5): real per-city timezone — every city schedules and displays in its own zone
Goal: close Phase 4's flagged limitation — a Vancouver plan resolved "lunch" against Toronto's clock and labeled stops in Toronto's zone, so a plausible-looking afternoon itinerary was actually morning in Vancouver (the same class of bug as Phase 1, reopened by Phase 4's scope). Highest-risk phase: resolveStartTime/buildSchedule/toTorontoISO/the filter day-of-week sit under parse, filter, schedule, swap, reroute, and every display surface.
What should be done: resolve a real IANA zone per plan (offline lat/lng lookup preferred over a new Google API); persist it on the itinerary (like Phase 4's parsed.city); replace server-local Date math with zone-aware math in schedule.ts (resolve/rollForward/day-part/category/toZonedISO), filter.ts (hours target + weather-gate day/hour, esp. day-of-week), timeLabels.ts (render in the plan's zone, not viewer's, not always-Toronto), and the dev ?now= sim; keep Toronto byte-identical; new non-Toronto tests independent of server AND viewer TZ; confirm forecastAt is already epoch-safe; confirm travel legs are unaffected.
What was done (technical): DEPENDENCY CALL (justified) — `tz-lookup` (~150KB unpacked; geo-tz measured at 72MB unpacked, over Vercel's serverless limit, rejected) for offline lat/lng→IANA, and `luxon` (~4.6MB) for zone math rather than extending the hand-rolled Intl.DateTimeFormat parsing that already caused the meridiem/TZ bugs in this log. Both offline, no new API key, no Cloud Console step. New pure `app/lib/zoneTime.ts` wraps them: `zoneFromLatLng` (never throws → default zone), `normalizeZone`, `toZonedISO` (byte-verified identical to the old Toronto formatter across DST), `wallClockParts` (day/hour/minute/**weekday 0=Sun**), `nextFullHourInZone`, `instantAtWallClock` (set a wall hour in a zone, DST-safe roll-forward) — 6 tests, run under TZ=UTC and Asia/Kolkata too. ONE FILE AT A TIME with a byte-compat check after each: (5b) schedule.ts — resolveStartTime/Checked/buildSchedule/isPlausibleAt/inBand/clock reasons take a `timeZone` param (default Toronto) and use the helpers; `toTorontoISO` kept as a `toZonedISO(d, DEFAULT)` alias for un-threaded callers. (5c) filter.ts — the hours `target` and weather-gate hour come from `wallClockParts(startInstant, zone)`; **the byte-compat bar caught a real bug here**: I destructured `day` (day-of-month, e.g. 12) where `TargetTime.day` is the day-of-WEEK, producing "closed at target undefined" and dropping every venue — fixed to `weekday`; confirmed forecastAt compares absolute epochs (zone-safe, untouched). (5d) timeLabels.ts — every label renders in the plan's zone via luxon `toFormat` (byte-verified "7:00 PM"/"Fri Jul 3" incl. the space char); this is the precise middle ground between Phase 1's bug (viewer zone) and Phase 4's bug (always Toronto). (5e) persistence + wiring — `itinerary.timeZone` stored (createItinerary param, POST body); `/api/geocode` returns `zoneFromLatLng(coords)`; page.tsx geocodes FIRST (the plausibility check needs the zone), threads `planZone` into places/search (body), buildSchedule, storeItinerary, and every display formatter (strip + map get a `timeZone` prop); swap.ts and reroute.ts read `itinerary.timeZone` and thread it to buildStop/findReplacement/filterPools + all `toZonedISO`. REAL MULTI-CITY BUG beyond formatting: timeChange's absolute "move it to 6pm" used `nd.setHours()` (server wall clock) — a Vancouver plan would set Toronto 6pm; fixed to `instantAtWallClock(nd, tz, h, m)`. Fail-soft: an unresolvable zone defaults to Toronto but logs `[timezone]` + could banner (never silent, never blocking). TRAVEL LEGS confirmed unaffected (Routes API works off lat/lng + absolute instants). Tests (+4, all server/viewer-TZ-independent via explicit instants+zones): schedule "lunch now" resolving to a LOCAL noon per city (Toronto rolls to tomorrow, Vancouver stays today) + band judged in the plan zone; timeLabels a stop rendering 3 PM Toronto vs 12 PM Vancouver for the SAME instant (both bugs closed, run under TZ=UTC); filter day-of-week edge — a Sat-only venue at one instant is OPEN for a Tokyo plan (Saturday there) and CLOSED for a Toronto plan (still Friday), via targetOverride to isolate the lookup. Verified: 164 unit tests across 15 suites green on the Toronto dev machine (the byte-compat bar — ZERO existing Toronto expectations changed), tsc clean, 17/17 mock e2e. LIVE on :3200: a "lunch" in Vancouver (real time 3:36 PM PDT / 6:36 PM EDT) → "tomorrow, 12:04 PM" = noon PACIFIC (log: `zone=America/Vancouver resolved=2026-07-12T19:00:00Z`), real Vancouver venue (JOEY Burrard) filtered on Vancouver's hours — NOT the 3 PM Toronto-clock render the old code gave; and a Toronto "dinner and drinks" still renders Toronto-local 7:17 PM (log `zone=America/Toronto`), Phase-1 fix intact. Pre-existing note: the schedule suite's OLD Toronto tests use server-local `new Date(2026,…)` fixtures and so only pass on a Toronto machine (measured 22/26 under TZ=UTC BEFORE this phase) — unchanged policy, not touched; the byte-compat bar is the Toronto dev machine, where it's 28/28.

## Feature (Phase 4): multi-city support — city + starting-address inputs
Goal: location was hardcoded in four places (buildQuery's "Toronto" literal, the weather route's Ossington coords, HOME's Chestnut Residence constant, the parse prompt's "Ossington" default) plus a CLAUDE.md policy saying not to build location plumbing. Scope: plain query inputs (city + starting address) — explicitly NOT geolocation/location services.
What should be done: two inputs on the empty state; a geocoding step turning both into lat/lng; parse's location field becomes neighbourhood-within-city; buildQuery uses the user's city; weather takes per-request coords; HOME becomes per-request; CLAUDE.md rewritten; tests proving a second city end to end; live-verify a non-Toronto plan.
What was done (technical): city (prefilled "Toronto" — visible and editable, never a silent fallback) + optional starting address inputs on the empty state; Plan disabled without a city. GEOCODING (JUDGMENT CALL): reused the Places API Text Search via new `POST /api/geocode` (label + lat/lng from the top result) instead of adding the separate Geocoding API — the spec offered both; reuse means NO new key/external dependency (.env.example + DEPLOY.md note the Places key now serves both; a failed geocode fails loud with "check the spelling?"). Mock seam #8: `mockGeocode` returns fixed Chestnut coords for ANY query, keeping mock travel legs byte-stable (CLAUDE.md seam count updated). CITY PLUMBING (design): `parsed.city` — the app injects the user's city into the parse output right after /api/parse, so it persists on `itinerary.parsed` and swap/reroute re-searches inherit it with ZERO engine/dep signature changes; buildQuery = [aesthetic, constraints, category, neighbourhood, city] with a Toronto fallback ONLY for pre-multi-city itineraries (documented). Parse SYSTEM_PROMPT: location = neighbourhood WITHIN the city ("west end"), "" if none, never a city name. Weather: `GET /api/weather?lat=&lng=` (validated floats; absent → the old Ossington anchor for the pre-plan ambient chip); the pipeline fetches the geocoded location's forecast and updates the chip. HOME: `itinerary.home` (`HomePoint` = geocoded address, or "<city> centre" when the address is blank) stored per plan; travel leg 0, the map/strip home cards, and swap's two home-origin fallbacks all read `itinerary.home ?? HOME` — the Chestnut constant survives ONLY as the pre-multi-city/tests default. Tests (+5): buildQuery city cases (Vancouver/west end; legacy no-city → Toronto; empty neighbourhood drops), mockGeocode determinism, createItinerary home persistence, and a swap test proving a time-swap on stop 0 departs from the ITINERARY's custom home (custom-coords origin captured via injected getSingleLeg). Existing fixtures keep location:"Ossington" as valid default-case fixtures per spec. E2E FOR A SECOND CITY (JUDGMENT CALL): mockGeocode is deliberately city-agnostic, so a mock second-city scenario would prove only plumbing — skipped in favour of unit tests on the query shape + the live verification; flagged as follow-up in CLAUDE.md open gaps. Verified: 154 unit tests across 14 suites, 17/17 mock e2e (prefilled Toronto keeps every existing flow byte-compatible), tsc clean. LIVE on :3200 — city "Vancouver", address "800 Robson St", prompt "coffee and a walk in the park this afternoon": home card "Start · 800 Robson St / leave by 2:00 PM", weather chip 17° Light rain (Vancouver's forecast), Nine Bar Coffee (real Vancouver café, 4.8★) → Stanley Park (4.8★, "Free" — Phase 3 composing with Phase 4), nothing anchored to Ossington/Toronto. KNOWN LIMITATION (recorded in CLAUDE.md open gaps): the CLOCK is still Toronto's — scheduling resolves and labels render in America/Toronto (Phase 1's fix), so a Vancouver plan is scheduled/displayed in Eastern time; per-city timezones need a tz lookup on the geocode result — future work, out of this phase's plain-inputs scope.

## Feature (Phase 3): passive-outdoor prompts land on real parks, not commercial venues
Goal: "sit on a bench and enjoy quiet scenery" returned patio bars / scenic restaurants. The pipeline already understood parks (isOutdoorCategory, the durations park resolver) — the parse never emitted the word "park" for this phrasing, and the search had no way to keep "scenic" commercial venues out of the pool.
What should be done: a parse-prompt normalization rule (passive outdoor/nature phrasing → category "park"); park-biased Places search preferring a real type filter over free-text keywords; a select rule favouring genuine public parks over commercial venues with a view, noting free entry when price is null; optional "Free" price label on park stops if quick; tests for normalization, query shape, and the select preference; live-verify end to end.
What was done (technical): parse SYSTEM_PROMPT gained the normalization rule (bench-sitting, quiet scenery, greenery, fresh air, people-watching outside, "somewhere calm outside" → "park", never a cafe/bar with a view), mirrored deterministically in mockParse (kept distinct from the existing "park walk" weather-gate rule). searchPlaces: new exported `includedTypeFor(category)` — green-space categories (park/trail/garden/green space/beach/bench/stroll/hike/\bwalk\b) get Places API (New) `includedType: "park"` on the searchText body, a HARD type filter (the API supports it directly — chosen over appending "public park" free text precisely so a "scenic lounge" cannot leak in); commercial categories unchanged. selectVenues SYSTEM_PROMPT: parks/outdoor-relaxation rule — highest-rated genuine public park or notable scenic spot beats any commercial "scenic" venue, and a null-price park pick may say it's free. UI (quick + low-risk, done): a park-category stop with no priceLevel shows "Free" in the facts row via `resolveCategory(category) === "park"` — keep-on-missing stays intact for every non-park category (unknown price still renders nothing). DISCOVERED GAP fixed en route: the SCHEDULER didn't know parks — no CATEGORY_START_DEFAULT and no plausible band, so a no-time park prompt anchored next-full-hour and then failed the generic 8–23 band off-hours (live repro at 5 AM refused with "add a time"). Parks got their own PLAUSIBLE_BANDS entry (6–22, dawn-to-dusk) and deliberately NO start default — a bench-sit is an anytime-daylight activity, so the immediate next-full-hour anchor is right (JUDGMENT CALL: a 14:00 "typical park time" default would have rolled a 3 PM request to TOMORROW 2 PM, which is worse than "go now"); midnight park prompts still refuse honestly. Tests: fixtures.test.ts (new — pins the mockParse normalization contract: bench/scenery → "park", "a walk in the park" stays "park walk", food prompts untouched), searchPlaces.test (includedTypeFor shapes: park/park walk/garden/quiet trail → "park"; bar/dinner/boardwalk-cafe → undefined; park text query unchanged), schedule.test (park band: 5 AM → 6:00 passes, midday passes, 11:30 PM refuses). JUDGMENT CALL on the requested select test: a unit test of "park beats commercial scenic venue" through the STUBBED Groq proves nothing about the prompt rule (the stub returns whatever it's told), so the select preference is pinned by the live verification instead — deterministic coverage lives at the parse/query/schedule layers. Verified: 149→153 unit tests green across 14 suites, 17/17 mock e2e, tsc clean; live on :3200 "sit on a bench and enjoy quiet scenery" → category PARK → Philosopher's Walk (genuine public footpath, 4.7★), "Free" label, 6:18 AM immediate anchor, park-appropriate description and reason — no commercial venue anywhere in sight.

## Feature (Phase 2): vague prompts get a real itinerary + lightweight clarifying questions
Goal: "not sure what to do" was rejected as gibberish (emptyParseReason treated any all-unspecified parse as unparseable); and thin prompts ran straight to search instead of asking 1–2 cheap questions first.
What should be done: emptyParseReason only fails a prompt that ALSO looks degenerate (real-words uncertainty falls through to the already-existing-but-unreachable general "things to do" pool in searchPools, anchored at now); a pure rule-based clarify module (no LLM — deterministic field checks per the architecture rule) asking "When?" when no time signal and a vibe question when aesthetic+group+constraints are all unspecified, skipped when a category plus a time or aesthetic already exist; wire inline into runPipeline after parse, before search; answering or skipping continues the pipeline.
What was done (technical): `emptyParseReason(parsed, prompt)` now returns `degeneratePromptReason(prompt)` only when the parse is empty (new `isEmptyParse` export) — gibberish still fails both-sides-tested, sincere-vague returns null and the pipeline reaches `searchPools`' general pool (query "things to do <location> Toronto" — not food-biased; live run surfaced a non-restaurant pick). New `app/lib/clarify.ts`: `clarifyQuestions` (the three rules above, max 2 questions) + `timeWindowForWhenAnswer` ("now" → literal "now"; "later today" → "evening" — JUDGMENT CALL: reuses the existing day-part machinery and rolls forward past 7 PM, rather than inventing a now+3h rule; free text passes through). `resolveStartTime` gained an explicit `now` branch (next full hour, overriding category defaults) so a "now" answer anchors immediately. runPipeline split: guards → clarify decision → `continuePipeline(parsed)` (band check onward); answers merge into the parse (`time_window`/`aesthetic`) before resuming; Skip resumes unchanged. Inline UI reuses the card/chip design tokens, rendered on both the empty state and the map stage. THE PHASE-1 FLAGGED INTERACTION, resolved for the explicit case: answering "now" late at night used to hit the generic "add a time" message (wrong branch — the user JUST gave a time); `resolveStartTimeChecked` now treats explicit "now" like a stated time and returns the specific band message — live-proven at 4:53 AM: "Couldn't plan that for 5 AM — nothing much is open then. Try a time between 8 AM and 11 PM?" (a fully-unspecified vague prompt at ~11:30 PM still gets the generic refusal — pinned in tests, acceptable). e2e: helpers auto-skip the clarify step (`dismissClarifyIfShown`) so every existing spec now exercises the skip path at any hour; the new scenario answers "later today" instead of Skip — JUDGMENT CALL: skipping anchors next-full-hour, which would make the spec flaky between 11 PM and 8 AM, while "later today"→evening is deterministic at any run hour. Tests: planGuards 8 (both sides: gibberish still fails, "not sure what to do"/"no idea, surprise me" pass), clarify 7 (each trigger + both skip conditions + answer mapping), schedule 25 (the "now" contract incl. the 3 AM specific-message case), 17/17 mock e2e (new: clarify → answer → "Fixture General One" itinerary + desync check). Live on :3200 (real Groq): "not sure what to do" → both questions rendered → "later today"+"quiet" → real general-pool itinerary (Neon Tiger, 7:29 PM), no rejection anywhere. NOTE: one confusing stale-bundle episode during verification (old client chunk served the pre-fix message after an edit; a clean server restart + recompile cleared it) — same failure mode this repo hit before, worth remembering before trusting a live disproof.

## Fix (Phase 1): midnight-for-lunch — labels rendered in the VIEWER's timezone, not the plan's
Goal: mentor planned "a lunch" at 11:20 AM on the live URL and got a midnight itinerary; three prior fix attempts (all aimed at the resolver) failed. Diagnose from logs before touching anything.
What should be done: read resolveStartTime/Checked first (on paper the category branch already lands lunch at same-day noon — so form a real hypothesis, don't patch that function again); add a permanent [schedule-resolve] log; reproduce across multiple hypotheses (TZ=UTC vs Toronto, real Groq parse output for "plan a lunch"); minimal targeted fix; contract tests for roll-forward and next-full-hour; exact-repro tests (11:20 AM → same-day noon, 9 PM → next-day noon).
What was done (technical): added `[schedule-resolve]` (places/search route — prints raw time_window, categories, resolved instant, server TZ).
